The process displayed in Figure
1
illustrates the estimates (
3.64
)-(
3.70
) later. The quantity
\Xi^{A}[\frac{59-24\gamma}{24(3-\gamma)};\frac{15}{8(3-\gamma)},0](g_{\sigma})
is dominated by directly employing the spatial-velocity indices iteration form (
1.74
) and the bound
\psi_{6;-\frac{1}{8(3-\gamma)},2(\beta_{\gamma}+\frac{1}{2})}
, see (
3.65
). When controlling the quantity
\Xi^{A}[\frac{9+\gamma}{3(3-\gamma)};\frac{7}{8(3-\gamma)},\beta_{\gamma}+\frac{1}{2}](g_{\sigma})
in (
3.66
), the spatial-velocity indices iteration form (
1.74
) and the following interpolation inequality are utilized:

\displaystyle l^{\frac{15-4\gamma}{12(3-\gamma)}}(l^{-6}\delta x+l)^{\frac{13}{8(3-\gamma)}}\leq\epsilon_{*}l^{\frac{59-24\gamma}{48(3-\gamma)}}(l^{-6}\delta x+l)^{\frac{15}{8(3-\gamma)}}+C_{\epsilon_{*}}l^{-\frac{3}{16(3-\gamma)}}l^{\frac{9+\gamma}{3(3-\gamma)}}(l^{-6}\delta x+l)^{\frac{7}{8(3-\gamma)}}\,.

Similarly, by applying the bound
\psi_{6;-\frac{1}{4(3-\gamma)},2(\beta_{\gamma}+\frac{1}{2})}\leq\mathscr{E}_{2}^{A}(g_{\sigma})
, the spatial-velocity indices iteration form (
1.74
), and the interpolation inequality

\displaystyle l^{\frac{279-40\gamma}{72(3-\gamma)}}(l^{-6}\delta x+l)^{\frac{3}{2(3-\gamma)}}\leq\epsilon_{*}l^{\frac{107-24\gamma}{24(3-\gamma)}}(l^{-6}\delta x+l)^{\frac{7}{4(3-\gamma)}}+C_{\epsilon_{*}}l^{-\frac{2(3+\gamma)}{3(3-\gamma)}}l^{\frac{297-40\gamma}{72(3-\gamma)}}(l^{-6}\delta x+l)^{\frac{3}{4(3-\gamma)}}\,,

the estimate (
3.68
) of the quantity
\Xi^{A}[\frac{297-40\gamma}{36(3-\gamma)};\frac{3}{4(3-\gamma)},\beta_{\gamma}+\frac{1}{2}](g_{\sigma})
can be obtained. Moreover, the estimate (
3.67
) of
\Xi^{A}[\frac{107-24\gamma}{12(3-\gamma)};\frac{7}{4(3-\gamma)},0](g_{\sigma})
can be derived from the spatial-velocity indices iteration form (
1.74
) and the interpolation inequality

\displaystyle l^{\frac{149-36\gamma}{24(3-\gamma)}}(l^{-6}\delta x+l)^{\frac{1}{2(3-\gamma)}}\leq\epsilon_{*}l^{\frac{297-40\gamma}{72(3-\gamma)}}(l^{-6}\delta x+l)^{\frac{3}{4(3-\gamma)}}+C_{\epsilon_{*}}l^{-\frac{2(3+\gamma)}{9(3-\gamma)}}l^{\frac{189-52\gamma}{72(3-\gamma)}}(l^{-6}\delta x+l)^{-\frac{1}{8(3-\gamma)}}\,.

Finally, by the bound
\psi_{6;-\frac{7}{8(3-\gamma)},3(\beta_{\gamma}+\frac{1}{2})}\leq\mathscr{E}_{2}^{A}(g_{\sigma})
for
\beta\geq 3(\beta_{\gamma}+\frac{1}{2})
and the spatial-velocity indices iteration form (
1.74
), the estimate (
3.69
) of
\Xi^{A}[\frac{189-52\gamma}{36(3-\gamma)};-\frac{1}{8(3-\gamma)},2(\beta_{\gamma}+\frac{1}{2})](g_{\sigma})
is gained. Once taking
\epsilon_{*}>0
sufficiently small and then choosing
l\geq 1
large enough, one can obtain the uniform bound of the quantity
\mathscr{E}_{2}^{A}(g_{\sigma})+\mathscr{E}_{\mathtt{NBE}}^{A}(g_{\sigma})
, hence, the closed uniform weighted
L^{2}_{x,v}
estimates in Lemma
3.5
holds.

(IV) Designing the uniform norms of (
CA-eq
).
Now we illustrate the process of deriving the uniform bounds of the problem (
3.1
) below (equivalently (
CA-eq
)). Due to the complication of deriving the uniform bounds, the following sketch map will be initially drawn for the sake of readers’ intuition (see Figure
2
below).

{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\mathscr{E}^{A}_{\infty}(g_{\sigma})}=[\![g_{\sigma}]\!]_{A;m,\beta,\vartheta}
Lemma
4.2
\|z_{\alpha^{\prime}}\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}w_{-\gamma,\vartheta}g_{\sigma}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}
Lemma
4.3
{\color[rgb]{1,0,0}\mathscr{E}^{A}_{\mathtt{cro}}(g_{\sigma})}=\|\nu^{\frac{1}{2}}z_{-\alpha}\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}w_{-\gamma,\vartheta}g_{\sigma}\|_{A}

+\|\nu^{-\frac{1}{2}}z_{-\alpha}\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}z_{1}w_{-\gamma,\vartheta}\partial_{x}g_{\sigma}\|_{A}
Lemma
4.4
Lemma
3.1
+
\|(\delta x+l)^{-\frac{m(1-\gamma)}{2(3-\gamma)}}\nu^{\frac{1}{2}}w_{-\gamma+\beta_{\gamma},\vartheta}g_{\sigma}\|_{A}
[\mathscr{E}_{2}^{A}(g_{\sigma})+\mathscr{E}_{\mathtt{NBE}}^{A}(g_{\sigma})]^{\frac{1}{2}}
(
4.41
)
(\delta\hbar)^{-\frac{15}{2}}l^{-\frac{3(3+\gamma)}{64(3-\gamma)}}

Figure 2
.
Derivation of uniform bounds for the connection auxiliary equation (
CA-eq
). Here we denote by
g_{\sigma}=e^{\hbar\sigma}g
.

Based on the Figure
2
, we now illustrate the main ideas. We mainly want to control the weighted
L^{\infty}_{x,v}
quantity
\mathscr{E}^{A}_{\infty}(g_{\sigma})
. However, the operator
K
is not compact in the weighted
L^{\infty}_{x,v}
spaces, which fails to obtain a closed estimate in the
L^{\infty}_{x,v}
framework. By applying the property of
K
in Lemma
2.4
, the quantity
\mathscr{E}^{A}_{\infty}(g_{\sigma})
can be bounded by the norm
\|z_{\alpha^{\prime}}\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}w_{-\gamma,\vartheta}g_{\sigma}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}
, see Lemma
4.2
. In this step, the
L^{\infty}_{x,v}
bounds for the operators
Y_{A},Z,U
defined in (
2.3
)-(
2.4
)-(
2.5
) are important, see Lemma
2.2
. By Lemma
4.3
, the quantity
\|z_{\alpha^{\prime}}\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}w_{-\gamma,\vartheta}g_{\sigma}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}
is thereby dominated by
\mathscr{E}^{A}_{\mathtt{cro}}(g_{\sigma})
. It is actually the Sobolev type interpolation in one dimensional space with different weights. Due to the structure of the equation, the singular weight
z_{-\alpha}(v)
is unavoidable.

By Lemma
4.4
, the quantity
\mathscr{E}^{A}_{\mathtt{cro}}(g_{\sigma})
can be bounded by

\|(\delta x+l)^{-\frac{m(1-\gamma)}{2(3-\gamma)}}\nu^{\frac{1}{2}}w_{-\gamma+\beta_{\gamma}-\mathbf{1}_{m<0}m(1-\gamma)/2,\vartheta}g_{\sigma}\|_{A}+\alpha_{*}(\delta\hbar)^{-\frac{15}{2}}l^{-\frac{3(3+\gamma)}{64(3-\gamma)}}\big[\mathscr{E}_{2}^{A}(g_{\sigma})+\mathscr{E}_{\mathtt{NBE}}^{A}(g_{\sigma})\big]^{\frac{1}{2}}\,,

where the first term can be further dominated by
[\mathscr{E}_{2}^{A}(g_{\sigma})+\mathscr{E}_{\mathtt{NBE}}^{A}(g_{\sigma})]^{\frac{1}{2}}
if
m\geq 1
, see (
4.41
). Note that the quantity
\mathscr{E}^{A}_{\mathtt{cro}}(g_{\sigma})
is considered in the
L^{2}_{x,v}
framework. The Nondissipative boundary lemma (Lemma
3.1
) is therefore required to deal with the weighted boundary energy as stated in Part (III) above. Then the second quantity above is obtained. The main goal of this step is to control the
(x,v)
-mixed polynomial type weight
\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}(x,v)
and the singular weight
z_{-\alpha}(v)
involved in
\mathscr{E}^{A}_{\mathtt{cro}}(g_{\sigma})
. The
(x,v)
-mixed weight
\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}(x,v)
can be controlled by
x
-polynomial and
|v|
-polynomial weights. The difficult is to deal with the singular weight
z_{-\alpha}(v)
associated with the operator
K
. Thanks to Lemma
2.5
, the weight
z_{-\alpha}(v)
is successfully removed, in which the key is to obtain the estimate
\mathsf{h}(v_{*})=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}z_{-\alpha}^{2}(v)|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}\mathfrak{M}^{\frac{3}{8}}(v)\mathrm{d}v\lesssim(1+|v_{*}|)^{\gamma}
, see (
8.61
) below. At the end, the quantity
\mathscr{E}_{2}^{A}(g_{\sigma})+\mathscr{E}_{\mathtt{NBE}}^{A}(g_{\sigma})
has been successfully controlled as in Part (III). Therefore, we obtain the uniform a priori estimates for (
CA-eq
) in Lemma
4.1
.

(V) Existence of the linear damped problem (
1.66
).
Based on the uniform a priori weighted
L^{\infty}_{x,v}\cap L^{2}_{x,v}
estimates on the connection auxiliary problem (
CA-eq
) in Lemma
4.1
, we sketch the proof of existence and uniqueness of the mild solution to the linear damped problem (
1.66
) (equivalently (
KLd
)).

Initially, together with the uniform a priori
L^{2}_{x,v}
estimates on the connection auxiliary problem (
CA-eq
) Lemma
3.5
, the existence and uniqueness of weak solution to the approximate problem (
CA-eq
) can be prove by employing the well-known Hahn-Banach Theorem and Lax-Milgram Theorem. Inspired by Lemma II.2 of
[
5
]
, one knows that the previous weak solution to (
CA-eq
) is exactly the mild solution with the form (
4.10
).

Then we can justify the existence and uniqueness of the mild solution to (
1.66
) by using Lemma
4.1
. By taking
\varphi_{A}(v)=0
and assuming
\mathscr{A}^{\infty}(e^{\hbar\sigma}h)<\infty
, the solution
g^{A}(x,v)
constructed in Lemma
4.1
obeys the uniform-in-
A
bound
\mathscr{E}^{A}(e^{\hbar\sigma}g^{A})\lesssim\mathscr{A}^{\infty}(e^{\hbar\sigma}h)
. Note that
g^{A}
is defined on
(x,v)\in(0,A)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
. We extend
g^{A}(x,v)
to
(x,v)\in\mathbb{R}_{+}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
as
\tilde{g}^{A}(x,v)=\mathbf{1}_{x\in(0,A)}g^{A}(x,v)
, which, together the bound of
g^{A}
, is uniformly bounded in the space
\mathbb{B}^{\hbar}_{\infty}
defined in (
5.1
). Moreover, we can prove that
\tilde{g}^{A}(x,v)
is a Cauchy sequence in
\mathbb{B}^{\hbar^{\prime}}_{\infty}
for any fixed
\hbar^{\prime}\in(0,\hbar)
. Then we can show that the limit of
\tilde{g}^{A}(x,v)
is the unique solution to (
1.66
). The result on Theorem
1.1
is thereby obtained.

(VI) Remove the artificial damping: Freezing Point Method.

In order to prove the existence of mild solution to the linear problem (
KL
), i.e., to prove Theorem
1.1
, we should remove the artificial damping
\mathbf{D}f_{*}
, which means that

\displaystyle\mathfrak{f}(x)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}v_{3}\psi_{3}^{*}f_{*}(x,v)\mathrm{d}v=0\,,\quad\mathfrak{F}(x)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}v_{3}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\widehat{\mathbb{B}}_{3}\\
\widehat{\mathbb{A}}_{13}\\
\widehat{\mathbb{A}}_{23}\end{array}\right)f_{*}(x,v)\mathrm{d}v=0

for all
x\geq 0
. Here
\mathfrak{f}(x)
is the coefficient of positive eigensubspace and
\mathfrak{F}(x)
is that of zero eigensubspace. Note that
\mathfrak{f}(x)
obeys the first order ODE
\frac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}x}\mathfrak{f}(x)=-\ss_{+}(\delta x+l)^{-\frac{1-\gamma}{3-\gamma}}\mathfrak{f}(x)
, which means that
\mathfrak{f}(x)=0
for all
x\geq 0
if and only if
\mathfrak{f}(0)=0
. This argument on removing the damping effect in the positive eigensubspace is similar as in Chen-Liu-Yang’s work
[
3
]
. Moreover, the coefficient
\mathfrak{F}(x)
of zero eigensubspace enjoys the second order ODE system

\displaystyle\tfrac{\mathrm{d}^{2}}{\mathrm{d}x^{2}}\mathfrak{F}(x)=(\delta x+l)^{-\frac{1-\gamma}{3-\gamma}}\mathrm{diag}(\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2})\mathfrak{F}(x)\,,\ \ \lim_{x\to+\infty}\mathfrak{F}(x)=0_{3}\,.

If
\gamma=1
, the above equation is a constant coefficient second order linear ODE system, so that the solution can be explicitly expressed, see Golse’s work
[
7
]
. Then it can be directly concluded that
\mathfrak{F}(x)=0
for all
x\geq 0
if and only if
\mathfrak{F}(0)=0
. However, it is a nonconstant coefficient second order linear ODE system for
-3<\gamma<1
, whose solution cannot be explicitly written down. For general
-3<\gamma\leq 1
, we also need to prove that
\mathfrak{F}(x)=0
for all
x\geq 0
if and only if
\mathfrak{F}(0)=0
. We employ the Freezing Point Method (developed in elliptic theory) to fix our issue. We consider the components equations of above ODE system: for
i=0,1,2
,

\displaystyle\tfrac{\mathrm{d}^{2}}{\mathrm{d}x^{2}}\mathfrak{F}_{i}(x)=\lambda_{i}(\delta x+l)^{-\frac{1-\gamma}{3-\gamma}}\mathfrak{F}_{i}(x)\,,\ \ \lim_{x\to+\infty}\mathfrak{F}_{i}(x)=0\,.

For any fixed
x_{0}\geq 0
and
-3<\gamma\leq 1
, we define

\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}(x_{0})=\lambda_{i}^{\frac{1}{2}}(\delta x_{0}+l)^{-\frac{1-\gamma}{2(3-\gamma)}}>0\,,\ \mathfrak{g}_{i}(x)=\lambda_{i}(\delta x+l)^{-\frac{1-\gamma}{3-\gamma}}\mathfrak{F}_{i}(x)-\lambda_{i}(\delta x_{0}+l)^{-\frac{1-\gamma}{3-\gamma}}\mathfrak{F}_{i}(x)\,,

which satisfies
\mathfrak{g}_{i}(x_{0})=0
. Then we can rewrite the component equation as the form

\displaystyle\tfrac{\mathrm{d}^{2}}{\mathrm{d}x^{2}}\mathfrak{F}_{i}(x)=\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}^{2}(x_{0})\mathfrak{F}_{i}(x)+\mathfrak{g}_{i}(x)\,,\ \lim_{x\to+\infty}\mathfrak{F}_{i}(x)=0\,.

By the standard process of solving the second order linear ODE with constant coefficients and applying the far-field condition
\lim_{x\to+\infty}\mathfrak{F}_{i}(x)=0
,
\mathfrak{F}_{i}(x)
can be expressed by

\displaystyle\mathfrak{F}_{i}(x)=\Big[C_{1}(x_{0})-\tfrac{1}{2\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}(x_{0})}\int_{x_{0}}^{x}e^{\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}(x_{0})y}\mathfrak{g}_{i}(y)\mathrm{d}y\Big]e^{-\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}(x_{0})x}

for some constant
C(x_{0})\in\mathbb{R}
, which means that
\mathfrak{F}_{i}(x_{0})=C_{1}(x_{0})e^{-\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}(x_{0})x_{0}}
. Moreover, a direct computation shows that
\tfrac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}x}\mathfrak{F}_{i}(x_{0})=-\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}(x_{0})C_{1}(x_{0})e^{-\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}(x_{0})x_{0}}
. By the arbitrariness of
x_{0}
, one has

\displaystyle\tfrac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}x}\mathfrak{F}_{i}(x)=-\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}(x)\mathfrak{F}_{i}(x)=-\lambda_{i}(\delta x+l)^{-\frac{1-\gamma}{3-\gamma}}\mathfrak{F}_{i}(x)\,,

which means that
\mathfrak{F}(x)=0
for all
x\geq 0
if and only if
\mathfrak{F}(0)=0
.

As a result, we have proved that the artificial damping
\mathbf{D}f_{*}(x,v)=0
for all
x\geq 0
if and only if the restriction (
5.46
) hold, which can re-characterize the vanishing sources set
\mathbb{VSS}_{\alpha_{*}}
defined in (
1.24
) as an equivalent form (
1.52
) in Remark
1.3
. Moreover, due to the linear independence of
\psi_{3}^{*}
,
\widehat{\mathbb{B}}_{3}
,
\widehat{\mathbb{A}}_{13}
and
\widehat{\mathbb{A}}_{23}
, we know that
\mathbb{VSS}_{\alpha_{*}}\cap\mathfrak{X}_{\gamma}^{\infty}
is the subspace of
\mathfrak{X}_{\gamma}^{\infty}
with codimension 4, which finish the conclusion of Theorem
1.1
.

(VII) Nonlinear problem (
KL-NL
).
At the end, we focus on the nonlinear problem (
KL-NL
), which can be equivalently represented by (
1.53
). The one of keys of studying the nonlinear problem is to obtain nonlinear estimate (
6.2
), i.e.,

\displaystyle\mathscr{E}^{\infty}((\delta x+l)^{\frac{1-\gamma}{3-\gamma}}e^{\hbar\sigma}\Gamma(f,g))\lesssim\mathscr{E}^{\infty}(e^{\hbar\sigma}f)\mathscr{E}^{\infty}(e^{\hbar\sigma}g)

given in Lemma
6.1
.

We first decompose the solution
f
to the problem (
1.53
) as two part
f_{1}=(\mathcal{I}-\mathcal{P}^{0})f
and
f_{2}=\mathcal{P}^{0}f
, whose subject to the equations (
6.3
). We then consider the nonlinear damping problem (
1.58
), hence, adding the artificial damping
\mathbf{D}f_{1}
in the
f_{1}
-equation of (
1.58
). By employing the linear theory constructed in Theorem
1.1
, the iterative scheme (
6.5
) is contraction under the small data assumption (
1.56
) (i.e.,
\varsigma_{0}>0
sufficiently small). Then the limit of the iterative scheme (
6.5
) uniquely solves the nonlinear problem (
1.58
), which means that the solution operator
\mathcal{I}_{\gamma}
in Remark
1.4
is well-defined. Following the same arguments as in the linear theory,
\mathbf{D}f_{1}(x,v)=0
for all
x\geq 0
if and only if (
6.13
), which is then equivalent to (
6.14
). Consequently, the vanishing sources set
\widetilde{\mathbb{VSS}}_{\alpha_{*}}
for the nonlinear problem (
KL-NL
) defined in (
1.55
) can be re-characterized as in Remark
1.4
. Thanks to the linear independence of
\psi_{3}^{*}
,
\widehat{\mathbb{B}}_{3}
,
\widehat{\mathbb{A}}_{13}
and
\widehat{\mathbb{A}}_{23}
, we know that
\widetilde{\mathbb{VSS}}_{\alpha_{*}}\cap\mathfrak{X}_{\gamma}^{\varsigma_{0}}
for sufficiently small
\varsigma_{0}>0
is the subspace of
\mathfrak{X}_{\gamma}^{\varsigma_{0}}
with codimension 4. The result on Theorem
1.2
is therefore constructed.

1.7.
Historical remarks

The known results on Knudsen layer equation with Maxwell type boundary condition has been listed in Subsection
1.6
above, hence,
[
4
,
8
,
13
,
14
,
15
]
, which all considered the hard sphere model (
\gamma=1
). For the incoming data boundary conditions with angular cutoff collisional kernel cases, there have been many results. The incoming data involves two cases: fixed boundary and moving boundary. For the fixed boundary case, Bardos-Caflisch-Nicolaenko
[
2
]
proved the exponential decay
e^{-cx}
in
L^{\infty}(\mathrm{d}x;L^{2}((1+|v|)\mathrm{d}v))
for hard sphere model
\gamma=1
, and Golse-Poupaud
[
9
]
then proved superalgebraic
O(x^{-\infty})
in
L^{\infty}(\mathrm{d}x;L^{2}(|v_{3}|\mathrm{d}v))
space for
-2\leq\gamma<1
. The results on moving boundary case are listed as follows. Coron-Golse-Sulem
[
4
]
studied the exponential decay
e^{-cx}
in
L^{\infty}(e^{cx}\mathrm{d}x;L^{2}(|v_{3}+u|\mathrm{d}v))
space for
\gamma=1
, both degenerate and nondegenerate moving velocities. Ukai-Yang-Yu
[
24
]
investigated the exponential decay
e^{-cx}
in
x
and algebraic decay
(1+|v|)^{-\beta}
in
v
in
L^{\infty}_{x,v}
space for
\gamma=1
and nondegenerate moving velocity. Then Golse
[
7
]
proved the same result for the degenerate moving velocity as in
[
24
]
. Chen-Liu-Yang
[
3
]
justified the exponential decay
e^{-cx^{\frac{2}{3-\gamma}}}
in
x
and algebraic decay
(1+|v|)^{-\beta}
in
v
in
L^{\infty}_{x,v}
space for
0<\gamma\leq 1
and nondegenerate moving velocity. Wang-Yang-Yang
[
27
]
proved the same decay results of
[
3
]
for
-2<\gamma\leq 0
and nondegenerate moving velocity. Yang
[
29
]
verified the superalgebraic decay
O(x^{-\infty})
and
O(|v|^{-\infty})
in
L^{\infty}_{x,v}
for
-3<\gamma\leq 1
, both degenerate and nondegenerate moving velocities. There also were some nonlinear stability results on the Knudsen boundary layer equation with incoming data, see
[
25
,
26
,
28
]
. We remark that there is no any result about the Knudsen boundary layer problems with noncutoff angular for all types of boundary conditions.

1.8.
Organization of current paper

In the next section, we give some preliminaries will be frequently used later. In Section
3
, we prove the existence and uniqueness of the weak solution to the connection auxiliary equation (
CA-eq
). The key point is to closed the nondissipative boundary condition in the
L^{2}_{x,v}
framework. Section
4
aims at deriving the uniform weighted
L^{\infty}_{x,v}
estimates for (
CA-eq
). In Section
5
, the existence and uniqueness of the linear problem (
KL
) is proved, i.e., Theorem
1.1
. In Section
6
, we justify the existence and uniqueness of the nonlinear problem (
KL-NL
), hence, Theorem
1.2
. The
L^{\infty}_{x,v}
bounds for the operators
Y_{A},Z,U
are studied in Section
7
, i.e., proving Lemma
2.2
. We then study the properties of the operator
K
, namely, to prove Lemma
2.3
-
2.4
-
2.5
in Section
8
. In Section
9
, the properties of the artificial damping operator
\mathbf{D}
are studied, i.e., verifying Lemma
2.6
-
2.7
-
2.8
-
2.9
.

2.
Preliminaries

2.1.
Properties of the
(x,v)
-mixed weights
\sigma(x,v)

From the works
[
3
,
26
,
27
,
28
]
, the weihgt
\sigma(x,v)
admits the following properties.

Lemma 2.1
.

For
-3<\gamma\leq 1
, some large constant
l>0
and small constant
\delta>0
, there are some positive constants
c,c_{1},c_{2}
such that

\displaystyle\sigma(x,v)\geq c(\delta x+l)^{\frac{2}{3-\gamma}}\,,\ |\sigma(x,v)-\sigma(x,v_{*})|\leq c\big||v-\mathfrak{u}|^{2}-|v_{*}-\mathfrak{u}|^{2}\big|\,,

\displaystyle 0<c_{1}\min\big\{(\delta x+l)^{-\frac{1-\gamma}{3-\gamma}}\,,(1+|v-\mathfrak{u}|)^{-1+\gamma}\big\}\leq\sigma_{x}(x,v)\leq c_{2}(\delta x+l)^{-\frac{1-\gamma}{3-\gamma}}\leq c_{2}l^{-\frac{1-\gamma}{3-\gamma}}\,,

\displaystyle|\sigma_{x}(x,v)v_{3}|\leq c\nu(v)\,,\ |\sigma_{xx}(x,v)v_{3}|\leq\delta\sigma_{x}\nu(v)\,.

2.2.
The operators
Y_{A}
,
Z
and
U

We introduce the functions

(2.1)

\displaystyle\kappa(x,v)=\int_{0}^{x}\big[-\tfrac{m\sigma_{xx}(y,v)}{2\sigma_{x}(y,v)}-\hbar\sigma_{x}(y,v)+\tfrac{\nu(v)}{v_{3}}\big]\mathrm{d}y\,.

Moreover, denote by

(2.2)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{M}_{\sigma}(v)=\mathfrak{M}(v)e^{-2\hbar\sigma(0,v)}\,.

We then define the following linear operators

(2.3)

{\scriptsize Y_{A}(f)=\left\{\begin{aligned} (1-\alpha_{*})e^{{\kappa}(A,R_{0}v)-{\kappa}(x,v)}f(A,R_{0}v)\hskip 16.38895pt&\\
+\alpha_{*}\tfrac{M_{w}(v)}{\sqrt{\mathfrak{M}_{\sigma}(v)}}\int_{v_{3}^{\prime}<0}(-v_{3}^{\prime})\tfrac{\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}(0,v)}{\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}(0,v^{\prime})}e^{{\kappa}(A,v^{\prime})-{\kappa}(x,v)}f(A,v^{\prime})\sqrt{\mathfrak{M}_{\sigma}(v^{\prime})}\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\,,\hskip 8.19447pt&\textrm{if }v_{3}>0\,,\\[10.0pt]
e^{{\kappa}(A,v)-{\kappa}(x,v)}f(A,v)\,,\hskip 8.19447pt&\textrm{if }v_{3}<0\,,\end{aligned}\right.}

and

(2.4)

{\scriptsize Z(f)=\left\{\begin{aligned} (1-\alpha_{*})\int_{0}^{A}e^{-[{\kappa}(x,v)-{\kappa}(x^{\prime},R_{0}v)]}\tfrac{1}{v_{3}}f(x^{\prime},R_{0}v)\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}\,\hskip 16.38895pt\hskip 8.19447pt&\\
-\alpha_{*}\tfrac{M_{w}(v)}{\sqrt{\mathfrak{M}_{\sigma}(v)}}\int_{v_{3}^{\prime}<0}\int_{0}^{A}(-v_{3}^{\prime})\tfrac{\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}(0,v)}{\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}(0,v^{\prime})}e^{-[{\kappa}(x,v)-{\kappa}(x^{\prime},v^{\prime})]}\tfrac{1}{v_{3}^{\prime}}f(x^{\prime},v^{\prime})\sqrt{\mathfrak{M}_{\sigma}(v^{\prime})}\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\,,\hskip 8.19447pt&\textrm{if }v_{3}>0\,,\\[10.0pt]
0\,,\hskip 8.19447pt&\textrm{if }v_{3}<0\,,\end{aligned}\right.}

and

(2.5)

{\small U(f)=\left\{\begin{aligned} \int_{0}^{x}e^{-[{\kappa}(x,v)-{\kappa}(x^{\prime},v)]}\tfrac{1}{v_{3}}f(x^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}\,,\hskip 9.24994pt&\textrm{if }v_{3}>0\,,\\
-\int_{x}^{A}e^{-[{\kappa}(x,v)-{\kappa}(x^{\prime},v)]}\tfrac{1}{v_{3}}f(x^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}\,,\hskip 9.24994pt&\textrm{if }v_{3}<0\,.\end{aligned}\right.}

These operators will play an essential role in estimating the weighted
L^{\infty}_{x,v}
norms of the Knudsen layer equations (
1.66
). More precisely, they obey the following estimates.

Lemma 2.2
.

Let
-3<\gamma\leq 1
,
l\geq 1
,
m,\beta\in\mathbb{R}
,
A>0
and
\vartheta>0
. Then, for sufficiently small
\hbar,\delta>0
, there are some positive constant
C>0
, independent of
l
,
\delta
,
\hbar
and
A
, such that

(1)

Y_{A}(f)
subjects to the estimate

(2.6)

\displaystyle\|Y_{A}(f)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\leq C\|f(A,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}_{v}}\,;

(2)

Z(f)
enjoys the bound

(2.7)

\displaystyle\|Z(f)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\leq C\|\nu^{-1}f\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\,;

(3)

U(f)
obeys the bound

(2.8)

\displaystyle\|U(f)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\leq C\|\nu^{-1}f\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\,.

The proof of Lemma
2.2
will be given in Section
7
later.

2.3.
Properties of the operator
K

In this subsection, the goal is to derive some useful properties of the operator
K
defined in (
1.8
)-(
1.9
)-(
1.10
). It then is focused on the following decay results of the operator
K
.

Lemma 2.3
.

Let
-3<\gamma\leq 1
,
A>0
,
m,\beta\in\mathbb{R}
,
\hbar,\vartheta\geq 0
with
\delta_{0}:=\tfrac{1}{4}-T(c\hbar+\vartheta)>0
, where
c>0
is given in Lemma
2.1
. Then there is a constant
C>0
, independent of
A,\hbar
, such that

(2.9)

\displaystyle\|\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}e^{\hbar\sigma}Kf\|_{A;\beta,\vartheta}\leq C\|\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}e^{\hbar\sigma}f\|_{A;\beta+\gamma-1,\vartheta}\,.

where the norm
\|\cdot\|_{A;\beta,\vartheta}
is defined in (
1.32
).

The proof of Lemma
2.3
will be given in Subsection
8.1
later.

Next we prove the boundedness of the operator
K_{\hbar}
from
Y_{m,0,\vartheta}^{\infty}(A)\cap L^{\infty}_{x}L^{2}_{v}
to
Y_{m,-\gamma,\vartheta}^{\infty}(A)
. Here the operator
K_{\hbar}
is defined as

(2.10)

\displaystyle K_{\hbar}g_{\sigma}=e^{\hbar\sigma}K(e^{-\hbar\sigma}g_{\sigma})\,.

More precisely, the following results hold.

Lemma 2.4
.

Let
-3<\gamma\leq 1
,
A>0
,
m\in\mathbb{R}
,
0\leq\alpha^{\prime}<\frac{1}{2}
and
\hbar,\vartheta\geq 0
sufficiently small. Then for any
\eta_{1}>0
, there is a
C_{\eta_{1}}>0
, independent of
A,\hbar
, such that

(2.11)

\displaystyle[\![\nu^{-1}K_{\hbar}g]\!]_{A;m,0,\vartheta}\leq\eta_{1}[\![g]\!]_{A;m,0,\vartheta}+C_{\eta_{1}}\|\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}z_{\alpha^{\prime}}w_{-\gamma,\vartheta}g\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}\,.

Here the weight
z_{\alpha^{\prime}}
is given in (
1.29
).

The proof of Lemma
2.4
will be given in Subsection
8.2
later.

Next we show the boundedness of the operator
K_{\hbar}
in the weighted
L^{2}_{v}
space. More precisely, the following conclusions hold.

Lemma 2.5
.

Let
-3<\gamma\leq 1
,
m,\beta\in\mathbb{R}
and
\hbar,\vartheta\geq 0
be sufficiently small. Define two nonempty sets

\displaystyle\mathcal{S}_{\gamma}:=\{b_{0}\in\mathbb{R}|b_{0}<2\,,0\leq b_{0}\leq 1-\gamma\,,b_{0}+\gamma+1>0\}\,,

\displaystyle\mathcal{T}_{\gamma}:=\{b_{1}\in\mathbb{R}|b_{1}<3\,,0\leq b_{1}\leq 1-\gamma\,,b_{1}+\gamma>0\}\,.

Denote by
\mu_{\gamma}:=\min\{\frac{1}{2},\frac{\gamma+3}{2},\frac{b_{0}+\gamma+1}{2},\frac{b_{1}+\gamma}{2}\}>0
with
b_{0}\in\mathcal{S}_{\gamma}
and
b_{1}\in\mathcal{T}_{\gamma}
. Let
0\leq\alpha<\mu_{\gamma}
. Then there is a positive constant
C>0
, independent of
\hbar
, such that

(2.12)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|\nu^{-\frac{1}{2}}z_{-\alpha}\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}w_{\beta,\vartheta}K_{\hbar}g(x,v)|^{2}\mathrm{d}v\leq C\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|\nu^{\frac{1}{2}}\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}w_{\beta,\vartheta}g(x,v)|^{2}\mathrm{d}v\,.

The proof of Lemma
2.5
will be given in Subsection
8.3
later.

2.4.
Properties of artificial damping operator
\mathbf{D}

In this subsection, the majority is to study the boundedness of the artificial damping operator
\mathbf{D}
over various weighted
L^{2}_{x,v}
or
L^{\infty}_{x,v}
spaces, which will play an essential role in closing the uniform bounds of the connection auxiliary problem (
CA-eq
). For convenience of later use, we introduce the scaled artificial damping operator
\mathbf{D}_{\hbar}
by

(2.13)

\displaystyle\mathbf{D}_{\hbar}g=e^{\hbar\sigma}\mathbf{D}e^{-\hbar\sigma}g\,,

where the weight function
\sigma(x,v)
is given in (
1.26
), and small
\hbar>0
is to be determined later.

We first state the coercivity results of the scaled artificial damping operator
\mathbf{D}_{\hbar}
.

Lemma 2.6

(Coercivity of
\mathbf{D}
)
.

Let
-3<\gamma\leq 1
,
0<\delta<1
,
\beta\in\mathbb{R}
. Set
\hbar>0,\vartheta\geq 0
be both small enough. Assume that
l>O(1)(\ln\frac{1}{\delta})^{\frac{3-\gamma}{2}}
,
\ss_{0},\ss_{+}\geq O(1)\delta\hbar
, where
O(1)>1
is large enough and independent of
\delta,\hbar,l
. Then there are
\mu_{0},C>0
, independent of
\delta,\hbar,l
, such that

(2.14)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}w_{\beta,\vartheta}^{2}g(\mathbf{D}_{\hbar}g-\hbar\sigma_{x}v_{3}g)\mathrm{d}v\geq

\displaystyle\mu_{0}\delta\hbar(\delta x+l)^{-\frac{1-\gamma}{3-\gamma}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|\mathcal{P}w_{\beta,\vartheta}g|^{2}\mathrm{d}v-C\hbar^{\frac{1}{2}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\nu(v)|\mathcal{P}^{\perp}w_{\beta,\vartheta}g|^{2}\mathrm{d}v\,.

The proof of Lemma
2.6
will be given in Subsection
9.1
later.

Lemma 2.7

(Weighted
L^{\infty}_{x,v}
estimate of
\mathbf{D}
)
.

Let
-3<\gamma\leq 1
,
m,\beta,\tilde{N}\in\mathbb{R}
, and
\hbar,\vartheta\geq 0
with
s_{0}:=\tfrac{c^{\prime}}{2}-c\hbar-\vartheta>0
, where
c>0
is given in Lemma
2.1
and
c^{\prime}>0
is mentioned in (
9.6
). Then there is a constant
C>0
such that

(2.15)

\displaystyle\|\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}e^{\hbar\sigma}\mathbf{D}g\|_{\beta,\vartheta}\leq C\delta\hbar\|\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}e^{\hbar\sigma}g\|_{\beta-\tilde{N},\vartheta}\,,

where the norm
\|\cdot\|_{\beta,\vartheta}
is defined in (
1.32
), and the constants
\ss_{+},\ss_{0}>0
involved in the operator
\mathbf{D}
are given Lemma
2.6
.

The proof of Lemma
2.6
will be given in Subsection
9.2
later.

Lemma 2.8

(Boundedness of
\mathbf{D}
from weighted
L^{\infty}_{x}L^{2}_{v}
to
L^{\infty}_{x,v}
)
.

Let
-3<\gamma\leq 1
,
A>0
,
m\in\mathbb{R}
,
0\leq\alpha^{\prime}<\frac{1}{2}
and
\hbar,\vartheta\geq 0
sufficiently small. Then there is a constant
C>0
such that

(2.16)

\displaystyle[\![\nu^{-1}\mathbf{D}_{\hbar}g]\!]_{A;m,0,\vartheta}\leq C\|\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}z_{\alpha^{\prime}}w_{-\gamma,\vartheta}g\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}\,.

Here the weight
z_{\alpha^{\prime}}
is given in (
1.29
).

The proof of Lemma
2.8
will be given in Subsection
9.3
later.

Lemma 2.9

(Weighted
L^{2}_{v}
boundedness of
\mathbf{D}
)
.

Let
-3<\gamma\leq 1
,
0\leq\alpha<\frac{1}{2}
,
m,\beta\in\mathbb{R}
and sufficiently small
\hbar,\vartheta\geq 0
. Then there is a positive constant
C>0
such that

(2.17)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|\nu^{-\frac{1}{2}}z_{-\alpha}\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}w_{\beta,\vartheta}\mathbf{D}_{\hbar}g(x,v)|^{2}\mathrm{d}v\leq C\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|\nu^{\frac{1}{2}}\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}w_{\beta,\vartheta}g(x,v)|^{2}\mathrm{d}v\,.

The proof of Lemma
2.9
will be given in Subsection
9.4
later.

3.
Weak solution to connection auxiliary problem (
CA-eq
)

In this section, we mainly prove the existence and uniqueness of weak solution to the connection auxiliary problem (
CA-eq
) in the weighted
L^{2}_{x,v}
space. Moreover, the energy bound is uniform in
A\geq 1
. Let
g_{\sigma}=e^{\hbar\sigma}g
. The system (
CA-eq
) can be equivalently expressed as

(3.1)

\left\{\begin{aligned} &v_{3}\partial_{x}g_{\sigma}+[-\hbar\sigma_{x}v_{3}+\nu(v)]g_{\sigma}-K_{\hbar}g_{\sigma}+\mathbf{D}_{\hbar}g=h_{\sigma}\,,\\
&g_{\sigma}(0,v)|_{v_{3}>0}=(1-\alpha_{*})g_{\sigma}(0,R_{0}v)+\alpha_{*}\tfrac{M_{w}(v)}{\sqrt{\mathfrak{M}_{\sigma}(v)}}\int_{v_{3}^{\prime}<0}(-v_{3}^{\prime})g_{\sigma}(0,v^{\prime})\sqrt{\mathfrak{M}_{\sigma}(v^{\prime})}\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\,,\\
&g_{\sigma}(A,v)|_{v_{3}<0}=\varphi_{A,\sigma}(v)\,,\end{aligned}\right.

where
K_{\hbar}
is defined in (
2.10
),
\mathbf{D}_{\hbar}
is given in (
2.13
) and
\mathfrak{M}_{\sigma}(v)
is given in (
2.2
), i.e.,
\mathfrak{M}_{\sigma}(v)=\tfrac{\mathfrak{M}(v)}{e^{2\hbar\sigma(0,v)}}
. Here
h_{\sigma}=e^{\hbar\sigma}h
.

As stated in Subsection
1.6
before, we consider the nondissipative Maxwell reflection boundary condition in this paper. As a result, we will first deal with the boundary integral while carrying the weighted
L^{2}_{x,v}
estimates.

3.1.
Nondissipative boundary energy

In this subsection, we first establish the following key lemma to deal with the nondissipative boundary condition. The nondissipation of the boundary energy comes from the form
\int_{v_{3}<0}|v_{3}|\mathfrak{w}(v)g_{\sigma}(0,v)\mathrm{d}v
, whose signs are indefinite. The main ideas are as follows. Together with the structure of equation, the integral
\int_{v_{3}<0}|v_{3}|\mathfrak{w}(v)g_{\sigma}(0,v)\mathrm{d}v
can be pulled to some interior integrals by using the Euler factor
e^{-(\delta x+l)}
. The interior integrals can be dominated by two parts. One is quantity coming from the coercivity of the linear Boltzmann collision operator
\mathcal{L}
with a small factor. The small factor is due to the interaction of spatial and velocity variables. The other one is the
L^{2}_{x,v}
norm with additional spatial weight
(l^{-\mathbf{Z}}\delta x+l)^{a}
for any
a>0
and
\mathbf{Z}\geq 0
. Thanks to the degree of freedom for the parameters
a>0
and
\mathbf{Z}\geq 0
, the weighted
L^{2}_{x,v}
norm can be controlled by seeking more estimates.

Lemma 3.1

(Nondissipative boundary lemma)
.

Let
-3<\gamma\leq 1
,
A\geq 1
,
0<\delta,\hbar<1
,
l\geq 1
,
0\leq\lambda\leq 1
. Let
\mathfrak{w}(v)
be of exponential decay form
e^{-b|v-\mathfrak{u}|^{2}}
for some
b>0
. Assume that
g_{\sigma}(x,v)
is a solution to (
3.1
). Then for any
a>0
and
\mathbf{Z}\geq 0
, one has

(3.2)

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{v_{3}<0}|v_{3}|\mathfrak{w}(v)g_{\sigma}(0,v)\mathrm{d}v\Big|\leq C\digamma_{\delta,l,a,\mathbf{Z}}(g_{\sigma})+C\digamma_{\delta,l,a,\mathbf{Z}}(\nu^{-1}h_{\sigma})+\Big|\int_{v_{3}<0}\mathfrak{w}(v)v_{3}\varphi_{A,\sigma}(v)\mathrm{d}v\Big|

for some constant
C>0
independent of
a
,
\lambda
,
A
,
\delta
,
\hbar
,
l
. Here
\digamma_{\delta,l,a,\mathbf{Z}}(\cdot)
is introduced by

(3.3)

\displaystyle\digamma_{\delta,l,a,\mathbf{Z}}(f)=\delta^{-\frac{1}{2}}l^{-50}\|(\delta x+l)^{-\frac{1-\gamma}{2(3-\gamma)}}\nu^{\frac{1}{2}}f\|_{A}+\delta^{-\frac{1}{2}}l^{-(a-\frac{1-\gamma}{2(3-\gamma)})}\|(l^{-\mathbf{Z}}\delta x+l)^{a}(\delta x+l)^{-\frac{1-\gamma}{2(3-\gamma)}}\nu^{\frac{1}{2}}f\|_{A}\,.

Proof.

Note that
g_{\sigma}
solves (
3.1
), i.e.,

\displaystyle\partial_{x}(v_{3}g_{\sigma})=[\hbar\sigma_{x}v_{3}-\nu(v)]g_{\sigma}+(K_{\hbar}-\mathbf{D}_{\hbar})g_{\sigma}+h_{\sigma}\,.

We multiply the above equation by the factor
e^{-(\delta x+l)}
. It thereby holds

(3.4)

\displaystyle\partial_{x}\big(e^{-(\delta x+l)}v_{3}g_{\sigma}\big)=

\displaystyle-\delta e^{-(\delta x+l)}v_{3}g_{\sigma}+[\hbar\sigma_{x}v_{3}-\nu(v)]e^{-(\delta x+l)}g_{\sigma}

\displaystyle+e^{-(\delta x+l)}(K_{\hbar}-\mathbf{D}_{\hbar})g_{\sigma}+e^{-(\delta x+l)}h_{\sigma}\,.

We integrate the equation (
3.4
) over
(x,v)\in(0,A)\times\{v_{3}<0\}
. Together with the boundary condition
g_{\sigma}(A,v)|_{v_{3}<0}=\varphi_{A,\sigma}(v)
, one has

\displaystyle-e^{-l}\int_{v_{3}<0}\mathfrak{w}(v)v_{3}g_{\sigma}(0,v)\mathrm{d}v=\underbrace{-\delta\int_{0}^{A}\int_{v_{3}<0}e^{-(\delta x+l)}\mathfrak{w}(v)v_{3}g_{\sigma}(x,v)\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}x}_{:=\mathfrak{A}_{1}}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\int_{0}^{A}\int_{v_{3}<0}[\hbar\sigma_{x}v_{3}-\nu(v)]e^{-(\delta x+l)}\mathfrak{w}(v)g_{\sigma}(x,v)\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}x}_{:=\mathfrak{A}_{2}}+\underbrace{\int_{0}^{A}\int_{v_{3}<0}e^{-(\delta x+l)}\mathfrak{w}(v)(K_{\hbar}-\mathbf{D}_{\hbar})g_{\sigma}(x,v)\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}x}_{:=\mathfrak{A}_{3}}

(3.5)

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\int_{0}^{A}\int_{v_{3}<0}e^{-(\delta x+l)}\mathfrak{w}(v)h_{\sigma}(x,v)\mathrm{d}x\mathrm{d}v}_{:=\mathfrak{A}_{4}}\ \underbrace{-e^{-(\delta A+l)}\int_{v_{3}<0}\mathfrak{w}(v)v_{3}\varphi_{A,\sigma}(v)\mathrm{d}v}_{:=\mathfrak{A}_{5}}\,,

where the weight function
\mathfrak{w}(v)
is given in Lemma
3.1
.

Step 1. Control of the quantity
\mathfrak{A}_{1}
.

Note that for any
a_{*}>0
and
0<\varepsilon<1
,

\displaystyle|\mathfrak{A}_{1}|\leq

\displaystyle C\delta\int_{0}^{A}\int_{v_{3}<0}(\delta x+l)^{a_{*}+\frac{1-\gamma}{2(3-\gamma)}}e^{-(\delta x+l)}|v_{3}|\nu^{-\frac{1}{2}}e^{-\frac{b}{2}|v-\mathfrak{u}|^{2}}

\displaystyle\times|(\delta x+l)^{-a_{*}-\frac{1-\gamma}{2(3-\gamma)}}e^{-\frac{b}{2}|v-\mathfrak{u}|^{2}}\nu^{\frac{1}{2}}g_{\sigma}(x,v)|\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\underbrace{C\delta\int_{0}^{A}\int_{v_{3}<0}(\cdots)\mathbf{1}_{|v-\mathfrak{u}|\geq 2}\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}x}_{U_{11}}+\underbrace{C\delta\int_{0}^{A}\int_{v_{3}<0}(\cdots)\mathbf{1}_{|v-\mathfrak{u}|\leq 2}\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}x}_{U_{12}}\,.

For the quantity
U_{11}
, the Hölder inequality implies

\displaystyle|U_{11}|\leq

\displaystyle C\delta\big(\int_{0}^{A}(\delta x+l)^{2a_{*}+\frac{1-\gamma}{3-\gamma}}e^{-2(\delta x+l)}\mathrm{d}x\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\big(\int_{v_{3}<0}\nu^{-1}|v_{3}|^{2}e^{-b|v-\mathfrak{u}|^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{|v-\mathfrak{u}|\geq 2}\mathrm{d}v\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\times\|\mathbf{1}_{|v-\mathfrak{u}|\geq 2}(\delta x+l)^{-a_{*}-\frac{1-\gamma}{2(3-\gamma)}}e^{-\frac{b}{2}|v-\mathfrak{u}|^{2}}\nu^{\frac{1}{2}}g_{\sigma}\|_{A}\,.

Note that
\big(\int_{v_{3}<0}\nu^{-1}|v_{3}|^{2}e^{-b|v-\mathfrak{u}|^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{|v-\mathfrak{u}|\geq 2}\mathrm{d}v\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq\big(\int_{v_{3}<0}\nu^{-1}|v_{3}|^{2}e^{-b|v-\mathfrak{u}|^{2}}\mathrm{d}v\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq C
and
\big(\int_{0}^{A}

(\delta x+l)^{2a_{*}+\frac{1-\gamma}{3-\gamma}}e^{-2(\delta x+l)}\mathrm{d}x\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq C\delta^{-\frac{1}{2}}l^{a_{*}+\frac{1-\gamma}{2(3-\gamma)}}e^{-l}
. Then one obtains

\displaystyle|U_{11}|\leq

\displaystyle C\delta\delta^{-\frac{1}{2}}l^{a_{*}+\frac{1-\gamma}{2(3-\gamma)}}e^{-l}\|\mathbf{1}_{|v-\mathfrak{u}|\geq 2}(\delta x+l)^{-a_{*}-\frac{1-\gamma}{2(3-\gamma)}}e^{-\frac{b}{2}|v-\mathfrak{u}|^{2}}\nu^{\frac{1}{2}}g_{\sigma}\|_{A}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\delta\delta^{-\frac{1}{2}}l^{a_{*}+\frac{1-\gamma}{2(3-\gamma)}}e^{-l}\big(\|\mathbf{1}_{2\leq|v-\mathfrak{u}|\leq(\delta x+l)^{\kappa_{0}}}(\delta x+l)^{-a_{*}-\frac{1-\gamma}{2(3-\gamma)}}e^{-\frac{b}{2}|v-\mathfrak{u}|^{2}}\nu^{\frac{1}{2}}g_{\sigma}\|_{A}

\displaystyle+\|\mathbf{1}_{|v-\mathfrak{u}|>(\delta x+l)^{\kappa_{0}}}(\delta x+l)^{-a_{*}-\frac{1-\gamma}{2(3-\gamma)}}e^{-\frac{b}{2}|v-\mathfrak{u}|^{2}}\nu^{\frac{1}{2}}g_{\sigma}\|_{A}\big)\,,

where
\kappa_{0}>0
is to be determined. If
2\leq|v-\mathfrak{u}|\leq(\delta x+l)^{\kappa_{0}}
, there holds

\displaystyle(\delta x+l)^{-a_{*}}e^{-\frac{b}{2}|v-\mathfrak{u}|^{2}}\leq 2^{-\frac{a_{*}}{\kappa_{0}}}\,.

Take a sufficiently small
\kappa_{0}>0
such that
2^{-\frac{a_{*}}{\kappa_{0}}}\leq l^{-50-a_{*}-\frac{1-\gamma}{2(3-\gamma)}}
, and fix this
\kappa_{0}>0
. It therefore follows that

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{1}_{2\leq|v-\mathfrak{u}|\leq(\delta x+l)^{\kappa_{0}}}(\delta x+l)^{-a_{*}-\frac{1-\gamma}{2(3-\gamma)}}e^{-\frac{b}{2}|v-\mathfrak{u}|^{2}}\nu^{\frac{1}{2}}g_{\sigma}\|_{A}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle l^{-50-a_{*}-\frac{1-\gamma}{2(3-\gamma)}}\|(\delta x+l)^{-\frac{1-\gamma}{2(3-\gamma)}}e^{-\frac{b}{2}|v-\mathfrak{u}|^{2}}\nu^{\frac{1}{2}}g_{\sigma}\|_{A}\,.

If
|v-\mathfrak{u}|>(\delta x+l)^{\kappa_{0}}
, one has

\displaystyle(\delta x+l)^{-a_{*}}e^{-\frac{b}{2}|v-\mathfrak{u}|^{2}}\leq l^{-a_{*}}e^{-\frac{b}{2}(\delta x+l)^{2\kappa_{0}}}\leq l^{-a_{*}}e^{-\frac{b}{2}l^{2\kappa_{0}}}\leq Cl^{-a_{*}-\frac{1-\gamma}{2(3-\gamma)}-50}

for some harmless constant
C>0
. Then

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{1}_{|v-\mathfrak{u}|>(\delta x+l)^{\kappa_{0}}}(\delta x+l)^{-a_{*}-\frac{1-\gamma}{2(3-\gamma)}}e^{-\frac{b}{2}|v-\mathfrak{u}|^{2}}\nu^{\frac{1}{2}}g_{\sigma}\|_{A}\leq Cl^{-a_{*}-\frac{1-\gamma}{2(3-\gamma)}-50}\|(\delta x+l)^{-\frac{1-\gamma}{2(3-\gamma)}}\nu^{\frac{1}{2}}g_{\sigma}\|_{A}\,.

As a result,
U_{11}
can be bounded by

(3.6)

\displaystyle|U_{11}|\leq C\delta\delta^{-\frac{1}{2}}l^{-50}e^{-l}\|(\delta x+l)^{-\frac{1-\gamma}{2(3-\gamma)}}\nu^{\frac{1}{2}}g_{\sigma}\|_{A}

For the quantity
U_{12}
, the Hölder inequality reduces to

\displaystyle|U_{12}|\leq

\displaystyle C\delta\big(\int_{0}^{A}(\delta x+l)^{\frac{1-\gamma}{3-\gamma}}e^{-2(\delta x+l)}\mathrm{d}x\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\big(\int_{v_{3}<0}\nu^{-1}|v_{3}|^{2}|v-\mathfrak{u}|^{-1}e^{-2b|v-\mathfrak{u}|^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{|v-\mathfrak{u}|\leq 2}\mathrm{d}v\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\times\|\mathbf{1}_{|v-\mathfrak{u}|\leq 2}(\delta x+l)^{-\frac{1-\gamma}{2(3-\gamma)}}|v-\mathfrak{u}|\nu^{\frac{1}{2}}g_{\sigma}\|_{A}

It is easy to see that
\big(\int_{0}^{A}(\delta x+l)^{\frac{1-\gamma}{3-\gamma}}e^{-2(\delta x+l)}\mathrm{d}x\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq Cl^{\frac{1-\gamma}{2(3-\gamma)}}e^{-l}
. Moreover, by using
|v_{3}|^{3}\leq|v-\mathfrak{u}|^{2}
,

\displaystyle\big(\int_{v_{3}<0}\nu^{-1}|v_{3}|^{2}|v-\mathfrak{u}|^{-1}e^{-2b|v-\mathfrak{u}|^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{|v-\mathfrak{u}|\leq 2}\mathrm{d}v\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq

\displaystyle\big(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\nu^{-1}|v-\mathfrak{u}|e^{-2b|v-\mathfrak{u}|^{2}}\mathbf{1}_{|v-\mathfrak{u}|\leq 2}\mathrm{d}v\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq C\,.

Then

\displaystyle|U_{12}|\leq C\delta\delta^{-\frac{1}{2}}l^{\frac{1-\gamma}{2(3-\gamma)}}e^{-l}\|\mathbf{1}_{|v-\mathfrak{u}|\leq 2}(\delta x+l)^{-\frac{1-\gamma}{2(3-\gamma)}}|v-\mathfrak{u}|\nu^{\frac{1}{2}}g_{\sigma}\|_{A}\,.

For any
a>0
and
\mathbf{Z}\geq 0
, one knows that
\mathbf{1}_{|v-\mathfrak{u}|\leq 2}|v-\mathfrak{u}|\leq 2\leq 2(l^{-\mathbf{Z}-1}\delta x+1)^{a}
, which means that

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{1}_{|v-\mathfrak{u}|\leq 2}(\delta x+l)^{-\frac{1-\gamma}{2(3-\gamma)}}|v-\mathfrak{u}|\nu^{\frac{1}{2}}g_{\sigma}\|_{A}\leq 2l^{-a}\|(l^{-\mathbf{Z}}\delta x+l)^{a}(\delta x+l)^{-\frac{1-\gamma}{2(3-\gamma)}}\nu^{\frac{1}{2}}g_{\sigma}\|_{A}

for any
a>0
and
\mathbf{Z}\geq 0
. The quantity
U_{12}
is then bounded by

\displaystyle|U_{12}|\leq C\delta\delta^{-\frac{1}{2}}l^{-(a-\frac{1-\gamma}{2(3-\gamma)})}e^{-l}\|(l^{-\mathbf{Z}}\delta x+l)^{a}(\delta x+l)^{-\frac{1-\gamma}{2(3-\gamma)}}\nu^{\frac{1}{2}}g_{\sigma}\|_{A}\,.

Collecting the bounds of
U_{11}
and
U_{12}
above, one then gains

(3.7)

\displaystyle|\mathfrak{A}_{1}|\leq C\delta\digamma_{\delta,l,a,\mathbf{Z}}(g_{\sigma})

for any
a>0
and
\mathbf{Z}\geq 0
, where the functional
\digamma_{\delta,l,a,\mathbf{Z}}(g_{\sigma})
is defined in (
3.3
).

Step 2. Control of the quantity
\mathfrak{A}_{2}
.
Lemma
2.1
indicates that
|v_{3}|\sigma_{x}(x,v)\leq c\nu(v)
, which means that
|\hbar\sigma_{x}v_{3}-\nu(v)|\leq C\nu(v)
. Then the similar arguments of estimating the quantity
\mathfrak{A}_{1}
in (
3.7
) follow that

(3.8)

\displaystyle|\mathfrak{A}_{2}|\leq C\digamma_{\delta,l,a,\mathbf{Z}}(g_{\sigma})

for any
a>0
and
\mathbf{Z}\geq 0
.

Step 3. Control of the quantity
\mathfrak{A}_{3}
.
Following the similar arguments of
|\mathfrak{A}_{2}|
in (
3.8
), one has

\displaystyle|\mathfrak{A}_{3}|\leq C\digamma_{\delta,l,a,\mathbf{Z}}(\nu^{-1}(K_{\hbar}-\mathbf{D}_{\hbar})g_{\sigma})

for any
a>0
and
\mathbf{Z}\geq 0
. By employing Lemma
2.5
and Lemma
2.9
with
\alpha=m=\beta=\vartheta=0
, one obtains
\|(ax+b)^{\mathfrak{z}}\nu^{-\frac{1}{2}}(K_{\hbar}-\mathbf{D}_{\hbar})g_{\sigma}\|_{A}\leq C\|(ax+b)^{\mathfrak{z}}\nu^{\frac{1}{2}}g_{\sigma}\|_{A}
for any
\mathfrak{z}\in\mathbb{R}
, which means that
\digamma_{\delta,l,a,\mathbf{Z}}(\nu^{-1}(K_{\hbar}-\mathbf{D}_{\hbar})g_{\sigma})\leq C\digamma_{\delta,l,a,\mathbf{Z}}(g_{\sigma})
. Consequently,

(3.9)

\displaystyle|\mathfrak{A}_{3}|\leq C\digamma_{\delta,l,a,\mathbf{Z}}(g_{\sigma})

for any
a>0
and
\mathbf{Z}\geq 0
.

Step 4. Control of the quantity
\mathfrak{A}_{4}
.
It infers from the similar arguments of
|\mathfrak{A}_{3}|
in (
3.9
) that

(3.10)

\displaystyle|\mathfrak{A}_{4}|\leq C\digamma_{\delta,l,a,\mathbf{Z}}(\nu^{-1}h_{\sigma})

for any
a>0
and
\mathbf{Z}\geq 0
.

Step 5. Control of the quantity
\mathfrak{A}_{5}
.
Note that

\displaystyle|\mathfrak{A}_{5}|=e^{-l}e^{-(\delta A+l)+l}\Big|\int_{v_{3}<0}\mathfrak{w}(v)v_{3}\varphi_{A,\sigma}(v)\mathrm{d}v\Big|\,.

Observe that
e^{-(\delta A+l)+l}\leq 1
. Then

(3.11)

\displaystyle|\mathfrak{A}_{5}|\leq e^{-l}\Big|\int_{v_{3}<0}\mathfrak{w}(v)v_{3}\varphi_{A,\sigma}(v)\mathrm{d}v\Big|\,.

Collecting the all above estimates on
|\mathfrak{A}_{i}|
(
1\leq i\leq 5
) in (
3.7
)-(
3.8
)-(
3.9
)-(
3.10
)-(
3.11
), one knows that the inequality (
3.2
) holds. Consequently, the proof of Lemma
3.1
is finished. ∎

Based on the Nondissipative boundary lemma (Lemma
3.1
) above, we will deal with the boundary energy with the form
-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}v_{3}w_{*}(v)g_{\sigma}^{2}(0,v)\mathrm{d}v
, where the weight
w_{*}(v)
is to be determined later.

Lemma 3.2

(Boundary energy lemma)
.

Let
-3<\gamma\leq 1
,
l,A\geq 1
,
a>0
,
\mathbf{Z}\geq 0
and
0\leq\alpha_{*}\leq 1
. Assume that (
1.46
) holds, i.e.,
0<T_{w}<2T
. We further assume that the weight
w_{*}(v)
satisfies

(3.12)

\displaystyle w_{*}(R_{0}v)=w_{*}(v)\,,\quad 0\leq w_{*}(v)\lesssim e^{\frac{1}{8}(\frac{1}{2T_{w}}-\frac{1}{4T})|v-\mathfrak{u}|^{2}}\,,

and the small
\hbar>0
is such that

(3.13)

\displaystyle e^{\hbar\sigma(0,v)}\lesssim e^{C_{T,T_{w}}|v-\mathfrak{u}|^{2}}\,.

where
C_{T,T_{w}}=\min\{\tfrac{1}{8T},\frac{1}{8}(\frac{1}{2T_{w}}-\frac{1}{4T})\}>0
. Let
g_{\sigma}(x,v)
be a solution to (
3.1
). Then there is a constant
C_{0}>0
, independent of
l,\delta,A,\lambda,a,\hbar
, such that

(3.14)

\displaystyle-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}v_{3}w_{*}(v)g_{\sigma}^{2}(0,v)\mathrm{d}v\geq

\displaystyle[1-(1-\alpha_{*})^{2}]\int_{v_{3}<0}|v_{3}|w_{*}(v)g_{\sigma}^{2}(0,v)\mathrm{d}v

\displaystyle-C_{0}\alpha_{*}

\displaystyle(\digamma_{\delta,l,a,\mathbf{Z}}(g_{\sigma})+\digamma_{\delta,l,a,\mathbf{Z}}(\nu^{-1}h_{\sigma}))^{2}-C_{0}\alpha_{*}\||v_{3}|^{\frac{1}{2}}\varphi_{A,\sigma}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\Sigma_{-}^{A}}}\,,

where the functional
\digamma_{\delta,l,a,\mathbf{Z}}(\cdot)
is mentioned as in (
3.3
).

Proof.

Observe that the solution
g_{\sigma}(x,v)
satisfies the boundary condition

\displaystyle g_{\sigma}(0,v)|_{v_{3}>0}=(1-\alpha_{*})g_{\sigma}(0,R_{0}v)+\alpha_{*}\tfrac{M_{w}(v)}{\sqrt{\mathfrak{M}_{\sigma}(v)}}\int_{v_{3}^{\prime}<0}(-v_{3}^{\prime})g_{\sigma}(0,v^{\prime})\sqrt{\mathfrak{M}_{\sigma}(v^{\prime})}\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\,,

where
\mathfrak{M}_{\sigma}
is defined in (
2.2
). It then follows from a direct calculation that

\displaystyle-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}v_{3}w_{*}(v)

\displaystyle g_{\sigma}^{2}(0,v)\mathrm{d}v=\int_{v_{3}<0}|v_{3}|w_{*}(v)g_{\sigma}^{2}(0,v)\mathrm{d}v-\int_{v_{3}>0}|v_{3}|w_{*}(v)g_{\sigma}^{2}(0,v)\mathrm{d}v

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle[1-(1-\alpha_{*})^{2}]\int_{v_{3}<0}|v_{3}|w_{*}(v)g_{\sigma}^{2}(0,v)\mathrm{d}v

\displaystyle-\alpha_{*}^{2}\int_{v_{3}<0}|v_{3}|w_{*}(v)\tfrac{M_{w}^{2}(v)}{\mathfrak{M}_{\sigma}(v)}\mathrm{d}v\Big(\int_{v_{3}<0}|v_{3}|g_{\sigma}(0,v)\sqrt{\mathfrak{M}_{\sigma}(v)}\mathrm{d}v\Big)^{2}

(3.15)

\displaystyle-2\alpha_{*}(1-\alpha_{*})\int_{v_{3}<0}|v_{3}|w_{*}(v)\tfrac{M_{w}(v)}{\sqrt{\mathfrak{M}_{\sigma}(v)}}g_{\sigma}(0,v)\mathrm{d}v\int_{v_{3}<0}|v_{3}|g_{\sigma}(0,v)\sqrt{\mathfrak{M}_{\sigma}(v)}\mathrm{d}v\,,

where we have utilized the facts

\displaystyle\int_{v_{3}>0}|v_{3}|w_{*}(v)g_{\sigma}^{2}(0,R_{0}v)\mathrm{d}v=\int_{v_{3}<0}|v_{3}|w_{*}(v)g_{\sigma}^{2}(0,v)\mathrm{d}v\,,

\displaystyle\int_{v_{3}>0}|v_{3}|w_{*}(v)\tfrac{M_{w}(v)}{\sqrt{\mathfrak{M}_{\sigma}(v)}}g_{\sigma}(0,R_{0}v)\mathrm{d}v=\int_{v_{3}<0}|v_{3}|w_{*}(v)\tfrac{M_{w}(v)}{\sqrt{\mathfrak{M}_{\sigma}(v)}}g_{\sigma}(0,v)\mathrm{d}v\,,

\displaystyle\int_{v_{3}>0}|v_{3}|w_{*}(v)\tfrac{M_{w}^{2}(v)}{\mathfrak{M}_{\sigma}(v)}\mathrm{d}v=\int_{v_{3}<0}|v_{3}|w_{*}(v)\tfrac{M_{w}^{2}(v)}{\mathfrak{M}_{\sigma}(v)}\mathrm{d}v\,.

It is easy to see that

\displaystyle\tfrac{M_{w}(v)}{\sqrt{\mathfrak{M}(v)}}=

\displaystyle\tfrac{\sqrt{2\pi}T^{\frac{3}{2}}}{\rho T_{w}^{2}}\mathrm{exp}\{-(\tfrac{1}{2T_{w}}-\tfrac{1}{4T})|v-\mathfrak{u}|^{2}-\tfrac{1}{T_{w}}(\mathfrak{u}-u_{w})\cdot(v-\mathfrak{u})-\tfrac{|\mathfrak{u}-u_{w}|^{2}}{2T_{w}}\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\mathrm{exp}\{-\tfrac{1}{2}(\tfrac{1}{2T_{w}}-\tfrac{1}{4T})|v-\mathfrak{u}|^{2}\}\,.

Together with the assumptions (
3.12
)-(
3.13
) and the definition of
\mathfrak{M}_{\sigma}(v)
in (
2.2
), one obtains

\displaystyle w_{*}(v)\tfrac{M_{w}(v)}{\sqrt{\mathfrak{M}_{\sigma}(v)}}\leq C\mathrm{exp}\{-\tfrac{1}{4}(\tfrac{1}{2T_{w}}-\tfrac{1}{4T})|v-\mathfrak{u}|^{2}\}\,,

\displaystyle w_{*}(v)\tfrac{M_{w}^{2}(v)}{\mathfrak{M}_{\sigma}(v)}\leq C\mathrm{exp}\{-\tfrac{5}{8}(\tfrac{1}{2T_{w}}-\tfrac{1}{4T})|v-\mathfrak{u}|^{2}\}\,,\ \sqrt{\mathfrak{M}_{\sigma}(v)}\leq C\mathrm{exp}\{-\tfrac{1}{8T}|v-\mathfrak{u}|^{2}\}\,,

who all exponentially decay at
|v|\to+\infty
under the assumption
0<T_{w}<2T
.

We now take
\mathfrak{w}(v)=\sqrt{\mathfrak{M}_{\sigma}(v)}
or
w_{*}(v)\tfrac{M_{w}(v)}{\sqrt{\mathfrak{M}_{\sigma}(v)}}
in Lemma
3.1
. Then the inequality (
3.2
) tells us

(3.16)

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{v_{3}<0}|v_{3}|\mathfrak{w}(v)g_{\sigma}(0,v)\mathrm{d}v\Big|\leq

\displaystyle C(\digamma_{\delta,l,a,\mathbf{Z}}(g_{\sigma})+\digamma_{\delta,l,a,\mathbf{Z}}(\nu^{-1}h_{\sigma}))+\Big|\int_{v_{3}<0}\mathfrak{w}(v)v_{3}\varphi_{A,\sigma}(v)\mathrm{d}v\Big|

for any
a>0
,
0<\delta<1
and
l\geq 1
. Moreover, the Hölder inequality implies

(3.17)

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{v_{3}<0}\mathfrak{w}(v)v_{3}\varphi_{A,\sigma}(v)\mathrm{d}v\Big|\leq C\||v_{3}|^{\frac{1}{2}}\varphi_{A,\sigma}\|_{L^{2}_{\Sigma_{-}^{A}}}

for
\mathfrak{w}(v)=\sqrt{\mathfrak{M}_{\sigma}(v)}
or
w_{*}(v)\frac{M_{w}(v)}{\sqrt{\mathfrak{M}_{\sigma}(v)}}
. From substituting the bound (
3.17
) into (
3.16
), one has

(3.18)

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{v_{3}<0}|v_{3}|\mathfrak{w}(v)g_{\sigma}(0,v)\mathrm{d}v\Big|\leq C(\digamma_{\delta,l,a,\mathbf{Z}}(g_{\sigma})+\digamma_{\delta,l,a,\mathbf{Z}}(\nu^{-1}h_{\sigma}))+C\||v_{3}|^{\frac{1}{2}}\varphi_{A,\sigma}\|_{L^{2}_{\Sigma_{-}^{A}}}

for
\mathfrak{w}(v)=\sqrt{\mathfrak{M}_{\sigma}(v)}
or
w_{*}(v)\frac{M_{w}(v)}{\sqrt{\mathfrak{M}_{\sigma}(v)}}
and any
a>0
. Furthermore, one has

(3.19)

\displaystyle\int_{v_{3}<0}|v_{3}|w_{*}(v)\tfrac{M_{w}^{2}(v)}{\mathfrak{M}_{\sigma}(v)}\mathrm{d}v\leq C\int_{v_{3}<0}\mathrm{exp}\{-\tfrac{5}{8}(\tfrac{1}{2T_{w}}-\tfrac{1}{4T})|v-\mathfrak{u}|^{2}\}\mathrm{d}v\leq C\,.

As a consequence, the equality (
3.15
) and the bounds (
3.18
)-(
3.19
) conclude the estimate (
3.14
). The proof of Lemma
3.2
is therefore completed.
∎

3.2.
w_{\beta,\vartheta}
-weighted
L^{2}_{x,v}
estimates

In this subsection, the majority is to dominate the quantity
[\mathscr{E}^{A}_{2}(g_{\sigma})]^{2}
defined in (
1.41
) by employing the weighted
L^{2}_{x,v}
energy method. In order to study the uniqueness of the solution, we also should consider the difference
{\vartriangle}g_{\sigma}=g_{\sigma 2}-g_{\sigma 1}
, where
g_{\sigma i}
is the solution to (
3.1
) associated with the source term
h_{\sigma i}
for
i=1,2
. Then the difference
{\vartriangle}g_{\sigma}
subjects to

(3.20)

{\footnotesize\left\{\begin{aligned} &v_{3}\partial_{x}({\vartriangle}g_{\sigma})+[-\hbar\sigma_{x}v_{3}+\nu(v)]({\vartriangle}g_{\sigma})-(K_{\hbar}-\mathbf{D}_{\hbar})({\vartriangle}g_{\sigma})={\vartriangle}h_{\sigma}\,,\\
&({\vartriangle}g_{\sigma})(0,v)|_{v_{3}>0}=(1-\alpha_{*})({\vartriangle}g_{\sigma})(0,R_{0}v)+\alpha_{*}\tfrac{M_{w}(v)}{\sqrt{\mathfrak{M}_{\sigma}(v)}}\int_{v_{3}^{\prime}<0}(-v_{3}^{\prime})({\vartriangle}g_{\sigma})(0,v^{\prime})\sqrt{\mathfrak{M}_{\sigma}(v^{\prime})}\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\,,\\
&({\vartriangle}g_{\sigma})(A,v)|_{v_{3}<0}=0\,,\end{aligned}\right.}

where
{\vartriangle}h_{\sigma}:=h_{\sigma 2}-h_{\sigma 1}
. More precisely, the following lemma holds.

Lemma 3.3
.

Let
-3<\gamma\leq 1
,
0\leq\alpha_{*}\leq 1
,
A\geq 1
,
a>0
,
\mathbf{Z}\geq 0
and
\beta\in\mathbb{R}
. Moreover, the parameters
\delta,l,\hbar,\vartheta
are given in Lemma
2.6
with further constraint
\hbar\leq o(1)\delta
, where
0<o(1)\ll 1
is independent of
\delta,l,\hbar
and
A
. Assume that
g_{\sigma}(x,v)
is a solution to (
3.1
). Then there is a constant
C>0
, independent of
A,\delta,l,a,\mathbf{Z}
and
\hbar
, such that

(3.21)

\displaystyle[1-(1-\alpha_{*})^{2}]\||v_{3}|^{\frac{1}{2}}w_{\beta,\vartheta}g_{\sigma}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\Sigma_{+}^{0}}}+\||v_{3}|^{\frac{1}{2}}w_{\beta,\vartheta}g_{\sigma}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\Sigma_{+}^{A}}}+\mathscr{E}^{A}_{2}(g_{\sigma})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\mathscr{A}^{A}_{2}(h_{\sigma})+C\mathscr{B}_{2}(\varphi_{A,\sigma})+C\alpha_{*}\big(\digamma_{\delta,l,a,\mathbf{Z}}^{2}(g_{\sigma})+\digamma_{\delta,l,a,\mathbf{Z}}^{2}(\nu^{-1}h_{\sigma})\big)\,,

where the functionals
\mathscr{E}^{A}_{2}(\cdot)
,
\mathscr{A}^{A}_{2}(\cdot)
,
\mathscr{B}_{2}(\cdot)
and
\digamma_{\delta,l,a,\mathbf{Z}}(\cdot)
are defined in (
1.41
), (
1.43
), (
1.44
) and (
3.3
), respectively. Moreover, the difference
{\vartriangle}g_{\sigma}
enjoys the bound

(3.22)

\displaystyle[1-(1-\alpha_{*})^{2}]\||v_{3}|^{\frac{1}{2}}w_{\beta,\vartheta}{\vartriangle}g_{\sigma}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\Sigma_{+}}}+\||v_{3}|^{\frac{1}{2}}w_{\beta,\vartheta}{\vartriangle}g_{\sigma}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\Sigma_{+}^{A}}}+\mathscr{E}^{A}_{2}({\vartriangle}g_{\sigma})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\mathscr{A}^{A}_{2}({\vartriangle}h_{\sigma})+C\alpha_{*}\big(\digamma_{\delta,l,a,\mathbf{Z}}^{2}({\vartriangle}g_{\sigma})+\digamma_{\delta,l,a,\mathbf{Z}}^{2}(\nu^{-1}{\vartriangle}h_{\sigma})\big)\,.

Proof of Lemma
3.3
.

Multiplying (
3.1
) by
w_{\beta,\vartheta}^{2}g_{\sigma}
and integrating by parts over
(x,v)\in\Omega_{A}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
, we have

\displaystyle\iint_{\Omega_{A}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}v_{3}\partial_{x}g_{\sigma}\cdot w_{\beta,\vartheta}^{2}g_{\sigma}\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}x+\mathscr{W}(g_{\sigma})=\iint_{\Omega_{A}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}h_{\sigma}\cdot w_{\beta,\vartheta}^{2}g_{\sigma}\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}x\,,

where

\displaystyle\mathscr{W}(g_{\sigma})=

\displaystyle\iint_{\Omega_{A}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}[-\hbar\sigma_{x}v_{3}+\nu(v)]g_{\sigma}\cdot w_{\beta,\vartheta}^{2}g_{\sigma}\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}x-\iint_{\Omega_{A}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}(K_{\hbar}-\mathbf{D}_{\hbar})g_{\sigma}\cdot w_{\beta,\vartheta}^{2}g_{\sigma}\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}x\,.

Note that

\displaystyle\iint_{\Omega_{A}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}v_{3}\partial_{x}g_{\sigma}\cdot w_{\beta,\vartheta}^{2}g_{\sigma}\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}x=\tfrac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}v_{3}w_{\beta,\vartheta}^{2}g_{\sigma}^{2}(A,v)\mathrm{d}v-\tfrac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}v_{3}w_{\beta,\vartheta}^{2}g_{\sigma}^{2}(0,v)\mathrm{d}v\,.

The boundary condition in (
3.1
) reduces to

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}v_{3}w_{\beta,\vartheta}^{2}g_{\sigma}^{2}(A,v)\mathrm{d}v=\tfrac{1}{2}\||v_{3}|^{\frac{1}{2}}w_{\beta,\vartheta}g_{\sigma}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\Sigma_{+}^{A}}}-\tfrac{1}{2}\||v_{3}|^{\frac{1}{2}}w_{\beta,\vartheta}\varphi_{A,\sigma}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\Sigma_{-}^{A}}}\,.

Moreover, it follows from Lemma
3.2
that

\displaystyle-\tfrac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}v_{3}w_{\beta,\vartheta}^{2}g_{\sigma}^{2}(0,v)\mathrm{d}v\geq

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{2}[1-(1-\alpha_{*})^{2}]\||v_{3}|^{\frac{1}{2}}w_{\beta,\vartheta}g_{\sigma}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\Sigma_{+}^{0}}}-C\alpha_{*}\||v_{3}|^{\frac{1}{2}}\varphi_{A,\sigma}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\Sigma_{-}^{A}}}

\displaystyle-C\alpha_{*}\big(\digamma_{\delta,l,a,\mathbf{Z}}(g_{\sigma})+\digamma_{\delta,l,a,\mathbf{Z}}(\nu^{-1}h_{\sigma})\big)^{2}\,.

It is easy to see that
\||v_{3}|^{\frac{1}{2}}\varphi_{A,\sigma}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\Sigma_{-}^{A}}}\leq C\mathscr{B}_{2}(\varphi_{A,\sigma})
. As a consequence, one has

\displaystyle\iint_{\Omega_{A}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}v_{3}\partial_{x}g_{\sigma}\cdot w_{\beta,\vartheta}^{2}g_{\sigma}\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{2}[1-(1-\alpha_{*})^{2}]\||v_{3}|^{\frac{1}{2}}w_{\beta,\vartheta}g_{\sigma}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\Sigma_{+}^{0}}}+\tfrac{1}{2}\||v_{3}|^{\frac{1}{2}}w_{\beta,\vartheta}g_{\sigma}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\Sigma_{+}^{A}}}-C\mathscr{B}_{2}(\varphi_{A,\sigma})

(3.23)

\displaystyle-C\alpha_{*}\big(\digamma_{\delta,l,a,\mathbf{Z}}(g_{\sigma})+\digamma_{\delta,l,a,\mathbf{Z}}(\nu^{-1}h_{\sigma})\big)^{2}\,.

Recalling
\mathcal{L}_{\hbar}g_{\sigma}=\nu(v)g_{\sigma}-K_{\hbar}g_{\sigma}
, there holds

\displaystyle\mathscr{W}(g_{\sigma})=\iint_{\Omega_{A}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathcal{L}_{\hbar}g_{\sigma}\cdot w_{\beta,\vartheta}^{2}g_{\sigma}\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}x+\iint_{\Omega_{A}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}(\mathbf{D}_{\hbar}g_{\sigma}-\hbar\sigma_{x}v_{3}g_{\sigma})w_{\beta,\vartheta}^{2}g_{\sigma}\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}x\,.

From using Lemma
2.6
, it follows that

(3.24)

\displaystyle\iint_{\Omega_{A}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}(\mathbf{D}_{\hbar}g_{\sigma}-\hbar\sigma_{x}v_{3}g_{\sigma})w_{\beta,\vartheta}^{2}g_{\sigma}\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}x\geq

\displaystyle\mu_{0}\delta\hbar\|(\delta x+l)^{-\frac{1-\gamma}{2(3-\gamma)}}\mathcal{P}w_{\beta,\vartheta}g_{\sigma}\|^{2}_{A}-\lambda C\hbar^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\nu^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{P}^{\perp}w_{\beta,\vartheta}g_{\sigma}\|^{2}_{A}\,.

Moreover, by Lemma 2.2 of
[
3
]
(or Lemma 2.4 of
[
27
]
) and the similar arguments in Corollary 1 of
[
23
]
, it infers that

(3.25)

\displaystyle\iint_{\Omega_{A}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathcal{L}_{\hbar}g_{\sigma}\cdot w_{\beta,\vartheta}^{2}g_{\sigma}\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}x\geq\lambda\mu_{2}\|\nu^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{P}^{\perp}w_{\beta,\vartheta}g_{\sigma}\|^{2}_{A}-C\hbar^{2}\|(\delta x+l)^{-\frac{1-\gamma}{2(3-\gamma)}}\mathcal{P}w_{\beta,\vartheta}g_{\sigma}\|^{2}_{A}

for
\mu_{2}>0
. Take small
\hbar\geq 0
such that
\mu_{2}-C\hbar^{\frac{1}{2}}\geq\frac{1}{2}\mu_{2}
and
\mu_{0}\delta-C\hbar\geq\frac{1}{2}\mu_{0}\delta
. Then

(3.26)

\displaystyle\mathscr{W}(g_{\sigma})\geq

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{2}\mu_{2}\|\nu^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{P}^{\perp}w_{\beta,\vartheta}g_{\sigma}\|^{2}_{A}+\tfrac{1}{2}\mu_{0}\delta\hbar\|(\delta x+l)^{-\frac{1-\gamma}{2(3-\gamma)}}\mathcal{P}w_{\beta,\vartheta}g_{\sigma}\|^{2}_{A}\geq c_{0}\mathscr{E}_{2}^{A}(g_{\sigma})\,,

where the functional
\mathscr{E}_{2}^{A}(\cdot)
is given in (
1.41
).

We then control the quantity
\iint_{\Omega_{A}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}h_{\sigma}\cdot w_{\beta,\vartheta}^{2}g_{\sigma}\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}x
. By the Hölder inequality one has

\displaystyle\iint_{\Omega_{A}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}h_{\sigma}\cdot w_{\beta,\vartheta}^{2}g_{\sigma}\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}x=

\displaystyle\iint_{\Omega_{A}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathcal{P}w_{\beta,\vartheta}h_{\sigma}\cdot\mathcal{P}w_{\beta,\vartheta}g_{\sigma}\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}x+\iint_{\Omega_{A}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathcal{P}^{\perp}w_{\beta,\vartheta}h_{\sigma}\cdot\mathcal{P}^{\perp}w_{\beta,\vartheta}g_{\sigma}\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{2}c_{0}\delta\hbar\|(\delta x+l)^{-\frac{1-\gamma}{2(3-\gamma)}}\mathcal{P}w_{\beta,\vartheta}g_{\sigma}\|^{2}_{A}+C(\delta\hbar)^{-1}\|(\delta x+l)^{\frac{1-\gamma}{2(3-\gamma)}}\mathcal{P}w_{\beta,\vartheta}h_{\sigma}\|^{2}_{A}

(3.27)

\displaystyle+\tfrac{1}{2}c_{0}\|\nu^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{P}^{\perp}w_{\beta,\vartheta}g_{\sigma}\|^{2}_{A}+C\|\nu^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{P}^{\perp}w_{\beta,\vartheta}h_{\sigma}\|^{2}_{A}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{2}c_{0}\mathscr{E}_{2}^{A}(g_{\sigma})+C\mathscr{A}_{2}^{A}(h_{\sigma})\,.

Collecting the all above estimates (
3.23
), (
3.26
) and (
3.27
), one concludes the bound (
3.21
). Moreover, as similar arguments as in (
3.21
), the difference
{\vartriangle}g_{\sigma}:=g_{\sigma 2}-g_{\sigma 1}
enjoys the estimate (
3.22
). Therefore, the proof of Lemma
3.3
is completed.
∎

We remark that the weighted
L^{2}_{x,v}
estimate (
3.21
) in Lemma
3.3
is not closed due to the quantity
\alpha_{*}\digamma_{\delta,l,a,\mathbf{Z}}^{2}(g_{\sigma})
. Recalling the definition of
\digamma_{\delta,l,a,\mathbf{Z}}(g_{\sigma})
in (
3.3
), one has

\displaystyle\digamma_{\delta,l,a,\mathbf{Z}}^{2}(g_{\sigma})\lesssim

\displaystyle\delta^{-1}l^{-100}\|(\delta x+l)^{-\frac{1-\gamma}{2(3-\gamma)}}\nu^{\frac{1}{2}}g_{\sigma}\|_{A}^{2}

\displaystyle+\delta^{-1}l^{-(2a-\frac{1-\gamma}{3-\gamma})}\|(l^{-\mathbf{Z}}\delta x+l)^{a}(\delta x+l)^{\-\frac{1-\gamma}{2(3-\gamma)}}\nu^{\frac{1}{2}}g_{\sigma}\|_{A}^{2}

for any
a>0
and
\mathbf{Z}\geq 0
. By (
1.41
), it is easy to see that
\|(\delta x+l)^{-\frac{1-\gamma}{2(3-\gamma)}}\nu^{\frac{1}{2}}g_{\sigma}\|_{A}^{2}\lesssim(\delta\hbar)^{-1}\mathscr{E}^{A}_{2}(g_{\sigma})
, which means that

\displaystyle\delta^{-1}l^{-100}\|(\delta x+l)^{-\frac{1-\gamma}{2(3-\gamma)}}\nu^{\frac{1}{2}}g_{\sigma}\|_{A}^{2}\leq(\delta\hbar)^{-1}\delta^{-1}l^{-100}\mathscr{E}^{A}_{2}(g_{\sigma})\,.

Consequently, one has

(3.28)

\displaystyle\digamma_{\delta,l,a,\mathbf{Z}}^{2}(g_{\sigma})\lesssim(\delta\hbar)^{-1}\delta^{-1}l^{-100}\mathscr{E}^{A}_{2}(g_{\sigma})+\delta^{-1}l^{-(2a-\frac{1-\gamma}{3-\gamma})}\|(l^{-\mathbf{Z}}\delta x+l)^{a}(\delta x+l)^{-\frac{1-\gamma}{2(3-\gamma)}}\nu^{\frac{1}{2}}g_{\sigma}\|_{A}^{2}\,.

Then, together with (
3.21
), one gains the following corollary.

Corollary 3.1
.

Under the same assumptions in Lemma
3.3
, there holds

(3.29)

\displaystyle\mathscr{E}^{A}_{2}(g_{\sigma})\lesssim

\displaystyle\alpha_{*}(\delta\hbar)^{-1}\delta^{-1}l^{-(2a-\frac{1-\gamma}{3-\gamma})}\|(l^{-\mathbf{Z}}\delta x+l)^{a}(\delta x+l)^{-\frac{1-\gamma}{2(3-\gamma)}}\nu^{\frac{1}{2}}g_{\sigma}\|_{A}^{2}

\displaystyle+\alpha_{*}(\delta\hbar)^{-1}\delta^{-1}l^{-100}\mathscr{E}^{A}_{2}(g_{\sigma})+\alpha_{*}\digamma_{\delta,l,a,\mathbf{Z}}^{2}(\nu^{-1}h_{\sigma})+\mathscr{A}^{A}_{2}(h_{\sigma})+\mathscr{B}_{2}(\varphi_{A,\sigma})

for any
\mathbf{Z}\geq 0
and
a>0
.

Remark that the quantity
\alpha_{*}(\delta\hbar)^{-1}\delta^{-1}l^{-100}\mathscr{E}^{A}_{2}(g_{\sigma})
can be absorbed by the quantity
\mathscr{E}^{A}_{2}(g_{\sigma})
in the left-hand side of (
3.29
), provided that
(\delta\hbar)^{-1}\delta^{-1}l^{-100}
is sufficiently small. Therefore, the quantity

\alpha_{*}(\delta\hbar)^{-1}\delta^{-1}l^{-(2a-\frac{1-\gamma}{3-\gamma})}\|(l^{-\mathbf{Z}}\delta x+l)^{a}(\delta x+l)^{-\frac{1-\gamma}{2(3-\gamma)}}\nu^{\frac{1}{2}}g_{\sigma}\|_{A}^{2}

in the right-hand side of (
3.29
) is the only one that should be further dominated by developing the so-called Spatial-velocity indices iteration approach, as given in next subsection.

3.3.
Spatial-velocity indices iteration approach

The main goal of this subsection is to control the quantity
\alpha_{*}(\delta\hbar)^{-1}\delta^{-1}l^{-(2a-\frac{1-\gamma}{3-\gamma})}\|(l^{-\mathbf{Z}}\delta x+l)^{a}(\delta x+l)^{-\frac{1-\gamma}{2(3-\gamma)}}\nu^{\frac{1}{2}}g_{\sigma}\|_{A}^{2}
in the right-hand side of (
3.29
). One emphasizes that the small factor
l^{-(2a-\frac{1-\gamma}{3-\gamma})}
is dominant when
a>\frac{1-\gamma}{2(3-\gamma)}
, while
\alpha_{*}(\delta\hbar)^{-1}\delta^{-1}
is subdominant. In other words, the smallness of the factor
l^{-(2a-\frac{1-\gamma}{3-\gamma})}
is based on sacrificing a positive power of the spatial polynomial weight
(l^{-\mathbf{Z}}\delta x+l)^{a}(\delta x+l)^{-\frac{1-\gamma}{2(3-\gamma)}}
. In order to deal with this weight, we try to develop a so-called spatial-velocity indices iteration approach to shift the power of spatial polynomial weight
(l^{-\mathbf{Z}}\delta x+l)^{a}
to a controllable velocity one, while the factor
(\delta x+l)^{-\frac{1-\gamma}{2(3-\gamma)}}
coincides with the weight in the functional
\mathscr{E}_{2}^{A}(g_{\sigma})
(see (
1.41
)) resulting from the coercivity of the operators
\mathcal{L}
and
\mathbf{D}
.

For any
\mathtt{q},\beta_{\sharp}\in\mathbb{R}
,
\mathbf{Z}\geq 0
and
a>0
, we define the spatial-velocity indices functions as

(3.30)

\displaystyle\psi_{\mathbf{Z};\mathtt{q},\beta_{\sharp}}:=

\displaystyle\delta\hbar\|(l^{-\mathbf{Z}}\delta x+l)^{\mathtt{q}}(\delta x+l)^{-\frac{1-\gamma}{2(3-\gamma)}}\mathcal{P}w_{\beta_{\sharp},0}g_{\sigma}\|^{2}_{A}+\|(l^{-\mathbf{Z}}\delta x+l)^{\mathtt{q}}\nu^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{P}^{\perp}w_{\beta_{\sharp},0}g_{\sigma}\|^{2}_{A}\,,

\displaystyle y_{a,\mathbf{Z};\mathtt{q},\beta_{\sharp}}:=

\displaystyle\alpha_{*}l^{2\mathtt{q}}\digamma_{\delta,l,a,\mathbf{Z}}^{2}(\nu^{-1}h_{\sigma})+(\delta\hbar)^{-1}\|(l^{-\mathbf{Z}}\delta x+l)^{\mathtt{q}}(\delta x+l)^{\frac{1-\gamma}{2(3-\gamma)}}\mathcal{P}w_{\beta_{\sharp},0}h_{\sigma}\|^{2}_{A}

\displaystyle+\alpha_{*}l^{2\mathtt{q}}\||v_{3}|^{\frac{1}{2}}(l^{-\mathbf{Z}}\delta A+l)^{\mathtt{q}}w_{\beta_{\sharp},0}\varphi_{A,\sigma}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\Sigma_{-}^{A}}}+\|(l^{-\mathbf{Z}}\delta x+l)^{\mathtt{q}}\nu^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{P}^{\perp}w_{\beta_{\sharp},0}h_{\sigma}\|^{2}_{A}\,,

where the functional
\digamma_{\delta,l,a,\mathbf{Z}}(\cdot)
is defined in (
3.3
). Here
\mathtt{q}
is the spatial polynomial weighted index, and
\beta_{\sharp}
stands for the velocity polynomial weighted index.

First, we establish the following spatial-velocity indices iteration lemma.

Lemma 3.4

(Spatial-velocity indices iteration form)
.

Let
-3<\gamma\leq 1
,
0\leq\alpha_{*}\leq 1
,
\beta_{\sharp},\mathtt{q}\in\mathbb{R}
,
A\geq 1
,
a>0
,
\mathbf{Z}\geq 0
. The parameters
\delta,\hbar,l
are given in Lemma
2.6
. Then there is a constant
C>0
, independent of
A,\delta,l,a,\mathbf{Z}
and
\hbar
, such that

(3.31)

\displaystyle\psi_{\mathbf{Z};\mathtt{q},\beta_{\sharp}}\leq

\displaystyle C|\mathtt{q}|l^{-\frac{2}{3-\gamma}\mathbf{Z}}\delta(\delta\hbar)^{-1}\psi_{\mathbf{Z};\mathtt{q}-\frac{1}{3-\gamma},\beta_{\sharp}+\beta_{\gamma}+\frac{1}{2}}+C\alpha_{*}\delta^{-1}l^{2\mathtt{q}}l^{-(2a-\frac{1-\gamma}{3-\gamma})}(\delta\hbar)^{-1}\psi_{\mathbf{Z};a,0}

\displaystyle+C(\delta\hbar)^{-2}\delta^{-1}l^{-100}\mathscr{E}^{A}_{2}(g_{\sigma})+Cy_{a,\mathbf{Z};\mathtt{q},\beta_{\sharp}}\,,

where
\beta_{\gamma}
is given in (
1.31
), and the functional
\mathscr{E}^{A}_{2}(\cdot)
is defined in (
1.41
).

Proof.

From multiplying (
3.1
) by
(l^{-\mathbf{Z}}\delta x+l)^{2\mathtt{q}}w_{\beta_{\sharp},0}^{2}g_{\sigma}
, it follows that

\displaystyle\iint_{\Omega_{A}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}\partial_{x}\big(\tfrac{1}{2}v_{3}(l^{-\mathbf{Z}}\delta x+l)^{2\mathtt{q}}w_{\beta_{\sharp},0}^{2}g_{\sigma}^{2}\big)\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}x-\mathtt{q}l^{-\mathbf{Z}}\delta\iint_{\Omega_{A}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}v_{3}(l^{-\mathbf{Z}}\delta x+l)^{2\mathtt{q}-1}w_{\beta_{\sharp},0}^{2}g_{\sigma}^{2}\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}x

(3.32)

\displaystyle+\iint_{\Omega_{A}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}(\nu(v)-K_{\hbar})g_{\sigma}\cdot(l^{-\mathbf{Z}}\delta x+l)^{2\mathtt{q}}w_{\beta_{\sharp},0}^{2}g_{\sigma}\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle+\iint_{\Omega_{A}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}[\mathbf{D}_{\hbar}-\hbar\sigma_{x}v_{3}]g_{\sigma}(l^{-\mathbf{Z}}\delta x+l)^{2\mathtt{q}}w_{\beta_{\sharp},0}^{2}g_{\sigma}\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}x=\iint_{\Omega_{A}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}h_{\sigma}\cdot(l^{-\mathbf{Z}}\delta x+l)^{2\mathtt{q}}w_{\beta_{\sharp},0}^{2}g_{\sigma}\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}x\,.

Observe that

\displaystyle\iint_{\Omega_{A}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}\partial_{x}\big(\tfrac{1}{2}v_{3}(l^{-\mathbf{Z}}\delta x+l)^{2\mathtt{q}}w_{\beta_{\sharp},0}^{2}g_{\sigma}^{2}\big)\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}v_{3}(l^{-\mathbf{Z}}\delta A+l)^{2\mathtt{q}}w_{\beta_{\sharp},0}^{2}g_{\sigma}^{2}(A,v)\mathrm{d}v-\tfrac{1}{2}l^{2\mathtt{q}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}v_{3}w_{\beta_{\sharp},0}^{2}g_{\sigma}^{2}(0,v)\mathrm{d}v\,.

It follows from the boundary condition in (
3.1
) that

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}v_{3}(l^{-\mathbf{Z}}\delta A+l)^{2\mathtt{q}}w_{\beta_{\sharp},0}^{2}g_{\sigma}^{2}(A,v)\mathrm{d}v

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{2}\||v_{3}|^{\frac{1}{2}}(l^{-\mathbf{Z}}\delta A+l)^{\mathtt{q}}w_{\beta_{\sharp},0}g_{\sigma}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\Sigma_{+}^{A}}}-\tfrac{1}{2}\||v_{3}|^{\frac{1}{2}}(l^{-\mathbf{Z}}\delta A+l)^{\mathtt{q}}w_{\beta_{\sharp},0}\varphi_{A,\sigma}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\Sigma_{-}^{A}}}\,.

Then Lemma
3.2
indicates that

\displaystyle-\tfrac{1}{2}l^{2\mathtt{q}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}v_{3}w_{\beta_{\sharp},0}^{2}g_{\sigma}^{2}(0,v)\mathrm{d}v

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle[1-(1-\alpha_{*})^{2}]l^{2\mathtt{q}}\||v_{3}|^{\frac{1}{2}}w_{\beta_{\sharp},0}g_{\sigma}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\Sigma_{+}^{0}}}-C\alpha_{*}l^{2\mathtt{q}}\||v_{3}|^{\frac{1}{2}}(l^{-\mathbf{Z}}\delta A+l)^{\mathtt{q}}w_{\beta_{\sharp},0}\varphi_{A,\sigma}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\Sigma_{-}^{A}}}

\displaystyle-C_{0}\alpha_{*}l^{2\mathtt{q}}(\digamma_{\delta,l,a,\mathbf{Z}}(g_{\sigma})+\digamma_{\delta,l,a,\mathbf{Z}}(\nu^{-1}h_{\sigma}))^{2}\,.

In summary, one has

\displaystyle\iint_{\Omega_{A}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}\partial_{x}\big(\tfrac{1}{2}v_{3}(l^{-\mathbf{Z}}\delta x+l)^{2\mathtt{q}}w_{\beta_{\sharp},0}^{2}g_{\sigma}^{2}\big)\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}x

(3.33)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{2}[1-(1-\alpha_{*})^{2}]l^{2\mathtt{q}}\||v_{3}|^{\frac{1}{2}}w_{\beta_{\sharp},0}g_{\sigma}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\Sigma_{+}^{0}}}+\tfrac{1}{2}\||v_{3}|^{\frac{1}{2}}(l^{-\mathbf{Z}}\delta A+l)^{\mathtt{q}}w_{\beta_{\sharp},0}g_{\sigma}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\Sigma_{+}^{A}}}

\displaystyle-C\alpha_{*}l^{2\mathtt{q}}(\digamma_{\delta,l,a,\mathbf{Z}}^{2}(g_{\sigma})+\digamma_{\delta,l,a,\mathbf{Z}}^{2}(\nu^{-1}h_{\sigma}))-C\alpha_{*}l^{2\mathtt{q}}\||v_{3}|^{\frac{1}{2}}(l^{-\mathbf{Z}}\delta A+l)^{\mathtt{q}}w_{\beta_{\sharp},0}\varphi_{A,\sigma}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\Sigma_{-}^{A}}}\,.

Moreover, the similar arguments in (
3.25
) show that

\displaystyle\iint_{\Omega_{A}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}(\nu(v)-K_{\hbar})g_{\sigma}\cdot(l^{-\mathbf{Z}}\delta x+l)^{2\mathtt{q}}w_{\beta_{\sharp},0}^{2}g_{\sigma}\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}x\geq\mu_{2}\|(l^{-\mathbf{Z}}\delta x+l)^{\mathtt{q}}\nu^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{P}^{\perp}w_{\beta_{\sharp},0}g_{\sigma}\|^{2}_{A}

\displaystyle-C\hbar^{2}\|(l^{-\mathbf{Z}}\delta x+l)^{\mathtt{q}}(\delta x+l)^{-\frac{1-\gamma}{2(3-\gamma)}}\mathcal{P}w_{\beta_{\sharp},0}g_{\sigma}\|^{2}_{A}\,.

As similarly in (
3.24
), one further obtains

\displaystyle\iint_{\Omega_{A}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}[\mathbf{D}_{\hbar}-\hbar\sigma_{x}v_{3}]g_{\sigma}(l^{-\mathbf{Z}}\delta x+l)^{2\mathtt{q}}w_{\beta_{\sharp},0}^{2}g_{\sigma}\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\mu_{0}\delta\hbar\|(l^{-\mathbf{Z}}\delta x+l)^{\mathtt{q}}(\delta x+l)^{-\frac{1-\gamma}{2(3-\gamma)}}\mathcal{P}w_{\beta_{\sharp},0}g_{\sigma}\|^{2}_{A}-C\hbar^{\frac{1}{2}}\|(l^{-\mathbf{Z}}\delta x+l)^{\mathtt{q}}\nu^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{P}^{\perp}w_{\beta_{\sharp},0}g_{\sigma}\|^{2}_{A}\,.

By taking small
\hbar\geq 0
such that
\mu_{2}-C\hbar^{\frac{1}{2}}\geq\frac{1}{2}\mu_{2}
and
\mu_{0}\delta-C\hbar\geq\frac{1}{2}\mu_{0}\delta
, one gains

\displaystyle\iint_{\Omega_{A}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}(\nu(v)-K_{\hbar})g_{\sigma}\cdot(l^{-\mathbf{Z}}\delta x+l)^{2\mathtt{q}}w_{\beta_{\sharp},0}^{2}g_{\sigma}\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}x

(3.34)

\displaystyle+\iint_{\Omega_{A}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}[\mathbf{D}_{\hbar}-\hbar\sigma_{x}v_{3}]g_{\sigma}(l^{-\mathbf{Z}}\delta x+l)^{2\mathtt{q}}w_{\beta_{\sharp},0}^{2}g_{\sigma}\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle c_{0}\delta\hbar l^{-\frac{1-\gamma}{3-\gamma}}\|(l^{-\mathbf{Z}}\delta x+l)^{\mathtt{q}}(\delta x+l)^{-\frac{1-\gamma}{2(3-\gamma)}}\mathcal{P}w_{\beta_{\sharp},0}g_{\sigma}\|^{2}_{A}+c_{0}\|(l^{-\mathbf{Z}}\delta x+l)^{\mathtt{q}}\nu^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{P}^{\perp}w_{\beta_{\sharp},0}g_{\sigma}\|^{2}_{A}\,,

where
c_{0}=\min\{\frac{1}{2}\mu_{0},\frac{1}{2}\mu_{2}\}>0
.

Furthermore, it follow from similar arguments in (
3.27
) that

\displaystyle|\iint_{\Omega_{A}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}h_{\sigma}\cdot(l^{-\mathbf{Z}}\delta x+l)^{2\mathtt{q}}w_{\beta_{\sharp},0}^{2}g_{\sigma}\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}x|

(3.35)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\tfrac{c_{0}}{2}\big(\delta\hbar\|(l^{-\mathbf{Z}}\delta x+l)^{\mathtt{q}}(\delta x+l)^{-\frac{1-\gamma}{2(3-\gamma)}}\mathcal{P}w_{\beta_{\sharp},0}g_{\sigma}\|^{2}_{A}+\|(l^{-\mathbf{Z}}\delta x+l)^{\mathtt{q}}\mathcal{P}^{\perp}w_{\beta_{\sharp},0}g_{\sigma}\|^{2}_{A}\big)

\displaystyle+C\big((\delta\hbar)^{-1}\|(l^{-\mathbf{Z}}\delta x+l)^{\mathtt{q}}(\delta x+l)^{\frac{1-\gamma}{2(3-\gamma)}}\mathcal{P}w_{\beta_{\sharp},0}h_{\sigma}\|^{2}_{A}+\|(l^{-\mathbf{Z}}\delta x+l)^{\mathtt{q}}\mathcal{P}^{\perp}w_{\beta_{\sharp},0}h_{\sigma}\|^{2}_{A}\big)\,.

Note that
|v_{3}|\lesssim\tfrac{\nu(v)|v-\mathfrak{u}|}{(1+|v-\mathfrak{u}|)^{\gamma}}\lesssim\nu(v)(1+|v-\mathfrak{u}|)^{2\beta_{\gamma}+1}
, where
\beta_{\gamma}
is given in (
1.31
). Moreover,
(\delta x+l)^{\frac{1-\gamma}{3-\gamma}}\leq l^{\frac{1-\gamma}{3-\gamma}\mathbf{Z}}(l^{-\mathbf{Z}}\delta x+l)^{\frac{1-\gamma}{3-\gamma}}
for
l\geq 1
,
\mathbf{Z}\geq 0
and
-3<\gamma\leq 1
. Then

\displaystyle|\mathtt{q}l^{-\mathbf{Z}}\delta\iint_{\Omega_{A}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}v_{3}(l^{-\mathbf{Z}}\delta x+l)^{2\mathtt{q}-1}w_{\beta_{\sharp},0}^{2}g_{\sigma}^{2}\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}x|

(3.36)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle|\mathtt{q}|l^{-\mathbf{Z}}\delta\iint_{\Omega_{A}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}|v_{3}|(l^{-\mathbf{Z}}\delta x+l)^{2\mathtt{q}-1}w_{\beta_{\sharp},0}^{2}g_{\sigma}^{2}\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C|\mathtt{q}|l^{-\mathbf{Z}}\delta\iint_{\Omega_{A}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}\nu(v)(1+|v-\mathfrak{u}|)^{2\beta_{\gamma}+1}(l^{-\mathbf{Z}}\delta x+l)^{2\mathtt{q}-1}(\delta x+l)^{\frac{1-\gamma}{3-\gamma}-\frac{1-\gamma}{3-\gamma}}w_{\beta_{\sharp},0}^{2}g_{\sigma}^{2}\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C|\mathtt{q}|l^{-\frac{2}{3-\gamma}\mathbf{Z}}\delta\|(l^{-\mathbf{Z}}\delta x+l)^{\mathtt{q}-\frac{1}{3-\gamma}}(\delta x+l)^{-\frac{1-\gamma}{2(3-\gamma)}}\nu^{\frac{1}{2}}w_{\beta_{\sharp}+\beta_{\gamma}+\frac{1}{2},0}g_{\sigma}\|^{2}_{A}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C|\mathtt{q}|l^{-\frac{2}{3-\gamma}\mathbf{Z}}\delta(\delta\hbar)^{-1}\psi_{\mathtt{q}-\frac{1}{3-\gamma},\beta_{\sharp}+\beta_{\gamma}+\frac{1}{2}}\,,

where the quantity
\psi_{\mathtt{q}-\frac{1}{3-\gamma},\beta_{\sharp}+\beta_{\gamma}+\frac{1}{2}}
is defined in (
3.30
).

Then collecting the estimates (
3.32
), (
3.33
), (
3.34
), (
3.35
) and (
3.36
) can conclude that

\displaystyle[1-(1-\alpha_{*})^{2}]l^{2\mathtt{q}}\||v_{3}|^{\frac{1}{2}}w_{\beta_{\sharp},0}g_{\sigma}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\Sigma_{+}^{0}}}+\||v_{3}|^{\frac{1}{2}}(l^{-\mathbf{Z}}\delta A+l)^{\mathtt{q}}w_{\beta_{\sharp},0}g_{\sigma}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\Sigma_{+}^{A}}}

\displaystyle+\|(l^{-\mathbf{Z}}\delta x+l)^{\mathtt{q}}\nu^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{P}^{\perp}w_{\beta_{\sharp},0}g_{\sigma}\|^{2}_{A}+\delta\hbar\|(l^{-\mathbf{Z}}\delta x+l)^{\mathtt{q}}(\delta x+l)^{-\frac{1-\gamma}{2(3-\gamma)}}\mathcal{P}w_{\beta_{\sharp},0}g_{\sigma}\|^{2}_{A}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C|\mathtt{q}|l^{-\frac{2}{3-\gamma}\mathbf{Z}}\delta(\delta\hbar)^{-1}\psi_{\mathtt{q}-\frac{1}{3-\gamma},\beta_{\sharp}+\beta_{\gamma}+\frac{1}{2}}+C_{0}\alpha_{*}l^{2\mathtt{q}}(\digamma_{\delta,l,a,\mathbf{Z}}^{2}(g_{\sigma})+\digamma_{\delta,l,a,\mathbf{Z}}^{2}(\nu^{-1}h_{\sigma}))

\displaystyle+C(\delta\hbar)^{-1}\|(l^{-\mathbf{Z}}\delta x+l)^{\mathtt{q}}(\delta x+l)^{\frac{1-\gamma}{2(3-\gamma)}}\mathcal{P}w_{\beta_{\sharp},0}h_{\sigma}\|^{2}_{A}

(3.37)

\displaystyle+C\alpha_{*}l^{2\mathtt{q}}\||v_{3}|^{\frac{1}{2}}(l^{-\mathbf{Z}}\delta A+l)^{\mathtt{q}}w_{\beta_{\sharp},0}\varphi_{A,\sigma}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\Sigma_{-}^{A}}}+C\|(l^{-\mathbf{Z}}\delta x+l)^{\mathtt{q}}\nu^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{P}^{\perp}w_{\beta_{\sharp},0}h_{\sigma}\|^{2}_{A}\,.

Together with (
3.30
), the bounds (
3.37
) and (
3.28
) completes the proof of Lemma
3.4
.
∎

Now, based on the spatial-velocity indices iteration form (
3.31
) in Lemma
3.4
, we will dominate the uncontrolled quantity
\alpha_{*}(\delta\hbar)^{-1}\delta^{-1}l^{-(2a-\frac{1-\gamma}{3-\gamma})}\|(l^{-\mathbf{Z}}\delta x+l)^{a}(\delta x+l)^{-\frac{1-\gamma}{2(3-\gamma)}}\nu^{\frac{1}{2}}g_{\sigma}\|_{A}^{2}
in the right-hand side of (
3.29
). Namely, we will close the estimates in Lemma
4.1
.

Lemma 3.5

(Uniform and closed weighted
L^{2}_{x,v}
estimates)
.

Let
-3<\gamma\leq 1
,
0\leq\alpha_{*}\leq 1
,
0<\delta<1
,
A\geq 1
,
\beta\geq 3(\beta_{\gamma}+\frac{1}{2})
,
0<\vartheta\leq\vartheta_{0}
,
0<\hbar\leq o(1)\delta
and
l\geq O(1)(\delta\hbar)^{-\frac{512(3-\gamma)}{3(3+\gamma)}}
, where the sufficiently small
0<\vartheta_{0},\hbar_{0},o(1)\ll 1
and large enough
O(1)\gg 1
are all independent of
\delta,\hbar,l,A
. Then the problem (
3.1
) admits the following uniform weighted
L^{2}_{x,v}
bound

(3.38)

\displaystyle\mathscr{E}_{2}^{A}(g_{\sigma})+\mathscr{E}_{\mathtt{NBE}}^{A}(g_{\sigma})\leq C_{l}\big[\mathscr{A}_{2}^{A}(h_{\sigma})+\mathscr{A}_{\mathtt{NBE}}^{A}(h_{\sigma})\big]+C_{l}\big[\mathscr{B}_{2}(\varphi_{A,\sigma})+\mathscr{B}_{\mathtt{NBE}}(\varphi_{A,\sigma})\big]

for some constant
C_{l}>0
depending on
l
but being independent of
\delta,\hbar,A
, where the functionals
\mathscr{E}_{2}^{A}(g_{\sigma}),\mathscr{E}_{\mathtt{NBE}}^{A}(g_{\sigma})
are respectively given in (
1.39
), (
1.41
), the functionals
\mathscr{A}_{2}^{A}(h_{\sigma}),\mathscr{A}_{\mathtt{NBE}}^{A}(h_{\sigma})
are defined in (
1.43
), and the functionals
\mathscr{B}_{2}(\varphi_{A,\sigma}),\mathscr{B}_{\mathtt{NBE}}(\varphi_{A,\sigma})
are introduced in (
1.44
).

Proof of Lemma
3.5
.

In order to avoid the tedious mathematical symbols, we denote by

(3.39)

\displaystyle\Theta(h_{\sigma},\varphi_{A,\sigma}):=\alpha_{*}\digamma_{\delta,l,a,\mathbf{Z}}^{2}(\nu^{-1}h_{\sigma})+\mathscr{A}^{A}_{2}(h_{\sigma})+\mathscr{B}_{2}(\varphi_{A,\sigma})\,.

The definition of
\psi_{\mathbf{Z};\mathtt{q},\beta_{\sharp}}
in (
3.30
) shows that

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \|(l^{-\mathbf{Z}}\delta x+l)^{a}(\delta x+l)^{-\frac{1-\gamma}{2(3-\gamma)}}\nu^{\frac{1}{2}}g_{\sigma}\|_{A}^{2}\lesssim(\delta\hbar)^{-1}\psi_{\mathbf{Z};a,0}\,.\end{aligned}

Then the bound (
3.29
) in Corollary
3.1
infers that

(3.40)

\displaystyle\mathscr{E}^{A}_{2}(g_{\sigma})\lesssim

\displaystyle\alpha_{*}(\delta\hbar)^{-2}\delta^{-1}l^{-2(a-\frac{1-\gamma}{2(3-\gamma)}-\mathbf{U}_{1})}l^{-2\mathbf{U}_{1}}\psi_{\mathbf{Z};a,0}

\displaystyle+\alpha_{*}(\delta\hbar)^{-1}\delta^{-1}l^{-100}\mathscr{E}^{A}_{2}(g_{\sigma})+\Theta(h_{\sigma},\varphi_{A,\sigma})

for
a>0
,
\mathbf{U}_{1}\in\mathbb{R}
and
\mathbf{Z}\geq 0
to be determined later. By (
3.31
) in Lemma
3.4
and (
3.39
), one has

(3.41)

\displaystyle l^{-2\mathbf{U}_{1}}

\displaystyle\psi_{\mathbf{Z};a,0}\lesssim\delta(\delta\hbar)^{-1}l^{-2(\mathbf{U}_{1}+\frac{1}{3-\gamma}\mathbf{Z})}\psi_{\mathbf{Z};a-\frac{1}{3-\gamma},\beta_{\gamma}+\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle+\delta^{-1}(\delta\hbar)^{-1}l^{-2(\mathbf{U}_{1}-(a-a_{1})-\frac{1-\gamma}{2(3-\gamma)})}\psi_{\mathbf{Z};a_{1},0}+\delta^{-1}(\delta\hbar)^{-1}l^{-100}\mathscr{E}^{A}_{2}(g_{\sigma})+y_{a_{1},\mathbf{Z};a,0}

for
a_{1}>0
to be determined later. Moreover,

(3.42)

\displaystyle l^{-2\mathbf{U}_{2}}\psi_{\mathbf{Z};a-\frac{1}{3-\gamma},\beta_{\gamma}+\frac{1}{2}}\lesssim

\displaystyle\delta(\delta\hbar)^{-1}l^{-2(\mathbf{U}_{2}+\frac{1}{3-\gamma}\mathbf{Z})}\psi_{\mathbf{Z};a-\frac{2}{3-\gamma},2(\beta_{\gamma}+\frac{1}{2})}

\displaystyle+\delta^{-1}(\delta\hbar)^{-1}l^{-2(\mathbf{U}_{2}-(a-a_{2}-\frac{1}{3-\gamma})-\frac{1-\gamma}{2(3-\gamma)})}\psi_{\mathbf{Z};a_{2},0}

\displaystyle+\delta^{-1}(\delta\hbar)^{-1}l^{-100}\mathscr{E}^{A}_{2}(g_{\sigma})+y_{a_{2},\mathbf{Z};a-\frac{1}{3-\gamma},\beta_{\gamma}+\frac{1}{2}}

for
a_{2}>0
and
\mathbf{U}_{2}\in\mathbb{R}
to be determined. Furthermore, Lemma
3.4
gives us

(3.43)

\displaystyle l^{-2\mathbf{U}_{3}}

\displaystyle\psi_{\mathbf{Z};a_{1},0}\lesssim\delta(\delta\hbar)^{-1}l^{-2(\mathbf{U}_{3}+\frac{1}{3-\gamma}\mathbf{Z})}\psi_{\mathbf{Z};a_{1}-\frac{1}{3-\gamma},\beta_{\gamma}+\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle+\delta^{-1}(\delta\hbar)^{-1}l^{-2(\mathbf{U}_{3}-(a_{1}-a_{3})-\frac{1-\gamma}{2(3-\gamma)})}\psi_{\mathbf{Z};a_{3},0}+\delta^{-1}(\delta\hbar)^{-1}l^{-100}\mathscr{E}^{A}_{2}(g_{\sigma})+y_{a_{3},\mathbf{Z};a_{1},0}\,,

and

(3.44)

\displaystyle l^{-2\mathbf{U}_{4}}\psi_{\mathbf{Z};a_{1}-\frac{1}{3-\gamma},\beta_{\gamma}+\frac{1}{2}}\lesssim

\displaystyle\delta(\delta\hbar)^{-1}l^{-2(\mathbf{U}_{4}+\frac{1}{3-\gamma}\mathbf{Z})}\psi_{\mathbf{Z};a_{1}-\frac{2}{3-\gamma},2(\beta_{\gamma}+\frac{1}{2})}

\displaystyle+\delta^{-1}(\delta\hbar)^{-1}l^{-2(\mathbf{U}_{4}-(a_{1}-a_{4}-\frac{1}{3-\gamma})-\frac{1-\gamma}{2(3-\gamma)})}\psi_{\mathbf{Z};a_{4},0}

\displaystyle+\delta^{-1}(\delta\hbar)^{-1}l^{-100}\mathscr{E}^{A}_{2}(g_{\sigma})+y_{a_{4},\mathbf{Z};a_{1}-\frac{1}{3-\gamma},\beta_{\gamma}+\frac{1}{2}}\,,

and

(3.45)

\displaystyle l^{-2\mathbf{U}_{5}}\psi_{\mathbf{Z};a-\frac{2}{3-\gamma},2(\beta_{\gamma}+\frac{1}{2})}\lesssim\delta(\delta\hbar)^{-1}l^{-2(\mathbf{U}_{5}+\frac{1}{3-\gamma}\mathbf{Z})}\psi_{\mathbf{Z};a-\frac{3}{3-\gamma},3(\beta_{\gamma}+\frac{1}{2})}

\displaystyle+\delta^{-1}(\delta\hbar)^{-1}l^{-2(\mathbf{U}_{5}-(a-a_{5})-\frac{1-\gamma}{2(3-\gamma)}+\frac{2}{3-\gamma})}\psi_{\mathbf{Z};a_{5},0}

\displaystyle+\delta^{-1}(\delta\hbar)^{-1}l^{-100}\mathscr{E}^{A}_{2}(g_{\sigma})+y_{a_{5},\mathbf{Z};a-\frac{2}{3-\gamma},2(\beta_{\gamma}+\frac{1}{2})}\,,

where the parameters
a_{3},a_{4},a_{5}>0
and
\mathbf{U}_{3},\mathbf{U}_{4},\mathbf{U}_{5}\in\mathbb{R}
are all to be determined later.

Now we determinate the all above parameters
a,a_{1},a_{2},a_{3},a_{4},a_{5}>0
,
\mathbf{U}_{1},\mathbf{U}_{2},\mathbf{U}_{3},\mathbf{U}_{4},\mathbf{U}_{5}\in\mathbb{R}
and
\mathbf{Z}\geq 0
so that the estimate are closed.

(i)

Take

(v)

Denote by

. Take

\displaystyle a-\tfrac{1}{3-\gamma}<a_{2}<a\,,\ \mathcal{J}_{5}:=\tfrac{1}{(3-\gamma)(a-a_{2})}W_{\gamma}(a,a_{2},\mathbf{U}_{1},\mathbf{U}_{2})-\mathbf{U}_{2}>0

such that the quantity

in the left-hand side of (

l^{-(\mathbf{U}_{2}-(a-a_{2}-\frac{1}{3-\gamma})-\frac{1-\gamma}{2(3-\gamma)})}(l^{-\mathbf{Z}}\delta x+l)^{a_{2}}
. A direct calculation shows

\displaystyle l^{-(\mathbf{U}_{2}-(a-a_{2}-\frac{1}{3-\gamma})-\frac{1-\gamma}{2(3-\gamma)})}(l^{-\mathbf{Z}}\delta x+l)^{a_{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\big[l^{-\mathbf{U}_{1}}(l^{-\mathbf{Z}}\delta x+l)^{a}\big]^{1-(3-\gamma)(a-a_{2})}\cdot l^{-W_{\gamma}(a,a_{2},\mathbf{U}_{1},\mathbf{U}_{2})}[(l^{-\mathbf{Z}}\delta x+l)^{a-\frac{1}{3-\gamma}}]^{(3-\gamma)(a-a_{2})}

\displaystyle\epsilon_{*}l^{-\mathbf{U}_{1}}(l^{-\mathbf{Z}}\delta x+l)^{a}+C_{\epsilon_{*}}l^{-\frac{1}{(3-\gamma)(a-a_{2})}W_{\gamma}(a,a_{2},\mathbf{U}_{1},\mathbf{U}_{2})}(l^{-\mathbf{Z}}\delta x+l)^{a-\frac{1}{3-\gamma}}

\epsilon_{*}>0
