\displaystyle 2\epsilon_{*}^{2}l^{-2\mathbf{U}_{1}}\psi_{\mathbf{Z};a,0}+2C_{\epsilon_{*}}^{2}[\delta^{-1}(\delta\hbar)^{-1}]^{\frac{2}{(3-\gamma)(a-a_{2})}}l^{-\frac{2}{(3-\gamma)(a-a_{2})}W_{\gamma}(a,a_{2},\mathbf{U}_{1},\mathbf{U}_{2})}\psi_{\mathbf{Z};a-\frac{1}{3-\gamma},\beta_{\gamma}+\frac{1}{2}}\,.

\epsilon_{*}>0
sufficiently small, the first term

\displaystyle-[\mathbf{U}_{3}-(a_{1}-a_{3})-\tfrac{1-\gamma}{2(3-\gamma)}]\,.

\displaystyle l^{-2(\mathbf{U}_{3}-(a_{1}-a_{3})-\frac{1-\gamma}{2(3-\gamma)})}\psi_{\mathbf{Z};a_{3},0}\leq\epsilon_{*}^{2}l^{-2\mathbf{U}_{4}}\psi_{\mathbf{Z};a_{1}-\frac{1}{3-\gamma},\beta_{\gamma}+\frac{1}{2}}

The next goal is to take the proper parameters

For

\displaystyle a=\tfrac{15}{8(3-\gamma)}\,,\ a_{1}=\tfrac{7}{4(3-\gamma)}\,,\ a_{2}=\tfrac{13}{8(3-\gamma)}\,,\ a_{3}=\tfrac{1}{2(3-\gamma)}\,,\ a_{4}=\tfrac{3}{2(3-\gamma)}\,,\ a_{5}=\tfrac{1}{3(3-\gamma)}\,,

\displaystyle\mathbf{U}_{1}=-\tfrac{59-24\gamma}{48(3-\gamma)}\,,\ \mathbf{U}_{2}=-\tfrac{9+\gamma}{6(3-\gamma)}\,,\ \mathbf{U}_{3}=-\tfrac{107-24\gamma}{24(3-\gamma)}\,,\ \mathbf{U}_{4}=-\tfrac{297-40\gamma}{72(3-\gamma)}\,,

\displaystyle\mathbf{U}_{5}=-\tfrac{189-52\gamma}{72(3-\gamma)}\,,\ \mathbf{Z}=6\,.

The choice of

and

(

) in (

)-(

(3.62)

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}_{1}=\tfrac{125}{48(3-\gamma)}>0\,,\ \mathcal{J}_{2}=\tfrac{301+32\gamma}{48(3-\gamma)}>0\,,\ \mathcal{J}_{3}=\tfrac{125}{48(3-\gamma)}>0\,,\ \mathcal{J}_{4}=\tfrac{27-\gamma}{6(3-\gamma)}>0\,,

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}_{5}=\tfrac{3}{16(3-\gamma)}>0\,,\ \mathcal{J}_{6}=\tfrac{51+4\gamma}{9(3-\gamma)}>0\,,\ \mathcal{J}_{7}=\tfrac{135+40\gamma}{72(3-\gamma)}>0\,,\ \mathcal{J}_{8}=\tfrac{2(3+\gamma)}{3(3-\gamma)}>0\,,

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}_{9}=-\tfrac{2(3+\gamma)}{9(3-\gamma)}<0\,,\ \mathcal{J}_{10}=\tfrac{2(3+\gamma)}{9(3-\gamma)}>0\,,\ \mathcal{J}_{11}=\tfrac{243+52\gamma}{72(3-\gamma)}>0\,.

It is easy to see that for any
-3<\gamma\leq 1
,

(3.63)

\displaystyle\mathcal{J}_{i}\geq\tfrac{3}{16(3-\gamma)}\geq\tfrac{3(3+\gamma)}{64(3-\gamma)}>0\ (i\neq 8,9,10)\,,\ \mathcal{J}_{8},-\mathcal{J}_{9},\mathcal{J}_{10}\geq\tfrac{3(3+\gamma)}{64(3-\gamma)}>0\,.

With the above choices (
3.61
), the quantities
\mathcal{J}_{i}
for

in (
3.62
) and the positive lower bound of
\mathcal{J}_{i}
in (

), the inequalities (
3.40
)-(
3.41
)-(
3.42
)-(
3.43
)-(
3.44
)-(
3.45
) infer that

(3.64)

\displaystyle\mathscr{E}^{A}_{2}(g_{\sigma})\lesssim

\displaystyle\alpha_{*}(\delta\hbar)^{-2}\delta^{-1}l^{-\frac{3(3+\gamma)}{32(3-\gamma)}}l^{\frac{59-24\gamma}{24(3-\gamma)}}\psi_{6;\frac{15}{8(3-\gamma)},0}

\displaystyle+\alpha_{*}(\delta\hbar)^{-1}\delta^{-1}l^{-100}\mathscr{E}^{A}_{2}(g_{\sigma})+\Theta(h_{\sigma},\varphi_{A,\sigma})\,,

and

(3.65)

\displaystyle l^{\frac{59-24\gamma}{24(3-\gamma)}}\psi_{6;\frac{15}{8(3-\gamma)},0}\lesssim

\displaystyle\delta(\delta\hbar)^{-1}l^{-\frac{3(3+\gamma)}{32(3-\gamma)}}l^{\frac{9+\gamma}{3(3-\gamma)}}\psi_{6;\frac{7}{8(3-\gamma)},\beta_{\gamma}+\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle+\delta^{-1}(\delta\hbar)^{-1}l^{-\frac{3(3+\gamma)}{32(3-\gamma)}}l^{\frac{107-24\gamma}{12(3-\gamma)}}\psi_{6;\frac{7}{4(3-\gamma)},0}

\displaystyle+\delta^{-1}(\delta\hbar)^{-1}l^{-100}\mathscr{E}^{A}_{2}(g_{\sigma})+y_{\frac{7}{4(3-\gamma)},6;\frac{15}{8(3-\gamma)},0}\,,

and

(3.66)

\displaystyle l^{\frac{9+\gamma}{3(3-\gamma)}}\psi_{6;\frac{7}{8(3-\gamma)},\beta_{\gamma}+\frac{1}{2}}\lesssim

\displaystyle\delta(\delta\hbar)^{-1}l^{-\frac{3(3+\gamma)}{32(3-\gamma)}}\psi_{6;-\frac{1}{8(3-\gamma)},2(\beta_{\gamma}+\frac{1}{2})}+2\epsilon_{*}^{2}l^{\frac{59-24\gamma}{24(3-\gamma)}}\psi_{6;\frac{15}{8(3-\gamma)},0}

\displaystyle+2C_{\epsilon_{*}}^{2}\delta^{-8}(\delta\hbar)^{-8}l^{-\frac{3(3+\gamma)}{32(3-\gamma)}}l^{\frac{9+\gamma}{3(3-\gamma)}}\psi_{6;\frac{7}{8(3-\gamma)},\beta_{\gamma}+\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle+\delta^{-1}(\delta\hbar)^{-1}l^{-100}\mathscr{E}^{A}_{2}(g_{\sigma})+y_{\frac{13}{8(3-\gamma)},6;\frac{7}{8(3-\gamma)},\beta_{\gamma}+\frac{1}{2}}

for any small
\epsilon_{*}>0
to be determined later, where the inequality (
3.51
) is used, and

(3.67)

\displaystyle l^{\frac{107-24\gamma}{12(3-\gamma)}}\psi_{6;\frac{7}{4(3-\gamma)},0}\lesssim

\displaystyle\delta(\delta\hbar)^{-1}l^{-\frac{3(3+\gamma)}{32(3-\gamma)}}l^{\frac{297-40\gamma}{36(3-\gamma)}}\psi_{6;\frac{3}{4(3-\gamma)},\beta_{\gamma}+\frac{1}{2}}+\epsilon_{*}^{2}l^{\frac{297-40\gamma}{36(3-\gamma)}}\psi_{6;\frac{3}{4(3-\gamma)},\beta_{\gamma}+\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle+C_{\epsilon_{*}}^{2}\delta^{-2}(\delta\hbar)^{-2}l^{-\frac{3(3+\gamma)}{32(3-\gamma)}}l^{\frac{189-52\gamma}{36(3-\gamma)}}\psi_{6;-\frac{1}{8(3-\gamma)},2(\beta_{\gamma}+\frac{1}{2})}

\displaystyle+\delta^{-1}(\delta\hbar)^{-1}l^{-100}\mathscr{E}^{A}_{2}(g_{\sigma})+y_{\frac{1}{2(3-\gamma)},6;\frac{7}{4(3-\gamma)},0}

for any small
\epsilon_{*}>0
to be determined later, where the inequality (
3.57
) is utilized, and

(3.68)

\displaystyle l^{\frac{297-40\gamma}{36(3-\gamma)}}\psi_{6;\frac{3}{4(3-\gamma)},\beta_{\gamma}+\frac{1}{2}}\lesssim

\displaystyle\delta(\delta\hbar)^{-1}l^{-\frac{3(3+\gamma)}{32(3-\gamma)}}\psi_{6;-\frac{1}{4(3-\gamma)},2(\beta_{\gamma}+\frac{1}{2})}+2\epsilon_{*}^{2}l^{\frac{107-24\gamma}{12(3-\gamma)}}\psi_{6;\frac{7}{4(3-\gamma)},0}

\displaystyle+2C_{\epsilon_{*}}^{2}\delta^{-8}(\delta\hbar)^{-8}l^{-\frac{3(3+\gamma)}{32(3-\gamma)}}l^{\frac{297-40\gamma}{36(3-\gamma)}}\psi_{6;\frac{3}{4(3-\gamma)},\beta_{\gamma}+\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle+\delta^{-1}(\delta\hbar)^{-1}l^{-100}\mathscr{E}^{A}_{2}(g_{\sigma})+y_{\frac{3}{2(3-\gamma)},6;\frac{3}{4(3-\gamma)},\beta_{\gamma}+\frac{1}{2}}

for any small
\epsilon_{*}>0
to be determined later, where the inequality (
3.55
) is employed, and

(3.69)

\displaystyle l^{\frac{189-52\gamma}{36(3-\gamma)}}\psi_{6;-\frac{1}{8(3-\gamma)},2(\beta_{\gamma}+\frac{1}{2})}\lesssim

\displaystyle\delta(\delta\hbar)^{-1}l^{-\frac{3(3+\gamma)}{32(3-\gamma)}}\psi_{6;-\frac{7}{8(3-\gamma)},3(\beta_{\gamma}+\frac{1}{2})}

\displaystyle+\delta^{-1}(\delta\hbar)^{-1}l^{-\frac{3(3+\gamma)}{32(3-\gamma)}}l^{\frac{297-40\gamma}{36(3-\gamma)}}\psi_{6;\frac{3}{4(3-\gamma)},\beta_{\gamma}+\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle+\delta^{-1}(\delta\hbar)^{-1}l^{-100}\mathscr{E}^{A}_{2}(g_{\sigma})+y_{\frac{1}{3(3-\gamma)},6;-\frac{1}{8(3-\gamma)},2(\beta_{\gamma}+\frac{1}{2})}\,,

where the inequality (
3.59
) is used.

Notice that if
\beta\geq 3(\beta_{\gamma}+\frac{1}{2})
involved in the functional
\mathscr{E}_{2}^{A}(\cdot)
in (
1.41
), one has

(3.70)

\displaystyle\big(\mathrm{RHS\ of\ \eqref{U2-d}}\big)_{1st}+\big(\mathrm{RHS\ of\ \eqref{U4-d}}\big)_{1st}+\big(\mathrm{RHS\ of\ \eqref{U5-d}}\big)_{1st}\lesssim\delta(\delta\hbar)^{-1}l^{-\frac{3(3+\gamma)}{32(3-\gamma)}}\mathscr{E}_{2}^{A}(g_{\sigma})\,,

where the symbol
\big(\mathrm{RHS\ of\ (X)}\big)_{1st}
means the first term in the right-hand side of the inequality (X). Recalling the definition of the functional
\mathscr{E}_{\mathtt{NBE}}^{A}(\cdot)
in (
1.39
) and summing up for the inequalities (
3.64
)-(
3.65
)-(
3.66
)-(
3.67
)-(
3.68
)-(
3.69
), one gains

(3.71)

\displaystyle\mathscr{E}_{2}^{A}(g_{\sigma})+\mathscr{E}_{\mathtt{NBE}}^{A}(g_{\sigma})\lesssim

\displaystyle\big[\epsilon_{*}^{2}+(1+C_{\epsilon_{*}}^{2})(\delta\hbar)^{-16}l^{-\frac{3(3+\gamma)}{32(3-\gamma)}}\big]\big[\mathscr{E}_{2}^{A}(g_{\sigma})+\mathscr{E}_{\mathtt{NBE}}^{A}(g_{\sigma})\big]

\displaystyle+y_{\frac{1}{2(3-\gamma)},6;\frac{7}{4(3-\gamma)},0}+y_{\frac{7}{4(3-\gamma)},6;\frac{15}{8(3-\gamma)},0}+y_{\frac{13}{8(3-\gamma)},6;\frac{7}{8(3-\gamma)},\beta_{\gamma}+\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle+\Theta(h_{\sigma},\varphi_{A,\sigma})+y_{\frac{3}{2(3-\gamma)},6;\frac{3}{4(3-\gamma)},\beta_{\gamma}+\frac{1}{2}}+y_{\frac{1}{3(3-\gamma)},6;-\frac{1}{8(3-\gamma)},2(\beta_{\gamma}+\frac{1}{2})}

for any
\epsilon_{*}>0
, where the facts
0<\delta,\hbar<1
and
l^{-100}\leq l^{-\frac{3(3+\gamma)}{32(3-\gamma)}}
for all
-3<\gamma\leq 1
and
l\geq 1
have been utilized. Initially taking
\epsilon_{*}>0
and then choosing
l\geq 1
large enough such that

(3.72)

\displaystyle l\geq O(1)(\delta\hbar)^{-\frac{512(3-\gamma)}{3(3+\gamma)}}\,,

where
O(1)\gg 1
is sufficiently large and independent of
\delta,\hbar
. Remark that
l\geq 1
in Lemma
2.6
is assumed by
l>O(1)(\ln\frac{1}{\delta})^{\frac{3-\gamma}{2}}
for
O(1)\gg 1
is sufficiently large and independent of
\delta,\hbar
, which can be naturally implied by (
3.72
). Recalling the definition of
\digamma_{\delta,l,a,\mathbf{Z}}(\nu^{-1}h_{\sigma})
in (
3.3
) and
y_{a,\mathbf{Z};\mathtt{q},\beta_{\sharp}}
in (
3.30
), one easily obtains

(3.73)

\displaystyle y_{\frac{1}{2(3-\gamma)},6;\frac{7}{4(3-\gamma)},0}+y_{\frac{7}{4(3-\gamma)},6;\frac{15}{8(3-\gamma)},0}+y_{\frac{13}{8(3-\gamma)},6;\frac{7}{8(3-\gamma)},\beta_{\gamma}+\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle+\Theta(h_{\sigma},\varphi_{A,\sigma})+y_{\frac{3}{2(3-\gamma)},6;\frac{3}{4(3-\gamma)},\beta_{\gamma}+\frac{1}{2}}+y_{\frac{1}{3(3-\gamma)},6;-\frac{1}{8(3-\gamma)},2(\beta_{\gamma}+\frac{1}{2})}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{l}\mathscr{A}_{\mathtt{NBE}}^{A}(h_{\sigma})+C_{l}\mathscr{B}_{\mathtt{NBE}}(\varphi_{A,\sigma})+\mathscr{A}^{A}_{2}(h_{\sigma})+\mathscr{B}_{2}(\varphi_{A,\sigma})\,,

where the functionals
\mathscr{A}_{\mathtt{NBE}}^{A}(h_{\sigma})
and
\mathscr{B}_{\mathtt{NBE}}(\varphi_{A,\sigma})
are defined in (
1.43
) and (
1.44
), respectively.

As a result, under (
3.72
), the inequalities (
3.71
) and (
3.73
) complete the bound (
3.38
), and the proof of Lemma
3.5
is then finished.
∎

3.4.
Existence and uniqueness of weak solution to the problem (
CA-eq
) with
\varphi_{A}=0

In this subsection, based on the uniform a priori estimate in Lemma
3.5
, we will prove the existence and uniqueness of the weak solution to the problem (
CA-eq
) (equivalently, the equation (
3.1
)) with the boundary source term
\varphi_{A}=0
(equivalently,
\varphi_{A,\sigma}=0
) by employing the well-known Hahn-Banach Theorem and Lax-Milgram Theorem. The existence result is stated as follows.

Lemma 3.6
.

Under the same assumptions on Lemma
3.5
, we assume that the source term
h_{\sigma}
in (
3.1
) satisfies

\displaystyle\mathscr{A}_{2}^{A}(h_{\sigma})+\mathscr{A}_{\mathtt{NBE}}^{A}(h_{\sigma})<\infty

and the boundary source term
\varphi_{A,\sigma}=0
. Then the problem (
3.1
) (equivalently (
CA-eq
)) admits a unique weak solution
g_{\sigma}(x,v)
enjoying the bound (
3.38
) in Lemma
3.5
.

Proof of Lemma
3.6
.

We first introduce the Hilbert space

\displaystyle\mathbb{X}:=\{f=f(x,v)|\mathscr{E}_{2}^{A}(f)+\mathscr{E}^{A}_{\mathtt{NBE}}(f)<\infty\}

with the inner product

\displaystyle\big(f,g\big)_{\mathbb{X}}:=

\displaystyle\delta\hbar\int_{0}^{A}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathcal{P}(w_{\beta,\vartheta}f)\mathcal{P}(w_{\beta,\vartheta}g)(\delta x+l)^{-\frac{1-\gamma}{3-\gamma}}\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{A}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathcal{P}^{\perp}(w_{\beta,\vartheta}f)\mathcal{P}^{\perp}(w_{\beta,\vartheta}g)\nu\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle+\sum_{[\mathbf{a};\mathbf{b},\mathbf{c}]\in\mathbbm{Index}}l^{\mathbf{a}}\delta\hbar\int_{0}^{A}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathcal{P}(w_{\mathbf{c},0}f)\mathcal{P}(w_{\mathbf{c},0}g)(l^{-6}\delta x+l)^{2\mathbf{b}}(\delta x+l)^{-\frac{1-\gamma}{3-\gamma}}\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle+\sum_{[\mathbf{a};\mathbf{b},\mathbf{c}]\in\mathbbm{Index}}l^{\mathbf{a}}\delta\hbar\int_{0}^{A}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathcal{P}^{\perp}(w_{\mathbf{c},0}f)\mathcal{P}^{\perp}(w_{\mathbf{c},0}g)\nu\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}x\,,

where the index set

\displaystyle\mathbbm{Index}=\Big\{[\tfrac{59-24\gamma}{24(3-\gamma)};\tfrac{15}{8(3-\gamma)},0]\,,[\tfrac{9+\gamma}{3(3-\gamma)};\tfrac{7}{8(3-\gamma)},\beta_{\gamma}+\tfrac{1}{2}]\,,[\tfrac{107-24\gamma}{12(3-\gamma)};\tfrac{7}{4(3-\gamma)},0]\,,

\displaystyle[\tfrac{297-40\gamma}{36(3-\gamma)};\tfrac{3}{4(3-\gamma)},\beta_{\gamma}+\tfrac{1}{2}]\,,[\tfrac{189-52\gamma}{36(3-\gamma)};-\tfrac{1}{8(3-\gamma)},2\beta_{\gamma}+1]\Big\}\,.

We further define the space

\displaystyle\mathbb{Y}:=\big\{h=h(x,v)|\mathscr{A}_{2}^{A}(h)+\mathscr{A}_{\mathtt{NBE}}^{A}(h)<\infty\big\}\,.

It is easy to see that
\mathbb{Y}
is a subspace of
\mathbb{X}
.

For any
\phi\in C_{0}^{\infty}((0,A)\times\mathbb{R}^{3})
with
\phi(0,v)|_{v_{3}<0}=0
, define

\displaystyle\chi=-v_{3}\partial_{x}\phi+[-\hbar\sigma_{x}v_{3}+\nu(v)]\phi-K_{\hbar}^{*}\phi+\mathbf{D}_{\hbar}^{*}\phi\,,

where
K_{\hbar}^{*}
and
\mathbf{D}_{\hbar}^{*}
are the duality of
K_{\hbar}
and
\mathbf{D}_{\hbar}
, respectively. Define the linear functional
\mathbb{L}_{\chi}:\mathbb{Y}\to\mathbb{R}
with
h\mapsto\mathbb{L}_{\chi}(h)=(h,\chi)_{\mathbb{X}}
. By the similar arguments in (
3.27
) or (
3.35
), one knows that
|\mathbb{L}_{\chi}(h)|\leq C\|h\|_{\mathbb{Y}}\|\chi\|_{\mathbb{X}}
. Note that
\mathbb{Y}\subseteq\mathbb{X}
. The Hahn-Banach Theorem tells us that the linear functional
\mathbb{L}_{\chi}
can be extended to the space
\mathbb{X}
, denoted by
\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}_{\chi}
, satisfying
\widetilde{\mathbb{L}}_{\chi}|_{\mathbb{Y}}=\mathbb{L}_{\chi}
. Moreover, Lemma
3.5
shows that
(\phi,\chi)_{\mathbb{X}}\geq c_{0}\|\phi\|^{2}_{\mathbb{X}}
for any
\phi\in C_{0}^{\infty}((0,A)\times\mathbb{R}^{3})
with
\phi(0,v)|_{v_{3}<0}=0
. Then the existence result follows from the Lax-Milgram Theorem.

Next we verify the uniqueness. We focus on the difference problem (
3.20
). Following the same arguments in Lemma
3.5
, one gains

\displaystyle\mathscr{E}_{2}^{A}({\vartriangle}g_{\sigma})+\mathscr{E}_{\mathtt{NBE}}^{A}({\vartriangle}g_{\sigma})\leq C_{l}\big[\mathscr{A}_{2}^{A}({\vartriangle}h_{\sigma})+\mathscr{A}_{\mathtt{NBE}}^{A}({\vartriangle}h_{\sigma})\big]\,,

where
{\vartriangle}g_{\sigma}=g_{\sigma 2}-g_{\sigma 1}
and
{\vartriangle}h_{\sigma}=h_{\sigma 2}-h_{\sigma 1}
. Then the uniqueness holds immediately.
∎

4.
Weighted
L^{\infty}_{x,v}
solution to connection auxiliary equation (
CA-eq
)

In this section, based on the weak solution to (
CA-eq
) constructed in Lemma
3.6
, we mainly derive the weighted
L^{\infty}_{x,v}
bounds uniformly in
A\geq 1
for the wean solution to the connection auxiliary problem (
CA-eq
) constructed in Lemma
3.6
. The main idea is to employ the trajectory approach. It is one of the cores in current work. We remark that merely the weighted
L^{\infty}_{x,v}
can not be closed, so that we shall close the estimates together with the weighted
L^{2}_{x,v}
bound derived in Lemma (
3.5
).

4.1.
Statements of the uniform results

We mainly focus on the equivalent form (
3.1
) of the equation (
CA-eq
), i.e.,

(4.1)

\left\{\begin{aligned} &\mathscr{L}_{\hbar}g_{\sigma}:=v_{3}\partial_{x}g_{\sigma}+[-\hbar\sigma_{x}v_{3}+\nu(v)]g_{\sigma}-K_{\hbar}g_{\sigma}+\mathbf{D}_{\hbar}g=h_{\sigma}\,,\\
&g_{\sigma}(0,v)|_{v_{3}>0}=(1-\alpha_{*})g_{\sigma}(0,R_{0}v)+\alpha_{*}\tfrac{M_{w}(v)}{\sqrt{\mathfrak{M}_{\sigma}(v)}}\int_{v_{3}^{\prime}<0}(-v_{3}^{\prime})g_{\sigma}(0,v^{\prime})\sqrt{\mathfrak{M}_{\sigma}(v^{\prime})}\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\,,\\
&g_{\sigma}(A,v)|_{v_{3}<0}=\varphi_{A,\sigma}(v)\,,\end{aligned}\right.

For notation convenience, let
g=\mathscr{L}_{\hbar}^{-1}(h)
be the solution to the problem (
4.1
). In Lemma
3.6
, we consider merely the case
\varphi_{A,\sigma}=0
. Here we study the general case
\varphi_{A,\sigma}
, which will be used to prove the uniqueness of the problem (
1.66
). First, we given the following a priori estimates for the operator
\mathscr{L}_{\hbar}^{-1}(h)
uniformly in
A\geq 1
. Note that

\displaystyle\partial_{x}(\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}g_{\sigma})+\partial_{x}{\kappa}(x,v)(\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}g_{\sigma})=\tfrac{1}{v_{3}}\underbrace{(\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}K_{\hbar}g_{\sigma}-\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}\mathbf{D}_{\hbar}g_{\sigma}+\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}h_{\sigma})}_{:=H(x,v)}\,,

which means

(4.2)

\displaystyle\partial_{x}\big[e^{{\kappa}(x,v)}\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}g_{\sigma}(x,v)\big]=e^{{\kappa}(x,v)}\tfrac{1}{v_{3}}H(x,v)\,.

Here
\kappa(x,v)
is introduced in (
2.1
).

If
v_{3}<0
, by integrating (
4.2
) from
x
to
A
, it follows that

(4.3)

\displaystyle\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}g_{\sigma}(x,v)=e^{{\kappa}(A,v)-{\kappa}(x,v)}\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}(A,v)\varphi_{A,\sigma}(v)-\int_{x}^{A}e^{-[{\kappa}(x,v)-{\kappa}(x^{\prime},v)]}\tfrac{1}{v_{3}}H(x^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}\,.

If
v_{3}>0
, integrating (
4.2
) from 0 to
x
and together with the specular reflection boundary condition in (
3.1
), one has

(4.4)

\displaystyle\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}g_{\sigma}(x,v)=e^{-{\kappa}(x,v)}\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}(0,v)g_{\sigma}(0,v)+\int_{0}^{x}e^{-[{\kappa}(x,v)-{\kappa}(x^{\prime},v)]}\tfrac{1}{v_{3}}H(x^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}\,.

By the boundary conditions in (
3.1
), one has

\displaystyle e^{-{\kappa}(x,v)}\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}(0,v)g_{\sigma}(0,v)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(1-\alpha_{*})e^{-{\kappa}(x,v)}\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}(0,R_{0}v)g_{\sigma}(0,R_{0}v)

(4.5)

\displaystyle+\alpha_{*}e^{-{\kappa}(x,v)}\tfrac{M_{w}(v)}{\sqrt{\mathfrak{M}_{\sigma}(v)}}\int_{v_{3}^{\prime}<0}(-v_{3}^{\prime})\tfrac{\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}(0,v)}{\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}(0,v^{\prime})}\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}(0,v^{\prime})g_{\sigma}(0,v^{\prime})\sqrt{\mathfrak{M}_{\sigma}(v^{\prime})}\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\,.

Here we have utilized the fact
\sigma_{x}(0,R_{0}v)=\sigma_{x}(0,v)
by (
1.30
). Note that (
4.2
) indicates

(4.6)

\displaystyle\partial_{x}\big[e^{{\kappa}(x,R_{0}v)}\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}g_{\sigma}(x,R_{0}v)\big]=e^{{\kappa}(x,R_{0}v)}\tfrac{1}{(R_{0}v)_{3}}H(x,R_{0}v)\,.

Due to
(R_{0}v)_{3}=-v_{3}<0
, together with the boundary condition in (
3.1
), integrating (
4.6
) from 0 to
A
implies

\displaystyle e^{{\kappa}(A,R_{0}v)}\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}(A,R_{0}v)\varphi_{A,\sigma}(R_{0}v)-\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}(0,R_{0}v)g_{\sigma}(0,R_{0}v)=\int_{0}^{A}e^{{\kappa}(x^{\prime},R_{0}v)}\tfrac{1}{-v_{3}}H(x^{\prime},R_{0}v)\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}\,,

which means that

(4.7)

\displaystyle e^{-{\kappa}(x,v)}\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}(0,R_{0}v)g_{\sigma}(0,R_{0}v)=

\displaystyle e^{{\kappa}(A,R_{0}v)-{\kappa}(x,v)}\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}(A,R_{0}v)\varphi_{A,\sigma}(R_{0}v)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{A}e^{-[{\kappa}(x,v)-{\kappa}(x^{\prime},R_{0}v)]}\tfrac{1}{v_{3}}H(x^{\prime},R_{0}v)\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}\,.

Furthermore, (
4.3
) shows that for
v_{3}^{\prime}<0
,

\displaystyle\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}(0,v^{\prime})g_{\sigma}(0,v^{\prime})=e^{{\kappa}(A,v^{\prime})}\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}(A,v^{\prime})\varphi_{A,\sigma}(v^{\prime})-\int_{0}^{A}e^{{\kappa}(x^{\prime},v^{\prime})}\tfrac{1}{v_{3}^{\prime}}H(x^{\prime},v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}\,.

It thereby holds

(4.8)

\displaystyle e^{-{\kappa}(x,v)}\tfrac{M_{w}(v)}{\sqrt{\mathfrak{M}_{\sigma}(v)}}\int_{v_{3}^{\prime}<0}(-v_{3}^{\prime})\tfrac{\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}(0,v)}{\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}(0,v^{\prime})}\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}(0,v^{\prime})g_{\sigma}(0,v^{\prime})\sqrt{\mathfrak{M}_{\sigma}(v^{\prime})}\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tfrac{M_{w}(v)}{\sqrt{\mathfrak{M}_{\sigma}(v)}}\int_{v_{3}^{\prime}<0}(-v_{3}^{\prime})\tfrac{\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}(0,v)}{\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}(0,v^{\prime})}e^{{\kappa}(A,v^{\prime})-{\kappa}(x,v)}\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}(A,v^{\prime})\varphi_{A,\sigma}(v^{\prime})\sqrt{\mathfrak{M}_{\sigma}(v^{\prime})}\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}

\displaystyle-\tfrac{M_{w}(v)}{\sqrt{\mathfrak{M}_{\sigma}(v)}}\int_{v_{3}^{\prime}<0}\int_{0}^{A}(-v_{3}^{\prime})\tfrac{\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}(0,v)}{\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}(0,v^{\prime})}e^{-[{\kappa}(x,v)-{\kappa}(x^{\prime},v^{\prime})]}\tfrac{1}{v_{3}^{\prime}}H(x^{\prime},v^{\prime})\sqrt{\mathfrak{M}_{\sigma}(v^{\prime})}\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\,.

It is thus derived from collecting the equations (
4.4
), (
4.5
), (
4.7
) and (
4.8
) that for
v_{3}>0
,

\displaystyle\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}g_{\sigma}(x,v)=

\displaystyle(1-\alpha_{*})e^{{\kappa}(A,R_{0}v)-{\kappa}(x,v)}\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}(A,R_{0}v)\varphi_{A,\sigma}(R_{0}v)

\displaystyle+(1-\alpha_{*})\int_{0}^{A}e^{-[{\kappa}(x,v)-{\kappa}(x^{\prime},R_{0}v)]}\tfrac{1}{v_{3}}H(x^{\prime},R_{0}v)\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}

\displaystyle+\alpha_{*}\tfrac{M_{w}(v)}{\sqrt{\mathfrak{M}_{\sigma}(v)}}\int_{v_{3}^{\prime}<0}(-v_{3}^{\prime})\tfrac{\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}(0,v)}{\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}(0,v^{\prime})}e^{{\kappa}(A,v^{\prime})-{\kappa}(x,v)}

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 9.24994pt\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\times\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}(A,v^{\prime})\varphi_{A,\sigma}(v^{\prime})\sqrt{\mathfrak{M}_{\sigma}(v^{\prime})}\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}

\displaystyle-\alpha_{*}\tfrac{M_{w}(v)}{\sqrt{\mathfrak{M}_{\sigma}(v)}}\int_{v_{3}^{\prime}<0}\int_{0}^{A}(-v_{3}^{\prime})\tfrac{\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}(0,v)}{\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}(0,v^{\prime})}e^{-[{\kappa}(x,v)-{\kappa}(x^{\prime},v^{\prime})]}\tfrac{1}{v_{3}^{\prime}}H(x^{\prime},v^{\prime})\sqrt{\mathfrak{M}_{\sigma}(v^{\prime})}\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}

(4.9)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{x}e^{-[{\kappa}(x,v)-{\kappa}(x^{\prime},v)]}\tfrac{1}{v_{3}}H(x^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}\,.

Summarily, the equations (
4.3
) and (
4.9
) indicate that

\displaystyle\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}g_{\sigma}=

\displaystyle Y_{A}(\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}\varphi_{A,\sigma})+Z(\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}K_{\hbar}g_{\sigma}-\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}\mathbf{D}_{\hbar}g_{\sigma}+\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}h_{\sigma})

(4.10)

\displaystyle+U(\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}K_{\hbar}g_{\sigma}-\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}\mathbf{D}_{\hbar}g_{\sigma}+\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}h_{\sigma})\,,

where the operators
Y_{A}(\cdot)
,
Z(\cdot)
and
U(\cdot)
are introduced in (
2.3
), (
2.4
) and (
2.5
), respectively.

Then we can establish the uniform bounds for the problem (
CA-eq
) (or equivalently (
3.1
), also (
4.1
)) in the following lemma.

Lemma 4.1

(Uniform weighted
L^{\infty}_{x,v}
bounds for (
4.1
))
.

Let
-3<\gamma\leq 1
,
0\leq\alpha_{*}\leq 1
,
m\geq 1
, the integer
\beta_{*}\geq 0
and
0<\alpha<\mu_{\gamma}
, where
\mu_{\gamma}>0
is given in Lemma
2.5
. The parameters
\delta,\hbar,l,\vartheta,\beta
are given in Lemma
3.6
. Assume that the source term
h_{\sigma}
and the boundary source term
\varphi_{A,\sigma}
of the system (
3.1
) satisfy

(4.11)

\displaystyle\mathscr{A}^{A}(h_{\sigma})\,,\mathscr{B}(\varphi_{A,\sigma})<\infty\,.

Then the problem (
CA-eq
) admits a mild solution
g(x,v)=e^{-\hbar\sigma(x,v)}g_{\sigma}(x,v)
, where
g_{\sigma}(x,v)
subjects to equation (
3.1
), such that
g_{\sigma}=g_{\sigma}(x,v)
enjoys the following bounds:

(4.12)

\displaystyle\mathscr{E}^{A}\big(g_{\sigma}\big)\leq C_{l}\big(\mathscr{A}^{A}(h_{\sigma})+\mathscr{B}(\varphi_{A,\sigma})\big)

for a constant
C_{l}>0
independent of
A
,
\hbar
and
\delta
.
Moreover, let
g_{\sigma i}=g_{\sigma i}(x,v)
be the solutions with respect to the source terms
h_{\sigma i}

(i=1,2)
, where
\mathscr{A}^{A}(h_{\sigma i})<\infty
for
i=1,2
. Then
g_{\sigma 2}-g_{\sigma 1}
satisfies

(4.13)

\displaystyle\mathscr{E}^{A}\big(g_{\sigma 2}-g_{\sigma 1}\big)\leq C\mathscr{A}^{A}(h_{\sigma 2}-h_{\sigma 1})\,.

Here the functionals
\mathscr{E}^{A}(\cdot)
,
\mathscr{A}^{A}(\cdot)
and
\mathscr{B}(\cdot)
are defined in (
1.36
) and (
1.42
).

Remark 4.1
.

As the similar arguments in Lemma II.2 of
[
5
]
, one knows that the weak solution to (
CA-eq
) constructed in Lemma
3.6
is equivalent to the mild solution to (
CA-eq
) with the form (
4.10
).

The proof of Lemma
4.1
will be completed in Subsection
4.5
later.

4.2.
Weighted
L^{\infty}_{x,v}
estimates in
Y^{\infty}_{m,\beta_{*},\vartheta}(A)
space

In this subsection, we aim at controlling the norm
[\![g_{\sigma}]\!]_{A;m,\beta_{*},\vartheta}
with respect to the space
Y^{\infty}_{m,\beta_{*},\vartheta}(A)
. Moreover, we will also investigate the continuous dependence of
g_{\sigma}
with respect to the source term
h_{\sigma}
. Set
g_{\sigma i}=\mathscr{L}_{\hbar}^{-1}(h_{\sigma i})
(
i=1,2
) for sufficiently small
\hbar\geq 0
. More precisely, the result is stated as follows.

Lemma 4.2
.

Let the parameters
\gamma,\alpha_{*},A
be given in Lemma
3.6
and the parameters
l,\delta,\hbar,\vartheta
be sufficiently small satisfying the assumptions given in Lemmas
2.2
-
2.3
-
2.4
-
2.7
-
2.8
. The integer
\beta_{*}\geq 0
,
m\in\mathbb{R}
,
0\leq\frac{1}{2}-\mu_{\gamma}<\alpha^{\prime}<\frac{1}{2}
, where the constant
\mu_{\gamma}>0
is given in Lemma
2.5
. Then there is a constant
C>0
, independent of
l
,
A
,
\delta
and
\hbar
, such that

(4.14)

\displaystyle\mathscr{E}_{\infty}^{A}(g_{\sigma})+[\![g_{\sigma}]\!]_{m,\beta_{*},\vartheta,\Sigma}\leq C\|z_{\alpha^{\prime}}\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}w_{-\gamma,\vartheta}g_{\sigma}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}+C\big(\mathscr{A}^{A}_{\infty}(h_{\sigma})+\mathscr{B}_{\infty}(\varphi_{A,\sigma})\big)\,,

where the functionals
\mathscr{E}_{\infty}^{A}(\cdot)
,
\mathscr{A}^{A}_{\infty}(\cdot)
and
\mathscr{B}_{\infty}(\cdot)
are defined in (
1.37
), (
1.43
) and (
1.44
), respectively. Moreover,
{\vartriangle}g_{\sigma}(x,v)
satisfying (
3.20
) enjoys the bound

(4.15)

\displaystyle\mathscr{E}_{\infty}^{A}({\vartriangle}g_{\sigma})+[\![{\vartriangle}g_{\sigma}]\!]_{m,\beta_{*},\vartheta,\Sigma}\leq C\|z_{\alpha^{\prime}}\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}w_{-\gamma,\vartheta}{\vartriangle}g_{\sigma}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}+C\mathscr{A}^{A}_{\infty}({\vartriangle}h_{\sigma})\,.

Proof.

By Lemma
2.2
, the equation (
4.10
) shows

\displaystyle[\![g_{\sigma}]\!]_{A;m,\beta_{*},\vartheta}+[\![g_{\sigma}]\!]_{m,\beta_{*},\vartheta,\Sigma}=\|\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}w_{\beta_{*},\vartheta}g_{\sigma}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}+\|\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}w_{\beta_{*},\vartheta}g_{\sigma}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\Sigma}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|w_{\beta_{*},\vartheta}Y_{A}(\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}\varphi_{A,\sigma})\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}+\sum_{\Xi\in\{Z,U\}}\|w_{\beta_{*},\vartheta}\Xi(\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}(K_{\hbar}-\mathbf{D}_{\hbar})g_{\sigma}+\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}h_{\sigma})\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}

(4.16)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathscr{B}_{\infty}(\varphi_{A,\sigma})+C\|\nu^{-1}(\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}(K_{\hbar}-\mathbf{D}_{\hbar})g_{\sigma}+\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}h_{\sigma})\|_{A;\beta_{*},\vartheta}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathscr{B}_{\infty}(\varphi_{A,\sigma})+C\big([\![\nu^{-1}(K_{\hbar}-\mathbf{D}_{\hbar})g_{\sigma}]\!]_{A;m,\beta_{*},\vartheta}+\mathscr{A}^{A}_{\infty}(h_{\sigma})\big)\,.

By Lemma
2.3
and Lemma
2.7
, one has
[\![\nu^{-1}(K_{\hbar}-\mathbf{D}_{\hbar})g_{\sigma}]\!]_{A;m,\beta_{*},\vartheta}\leq C[\![g_{\sigma}]\!]_{A;m,\beta_{*}-1,\vartheta}
. It therefore infers that

\displaystyle[\![g_{\sigma}]\!]_{A;m,\beta_{*},\vartheta}+[\![g_{\sigma}]\!]_{m,\beta_{*},\vartheta,\Sigma}\leq C[\![g_{\sigma}]\!]_{A;m,\beta_{*}-1,\vartheta}+C\mathscr{A}^{A}_{\infty}(h_{\sigma})+\mathscr{B}_{\infty}(\varphi_{A,\sigma})\,.

Inductively, for any given integer
\beta_{*}\geq 0
, it follows that

(4.17)

\displaystyle[\![g_{\sigma}]\!]_{A;m,\beta_{*},\vartheta}+[\![g_{\sigma}]\!]_{m,\beta_{*},\vartheta,\Sigma}\leq C[\![g_{\sigma}]\!]_{A;m,0,\vartheta}+C\big(\mathscr{A}^{A}_{\infty}(h_{\sigma})+\mathscr{B}_{\infty}(\varphi_{A,\sigma})\big)\,,

where we have utilized
[\![\nu^{-1}h_{\sigma}]\!]_{A;m,i,\vartheta}\leq C[\![\nu^{-1}h_{\sigma}]\!]_{A;m,\beta_{*},\vartheta}
for
0\leq i\leq\beta_{*}
.

It remains to dominate the quantity
[\![g_{\sigma}]\!]_{A;m,0,\vartheta}
. Together with (
4.10
), the similar arguments in (
4.16
) indicate that

\displaystyle[\![g_{\sigma}]\!]_{A;m,0,\vartheta}+[\![g_{\sigma}]\!]_{m,0,\vartheta,\Sigma}\leq

\displaystyle C[\![\nu^{-1}(K_{\hbar}-\mathbf{D}_{\hbar})g_{\sigma}]\!]_{A;m,0,\vartheta}+C\big([\![\nu^{-1}h_{\sigma}]\!]_{A;m,0,\vartheta}+\|\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}(A,\cdot)w_{0,\vartheta}\varphi_{A,\sigma}\|_{L^{\infty}_{v}}\big)\,.

Lemmas
2.4
and Lemma
2.8
show that

\displaystyle[\![\nu^{-1}(K_{\hbar}-\mathbf{D}_{\hbar})g_{\sigma}]\!]_{A;m,0,\vartheta}\leq\eta_{1}[\![g_{\sigma}]\!]_{A;m,0,\vartheta}+C_{\eta_{1}}\|z_{\alpha^{\prime}}\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}w_{-\gamma,\vartheta}g_{\sigma}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}

for any small
\eta_{1}>0
. Taking
\eta_{1}>0
such that
C\eta_{1}\leq\frac{1}{2}
, it follows that

(4.18)

\displaystyle[\![g_{\sigma}]\!]_{A;m,0,\vartheta}+[\![g_{\sigma}]\!]_{m,0,\vartheta,\Sigma}\leq C\|z_{\alpha^{\prime}}\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}w_{-\gamma,\vartheta}g_{\sigma}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}+C\big(\mathscr{A}^{A}_{\infty}(h_{\sigma})+\mathscr{B}_{\infty}(\varphi_{A,\sigma})\big)\,.

Then (
4.17
) and (
4.18
) imply the bound (
4.14
).

By the virtue of the similar arguments in (
4.14
),
{\vartriangle}g_{\sigma}
subjecting to the equation (
3.20
) satisfies the bound (
4.15
). Then the proof of Lemma
4.2
is finished.
∎

4.3.
Estimate for
L^{\infty}_{x}L^{2}_{v}
norm with weight
z_{\alpha^{\prime}}\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}w_{-\gamma,\vartheta}

In this subsection, we will control the norm
\|z_{\alpha^{\prime}}\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}w_{-\gamma,\vartheta}g_{\sigma}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}
appeared in the right hand side of (
4.14
), Lemma
4.2
. More precisely, the follow lemma holds.

Lemma 4.3
.

Under the same assumptions in Lemma
4.2
, let
\alpha=\frac{1}{2}-\alpha^{\prime}
, where
\alpha^{\prime}
is given in Lemma
4.2
. Then there is a constant
C>0
, independent of
l
,
A
,
\delta
and
\hbar
, such that

(4.19)

\displaystyle\|z_{\alpha^{\prime}}\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}w_{-\gamma,\vartheta}g_{\sigma}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}\leq C\mathscr{E}_{\mathtt{cro}}^{A}(g_{\sigma})\,,

where the functional
\mathscr{E}_{\mathtt{cro}}^{A}(\cdot)
is given in (
1.38
). Moreover, one similarly has

(4.20)

\displaystyle\|z_{\alpha^{\prime}}\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}w_{-\gamma,\vartheta}{\vartriangle}g_{\sigma}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}\leq C\mathscr{E}_{\mathtt{cro}}^{A}({\vartriangle}g_{\sigma})\,.

Proof.

Denote by

\displaystyle\phi(x)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|z_{\alpha^{\prime}}\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}w_{-\gamma,\vartheta}g_{\sigma}(x,v)|^{2}\mathrm{d}v\,,\bar{\phi}(x,v)=\tfrac{\partial}{\partial x}|z_{\alpha^{\prime}}\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}w_{-\gamma,\vartheta}g_{\sigma}|^{2}(x,v)\,.

Then for any
x,y\in\bar{\Omega}_{A}
,

(4.21)

\displaystyle\phi(x)-\phi(y)=\int_{y}^{x}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\bar{\phi}(x^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}\,.

We claim that

(4.22)

\displaystyle\|\bar{\phi}\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}\leq C\mathscr{E}_{\mathtt{cro}}^{A}(g_{\sigma})\,.

Indeed, a direct computation yields

\displaystyle\|\bar{\phi}\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}\leq

\displaystyle\underbrace{2\int_{0}^{A}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}z_{\alpha^{\prime}}w_{-\gamma,\vartheta}g_{\sigma}|\cdot|\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}z_{\alpha^{\prime}}w_{-\gamma,\vartheta}\partial_{x}g_{\sigma}|\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}x}_{:=\scorpio_{1}}

(4.23)

\displaystyle+\underbrace{m\int_{0}^{A}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}z_{\alpha^{\prime}}w_{-\gamma,\vartheta}g_{\sigma}|\cdot|\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}-1}\sigma_{xx}z_{\alpha^{\prime}}w_{-\gamma,\vartheta}g_{\sigma}|\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}x}_{:=\scorpio_{2}}\,.

By the virtue of
|z_{1}\sigma_{xx}|\leq|v_{3}\sigma_{xx}|\leq\delta\nu(v)\sigma_{x}
derived from Lemma
2.1
, we have

(4.24)

\displaystyle\scorpio_{2}\leq\delta|m|\int_{0}^{A}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\nu(v)\sigma_{x}^{m}(x,v)z_{2\alpha^{\prime}-1}w_{-\gamma,\vartheta}^{2}(v)g_{\sigma}^{2}(x,v)\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}x=

\displaystyle\delta|m|\|\nu^{\frac{1}{2}}z_{-\alpha}\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}w_{-\gamma,\vartheta}g_{\sigma}\|^{2}_{A}\,,

where the fact
z_{2\alpha^{\prime}-1}=z_{-2\alpha}=z^{2}_{-\alpha}
with
\alpha=\frac{1}{2}-\alpha^{\prime}
has been used.

Furthermore, the relation
z_{2\alpha^{\prime}-1}=z^{2}_{-\alpha}
also implies

(4.25)

\displaystyle\scorpio_{1}=

\displaystyle 2\int_{0}^{A}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|\nu^{\frac{1}{2}}z_{-\alpha}\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}w_{-\gamma,\vartheta}g_{\sigma}|\cdot|\nu^{-\frac{1}{2}}z_{-\alpha}\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}z_{1}w_{-\gamma,\vartheta}\partial_{x}g_{\sigma}|\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\|\nu^{\frac{1}{2}}z_{-\alpha}\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}w_{-\gamma,\vartheta}g_{\sigma}\|_{A}\|\nu^{-\frac{1}{2}}z_{-\alpha}\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}z_{1}w_{-\gamma,\vartheta}\partial_{x}g_{\sigma}\|_{A}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|\nu^{\frac{1}{2}}z_{-\alpha}\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}w_{-\gamma,\vartheta}g_{\sigma}\|^{2}_{A}+\|\nu^{-\frac{1}{2}}z_{-\alpha}\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}z_{1}w_{-\gamma,\vartheta}\partial_{x}g_{\sigma}\|^{2}_{A}\,.

By using
w_{-\gamma,\vartheta}\leq w_{-\gamma+\beta_{\gamma},\vartheta}
, one can conclude the claim (
4.22
) from (
4.23
)-(
4.24
)-(
4.25
).

Since the claim (
4.22
) holds with the finite value in the right hand side of (
4.22
), the relation (
4.21
) tells us that
\phi\in C(\bar{\Omega}_{A})
. Let
M_{\phi}=\max_{x\in\bar{\Omega}_{A}}\phi(x)\geq 0
and
m_{\phi}=\min_{x\in\bar{\Omega}_{A}}\phi(x)\geq 0
. Then there are two points
x_{M}
,
x_{m}\in\bar{\Omega}_{A}
such that

\displaystyle 0\leq M_{\phi}-m_{\phi}=\phi(x_{M})-\phi(x_{m})=\int_{x_{m}}^{x_{M}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\bar{\phi}(x,v)\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}x\leq\|\bar{\phi}\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}\,.

If
M_{\phi}\geq\frac{3}{2}m_{\phi}
, one has
M_{\phi}\leq\tfrac{2}{3}M_{\phi}+\|\bar{\phi}\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}
, which means that

(4.26)

\displaystyle M_{\phi}\leq 3\|\bar{\phi}\|_{L^{1}_{x,v}}\,.

If
M_{\phi}<\tfrac{3}{2}m_{\phi}
, one has

(4.27)

\displaystyle M_{\phi}<

\displaystyle\tfrac{3}{2}m_{\phi}\leq\tfrac{3}{2A}\int_{0}^{A}\phi(x)\mathrm{d}x=\tfrac{3}{2A}\|\nu^{-\frac{1}{2}}z_{\alpha}z_{\alpha^{\prime}}\nu^{\frac{1}{2}}z_{-\alpha}\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}w_{-\gamma,\vartheta}g_{\sigma}\|^{2}_{A}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\|\nu^{\frac{1}{2}}z_{-\alpha}\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}w_{-\gamma+\beta_{\gamma},\vartheta}g_{\sigma}\|^{2}_{A}\,,

where the last inequality is derived from
A^{-1}\leq 1
for
A\geq 1
and
\nu^{-\frac{1}{2}}z_{\alpha}z_{\alpha^{\prime}}w_{-\gamma,\vartheta}\leq Cw_{-\gamma+\beta_{\gamma},\vartheta}
. We remark that the index
\beta_{\gamma}
given in (
1.31
) is required here. Consequently, (
4.22
), (
4.26
) and (
4.27
) indicate that

\displaystyle\|z_{\alpha^{\prime}}\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}w_{-\gamma,\vartheta}g_{\sigma}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}=M_{\phi}^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq C\mathscr{E}_{\mathtt{cro}}^{A}(g_{\sigma})\,.

Namely, the bound (
4.19
) holds. Furthermore, the estimate of the bound (
4.20
) is similar to that of (
4.19
). Thus the proof of Lemma
4.3
is completed.
∎

4.4.
Estimate for
L^{2}_{x,v}
with weight
\nu^{\frac{1}{2}}\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}z_{-\alpha}w_{-\gamma+\beta_{\gamma},\vartheta}

In this subsection, we will dominate the quantity
\mathscr{E}_{\mathtt{cro}}^{A}(g_{\sigma})=\|\nu^{-\frac{1}{2}}z_{-\alpha}\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}z_{1}w_{-\gamma+\beta_{\gamma},\vartheta}\partial_{x}g_{\sigma}\|_{A}
+\|\nu^{\frac{1}{2}}z_{-\alpha}\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}w_{-\gamma+\beta_{\gamma},\vartheta}g_{\sigma}\|_{A}
in the right hand side of (
4.19
).

Lemma 4.4
.

Under the same assumptions in Lemma
4.3
, there is a constant
C>0
, independent of
A
,
l
,
\delta
and
\hbar
, such that

(4.28)

\displaystyle[1-(1-\alpha_{*})^{2}]\||v_{3}|^{\frac{1}{2}}\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}z_{-\alpha}w_{-\gamma+\beta_{\gamma},\vartheta}g_{\sigma}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\Sigma_{+}^{0}}}+\||v_{3}|^{\frac{1}{2}}\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}z_{-\alpha}w_{-\gamma+\beta_{\gamma},\vartheta}g_{\sigma}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\Sigma_{+}^{A}}}+[\mathscr{E}_{\mathtt{cro}}^{A}(g_{\sigma})]^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\|(\delta x+l)^{-\frac{m(1-\gamma)}{2(3-\gamma)}}\nu^{\frac{1}{2}}w_{-\gamma+\beta_{\gamma}-\mathbf{1}_{m<0}m(1-\gamma)/2,\vartheta}g_{\sigma}\|^{2}_{A}+C[\mathscr{A}^{A}_{\mathtt{cro}}(h_{\sigma})]^{2}+C[\mathscr{B}_{\mathtt{cro}}(\varphi_{A,\sigma})]^{2}

\displaystyle+C\alpha_{*}(\delta\hbar)^{-15}l^{-\frac{3(3+\gamma)}{32(3-\gamma)}}\big[\mathscr{E}^{A}_{2}(g_{\sigma})+\mathscr{E}_{\mathtt{NBE}}^{A}(g_{\sigma})\big]+C_{l}\alpha_{*}\mathscr{A}_{\mathtt{NBE}}^{A}(h_{\sigma})

for a constant
C_{l}>0
depending on
l
but being independent of
A,\delta,\hbar
, where the functionals
\mathscr{E}_{\mathtt{cro}}^{A}(\cdot)
,
\mathscr{E}_{2}^{A}(\cdot)
,
\mathscr{E}_{\mathtt{NBE}}^{A}(\cdot)
,
\mathscr{A}_{\mathtt{NBE}}^{A}(\cdot)
,
\mathscr{A}^{A}_{\mathtt{cro}}(\cdot)
and
\mathscr{B}_{\mathtt{cro}}(\cdot)
are introduced in (
1.38
), (
1.41
), (
1.39
), (
1.43
), (
1.43
) and (
1.44
), respectively. Similarly, there holds

\displaystyle[1-(1-\alpha_{*})^{2}]\||v_{3}|^{\frac{1}{2}}\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}z_{-\alpha}w_{-\gamma+\beta_{\gamma},\vartheta}{\vartriangle}g_{\sigma}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\Sigma_{+}^{0}}}+\||v_{3}|^{\frac{1}{2}}\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}z_{-\alpha}w_{-\gamma+\beta_{\gamma},\vartheta}{\vartriangle}g_{\sigma}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\Sigma_{+}^{A}}}+[\mathscr{E}_{\mathtt{cro}}^{A}({\vartriangle}g_{\sigma})]^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\|(\delta x+l)^{-\frac{m(1-\gamma)}{2(3-\gamma)}}\nu^{\frac{1}{2}}w_{-\gamma+\beta_{\gamma}-\mathbf{1}_{m<0}m(1-\gamma)/2,\vartheta}{\vartriangle}g_{\sigma}\|^{2}_{A}+C[\mathscr{A}^{A}_{\mathtt{cro}}({\vartriangle}h_{\sigma})]^{2}

(4.29)

\displaystyle+C\alpha_{*}(\delta\hbar)^{-15}l^{-\frac{3(3+\gamma)}{32(3-\gamma)}}\big[\mathscr{E}^{A}_{2}({\vartriangle}g_{\sigma})+\mathscr{E}_{\mathtt{NBE}}^{A}({\vartriangle}g_{\sigma})\big]+C_{l}\alpha_{*}\mathscr{A}_{\mathtt{NBE}}^{A}({\vartriangle}h_{\sigma})\,.

Proof.

For
0\leq\alpha<\mu_{\gamma}
with
\mu_{\gamma}>0
given in Lemma
2.5
, multiplying (
3.1
) by
\sigma_{x}^{m}z_{-\alpha}^{2}w_{-\gamma+\beta_{\gamma},\vartheta}^{2}g_{\sigma}
and integrating by parts over
(x,v)\in\Omega_{A}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
, one has

\displaystyle\iint_{\Omega_{A}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}\partial_{x}\big(\tfrac{1}{2}v_{3}z_{-\alpha}^{2}\sigma_{x}^{m}w_{-\gamma+\beta_{\gamma},\vartheta}^{2}g_{\sigma}^{2}\big)\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}x-\tfrac{1}{2}\iint_{\Omega_{A}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}mv_{3}z_{-\alpha}^{2}\sigma_{xx}\sigma_{x}^{m-1}w_{-\gamma+\beta_{\gamma},\vartheta}^{2}g_{\sigma}^{2}\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle+\iint_{\Omega_{A}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}[-\hbar\sigma_{x}v_{3}+\nu(v)]\sigma_{x}^{m}z_{-\alpha}^{2}w_{-\gamma+\beta_{\gamma},\vartheta}^{2}g_{\sigma}^{2}\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle-\iint_{\Omega_{A}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}(K_{\hbar}-\mathbf{D}_{\hbar})g_{\sigma}\cdot\sigma_{x}^{m}z_{-\alpha}^{2}w_{-\gamma+\beta_{\gamma},\vartheta}^{2}g_{\sigma}^{2}\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}x=\iint_{\Omega_{A}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}h_{\sigma}\cdot\sigma_{x}^{m}z_{-\alpha}^{2}w_{-\gamma+\beta_{\gamma},\vartheta}^{2}g_{\sigma}^{2}\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}x\,.

Notice that by Lemma
2.1
,

(4.30)

\displaystyle\iint_{\Omega_{A}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}[-\hbar\sigma_{x}v_{3}+\nu(v)]\sigma_{x}^{m}z_{-\alpha}^{2}w_{-\gamma+\beta_{\gamma},\vartheta}^{2}g_{\sigma}^{2}\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}x\geq c_{\hbar}\|\nu^{\frac{1}{2}}z_{-\alpha}\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}w_{-\gamma+\beta_{\gamma},\vartheta}g_{\sigma}\|^{2}_{A}\,,

where
c_{\hbar}=1-c\hbar>\frac{1}{2}
for sufficiently small
\hbar\geq 0
. Observe that

\displaystyle\iint_{\Omega_{A}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}\partial_{x}\big(\tfrac{1}{2}v_{3}z_{-\alpha}^{2}\sigma_{x}^{m}w_{-\gamma+\beta_{\gamma},\vartheta}^{2}g_{\sigma}^{2}\big)\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}x

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}v_{3}z_{-\alpha}^{2}\sigma_{x}^{m}w_{-\gamma+\beta_{\gamma},\vartheta}^{2}g_{\sigma}^{2}(A,v)\mathrm{d}v-\tfrac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}v_{3}z_{-\alpha}^{2}\sigma_{x}^{m}(0,v)w_{-\gamma+\beta_{\gamma},\vartheta}^{2}(v)g_{\sigma}^{2}(0,v)\mathrm{d}v\,.

By the boundary conditions in (
3.1
), it infers that

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}v_{3}z_{-\alpha}^{2}\sigma_{x}^{m}w_{-\gamma+\beta_{\gamma},\vartheta}^{2}g_{\sigma}^{2}(A,v)\mathrm{d}v

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{v_{3}>0}v_{3}z_{-\alpha}^{2}\sigma_{x}^{m}w_{-\gamma+\beta_{\gamma},\vartheta}^{2}g_{\sigma}^{2}(A,v)\mathrm{d}v+\int_{v_{3}<0}v_{3}z_{-\alpha}^{2}\sigma_{x}^{m}w_{-\gamma+\beta_{\gamma},\vartheta}^{2}g_{\sigma}^{2}(A,v)\mathrm{d}v

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{v_{3}>0}|v_{3}|z_{-\alpha}^{2}\sigma_{x}^{m}w_{-\gamma+\beta_{\gamma},\vartheta}^{2}g_{\sigma}^{2}(A,v)\mathrm{d}v-\int_{v_{3}<0}|v_{3}|z_{-\alpha}^{2}\sigma_{x}^{m}w_{-\gamma+\beta_{\gamma},\vartheta}^{2}\varphi_{A,\sigma}^{2}(A,v)\mathrm{d}v

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\||v_{3}|^{\frac{1}{2}}z_{-\alpha}\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}w_{-\gamma+\beta_{\gamma},\vartheta}g_{\sigma}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\Sigma_{+}^{A}}}-\||v_{3}|^{\frac{1}{2}}z_{-\alpha}\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}w_{-\gamma+\beta_{\gamma},\vartheta}\varphi_{A,\sigma}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\Sigma_{-}^{A}}}\,.

Moreover, we choose
w_{*}(v)=z_{-\alpha}^{2}\sigma_{x}^{m}(0,v)w^{2}_{-\gamma+\beta_{\gamma},\vartheta}(v)
in Lemma
3.2
. Then the estimate (
3.14
) thereby reduces to

\displaystyle-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}v_{3}z_{-\alpha}^{2}\sigma_{x}^{m}(0,v)w_{-\gamma+\beta_{\gamma},\vartheta}^{2}(v)g_{\sigma}^{2}(0,v)\mathrm{d}v

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle[1-(1-\alpha_{*})^{2}]\int_{v_{3}<0}|v_{3}|z_{-\alpha}^{2}\sigma_{x}^{m}(0,v)w_{-\gamma+\beta_{\gamma},\vartheta}^{2}(v)g_{\sigma}^{2}(0,v)\mathrm{d}v

\displaystyle-C_{0}\alpha_{*}\big(\digamma_{\delta,l,a,\mathbf{Z}}(g_{\sigma})+\digamma_{\delta,l,a,\mathbf{Z}}(\nu^{-1}h_{\sigma})\big)^{2}-C_{0}\alpha_{*}\||v_{3}|^{\frac{1}{2}}\varphi_{A,\sigma}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\Sigma_{-}^{A}}}\,,

where the functional
\digamma_{\delta,l,a,\mathbf{Z}}(\cdot)
is defined in (
3.3
). Observe that
1\leq\sqrt{C_{1}}z_{-\alpha}\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}w_{-\gamma+\beta_{\gamma},\vartheta}
for some universal constant
C_{1}>0
. As a result, one has

\displaystyle\iint_{\Omega_{A}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}\partial_{x}\big(\tfrac{1}{2}v_{3}z_{-\alpha}^{2}\sigma_{x}^{m}w_{-\gamma+\beta_{\gamma},\vartheta}^{2}g_{\sigma}^{2}\big)\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}x

(4.31)

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{2}[1-(1-\alpha_{*})^{2}]\||v_{3}|^{\frac{1}{2}}z_{-\alpha}\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}w_{-\gamma+\beta_{\gamma},\vartheta}g_{\sigma}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\Sigma_{+}^{0}}}+\tfrac{1}{2}\||v_{3}|^{\frac{1}{2}}z_{-\alpha}\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}w_{-\gamma+\beta_{\gamma},\vartheta}g_{\sigma}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\Sigma_{+}^{A}}}

\displaystyle-\tfrac{1}{2}C_{0}\alpha_{*}\big(\digamma_{\delta,l,a,\mathbf{Z}}(g_{\sigma})+\digamma_{\delta,l,a,\mathbf{Z}}(\nu^{-1}h_{\sigma})\big)^{2}-\tfrac{1}{2}(1+C_{0}C_{1}\alpha_{*})\||v_{3}|^{\frac{1}{2}}z_{-\alpha}\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}w_{-\gamma+\beta_{\gamma},\vartheta}\varphi_{A,\sigma}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\Sigma_{-}^{A}}}\,.

By Lemma
2.1
,
|v_{3}\sigma_{xx}|\leq\delta\sigma_{x}\nu(v)
. Then it holds

\displaystyle\big|\tfrac{1}{2}\iint_{\Omega_{A}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}mv_{3}z_{-\alpha}^{2}\sigma_{xx}\sigma_{x}^{m-1}w_{-\gamma+\beta_{\gamma},\vartheta}^{2}g_{\sigma}^{2}\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}x\big|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\delta\|\nu^{\frac{1}{2}}z_{-\alpha}\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}w_{-\gamma+\beta_{\gamma},\vartheta}g_{\sigma}\|^{2}_{A}\leq\tfrac{c_{\hbar}}{16}\|\nu^{\frac{1}{2}}z_{-\alpha}\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}w_{-\gamma+\beta_{\gamma},\vartheta}g_{\sigma}\|^{2}_{A}\,,

where
\delta>0
is taken small enough such that
C\delta\leq\frac{c_{\hbar}}{16}
. Moreover, the Hölder inequality shows

\displaystyle\big|\iint_{\Omega_{A}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}h_{\sigma}\cdot\sigma_{x}^{m}z_{-\alpha}^{2}w_{-\gamma+\beta_{\gamma},\vartheta}^{2}g_{\sigma}^{2}\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}x\big|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\tfrac{c_{\hbar}}{8}\|\nu^{\frac{1}{2}}z_{-\alpha}\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}w_{-\gamma+\beta_{\gamma},\vartheta}g_{\sigma}\|^{2}_{A}+C\|\nu^{-\frac{1}{2}}z_{-\alpha}\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}w_{-\gamma+\beta_{\gamma},\vartheta}h_{\sigma}\|^{2}_{A}\,,

and

\displaystyle\big|\iint_{\Omega_{A}\times\mathbb{R}^{3}}(K_{\hbar}-\mathbf{D}_{\hbar})g_{\sigma}\cdot\sigma_{x}^{m}z_{-\alpha}^{2}w_{-\gamma+\beta_{\gamma},\vartheta}^{2}g_{\sigma}^{2}\mathrm{d}v\mathrm{d}x\big|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\tfrac{c_{\hbar}}{4}\|\nu^{\frac{1}{2}}z_{-\alpha}\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}w_{-\gamma+\beta_{\gamma},\vartheta}g_{\sigma}\|^{2}_{A}+C\|\nu^{-\frac{1}{2}}z_{-\alpha}\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}w_{-\gamma+\beta_{\gamma},\vartheta}(K_{\hbar}-\mathbf{D}_{\hbar})g_{\sigma}\|^{2}_{A}\,.

We thereby establish

\displaystyle[1-(1-\alpha_{*})^{2}]\||v_{3}|^{\frac{1}{2}}\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}z_{-\alpha}w_{-\gamma+\beta_{\gamma},\vartheta}g_{\sigma}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\Sigma_{+}}}

\displaystyle+\||v_{3}|^{\frac{1}{2}}\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}z_{-\alpha}w_{-\gamma+\beta_{\gamma},\vartheta}g_{\sigma}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\Sigma_{+}^{A}}}+\|\nu^{\frac{1}{2}}z_{-\alpha}\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}w_{-\gamma+\beta_{\gamma},\vartheta}g_{\sigma}\|^{2}_{A}

(4.32)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\|\nu^{-\frac{1}{2}}z_{-\alpha}\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}xw_{-\gamma+\beta_{\gamma},\vartheta}(K_{\hbar}-\mathbf{D}_{\hbar})g_{\sigma}\|^{2}_{A}+C[\mathscr{A}^{A}_{\mathtt{cro}}(h_{\sigma})]^{2}+C[\mathscr{B}_{\mathtt{cro}}(\varphi_{A,\sigma})]^{2}

\displaystyle+C\alpha_{*}\big(\digamma_{\delta,l,a,\mathbf{Z}}(g_{\sigma})+\digamma_{\delta,l,a,\mathbf{Z}}(\nu^{-1}h_{\sigma})\big)^{2}\,.

Lemma
2.5
and Lemma
2.9
show that for
0\leq\alpha<\mu_{\gamma}
and
-3<\gamma\leq 1
,

(4.33)

\displaystyle\|\nu^{-\frac{1}{2}}z_{-\alpha}\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}w_{-\gamma+\beta_{\gamma},\vartheta}(K_{\hbar}-\mathbf{D}_{\hbar})g_{\sigma}\|^{2}_{A}\lesssim\|\nu^{\frac{1}{2}}\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}w_{-\gamma+\beta_{\gamma},\vartheta}g_{\sigma}\|^{2}_{A}\,.

It follow from Lemma
2.1
that

\displaystyle\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}(x,v)\lesssim

\displaystyle\max\{(\delta x+l)^{-\frac{m(1-\gamma)}{2(3-\gamma)}},(1+|v-\mathfrak{u}|)^{-\frac{m(1-\gamma)}{2}}\}\lesssim

\displaystyle(\delta x+l)^{-\frac{m(1-\gamma)}{2(3-\gamma)}}(1+|v-\mathfrak{u}|)^{-\frac{m(1-\gamma)}{2}}

for
m<0
, and

\displaystyle\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}(x,v)\lesssim(\delta x+l)^{-\frac{m(1-\gamma)}{2(3-\gamma)}}

for
m\geq 0
. It thereby infers

(4.34)

\displaystyle\|\nu^{\frac{1}{2}}\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}w_{-\gamma+\beta_{\gamma},\vartheta}g_{\sigma}\|_{A}\lesssim\|(\delta x+l)^{-\frac{m(1-\gamma)}{2(3-\gamma)}}\nu^{\frac{1}{2}}w_{-\gamma+\beta_{\gamma}-\mathbf{1}_{m<0}m(1-\gamma)/2,\vartheta}g_{\sigma}\|_{A}\,.

If we take
a=\frac{15}{8(3-\gamma)}
and
\mathbf{Z}=6
, then the inequality (
3.28
) indicates that

(4.35)

\displaystyle\digamma_{\delta,l,a,\mathbf{Z}}^{2}(g_{\sigma})\lesssim

\displaystyle(\delta\hbar)^{-2}l^{-100}\mathscr{E}^{A}_{2}(g_{\sigma})

\displaystyle+(\delta\hbar)^{-2}l^{-\frac{125}{24(3-\gamma)}}l^{\frac{59-24\gamma}{24(3-\gamma)}}\|(l^{-6}\delta x+l)^{\frac{15}{8(3-\gamma)}}(\delta x+l)^{-\frac{1-\gamma}{2(3-\gamma)}}\nu^{\frac{1}{2}}g_{\sigma}\|_{A}^{2}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle(\delta\hbar)^{-2}l^{-100}\mathscr{E}^{A}_{2}(g_{\sigma})+(\delta\hbar)^{-2}l^{-\frac{125}{24(3-\gamma)}}\mathscr{E}_{\mathtt{NBE}}^{A}(g_{\sigma})\,,

where the functionals
\mathscr{E}^{A}_{2}(g_{\sigma})
and
\mathscr{E}_{\mathtt{NBE}}^{A}(g_{\sigma})
are defined in (
1.41
) and (
1.39
), respectively. Moreover, under the choice
a=\frac{15}{8(3-\gamma)}
and
\mathbf{Z}=6
, the quantity
\digamma_{\delta,l,a,\mathbf{Z}}^{2}(\nu^{-1}h_{\sigma})
can be easily bounded by

(4.36)

\displaystyle\digamma_{\delta,l,a,\mathbf{Z}}^{2}(\nu^{-1}h_{\sigma})\leq C_{l}\mathscr{A}_{\mathtt{NBE}}^{A}(h_{\sigma})

for some constant
C_{l}>0
depending on
l
but being independent of
A,\delta,\hbar
, where the functional
\mathscr{A}_{\mathtt{NBE}}^{A}(h_{\sigma})
is given in (
1.43
).

As a result, the bounds (
4.32
), (
4.33
), (
4.34
), (
4.35
) and (
4.36
) indicate that

\displaystyle[1-(1-\alpha_{*})^{2}]\||v_{3}|^{\frac{1}{2}}\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}z_{-\alpha}w_{-\gamma+\beta_{\gamma},\vartheta}g_{\sigma}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\Sigma_{+}^{0}}}

\displaystyle+\||v_{3}|^{\frac{1}{2}}\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}z_{-\alpha}w_{-\gamma+\beta_{\gamma},\vartheta}g_{\sigma}\|^{2}_{L^{2}_{\Sigma_{+}^{A}}}+\|\nu^{\frac{1}{2}}z_{-\alpha}\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}w_{-\gamma+\beta_{\gamma},\vartheta}g_{\sigma}\|^{2}_{A}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\|(\delta x+l)^{-\frac{m(1-\gamma)}{2(3-\gamma)}}\nu^{\frac{1}{2}}w_{-\gamma+\beta_{\gamma}-\mathbf{1}_{m<0}m(1-\gamma)/2,\vartheta}g_{\sigma}\|^{2}_{A}+C[\mathscr{A}^{A}_{\mathtt{cro}}(h_{\sigma})]^{2}+C[\mathscr{B}_{\mathtt{cro}}(\varphi_{A,\sigma})]^{2}

(4.37)

\displaystyle+C\alpha_{*}(\delta\hbar)^{-2}l^{-100}\mathscr{E}^{A}_{2}(g_{\sigma})+C\alpha_{*}(\delta\hbar)^{-2}l^{-\frac{125}{24(3-\gamma)}}\mathscr{E}_{\mathtt{NBE}}^{A}(g_{\sigma})+C_{l}\alpha_{*}\mathscr{A}_{\mathtt{NBE}}^{A}(h_{\sigma})

for
0\leq\alpha<\mu_{\gamma}
. Here the constant
C>0
is independent of
A
,
\delta
,
\hbar
and
l
.

Recalling (
3.1
), one has
\partial_{x}g_{\sigma}=-[-\hbar\sigma_{x}+\tfrac{\nu(v)}{v_{3}}]g_{\sigma}+\tfrac{1}{v_{3}}(K_{\hbar}-\mathbf{D}_{\hbar})g_{\sigma}+\tfrac{1}{v_{3}}h_{\sigma}
, which means

\displaystyle|\nu^{-\frac{1}{2}}z_{-\alpha}\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}z_{1}w_{-\gamma+\beta_{\gamma},\vartheta}\partial_{x}g_{\sigma}|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C(\hbar\nu^{-1}z_{1}\sigma_{x}+\tfrac{z_{1}}{|v_{3}|})|\nu^{\frac{1}{2}}z_{-\alpha}\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}w_{-\gamma+\beta_{\gamma},\vartheta}g_{\sigma}|

\displaystyle+C|\tfrac{z_{1}}{|v_{3}|}\nu^{-\frac{1}{2}}\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}z_{-\alpha}w_{-\gamma+\beta_{\gamma},\vartheta}(K_{\hbar}-\mathbf{D}_{\hbar})g_{\sigma}|+C|\tfrac{z_{1}}{|v_{3}|}\nu^{-\frac{1}{2}}\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}z_{-\alpha}w_{-\gamma+\beta_{\gamma},\vartheta}h_{\sigma}|\,.

Observe that
\frac{z_{1}}{|v_{3}|}\leq 1
, and
\sigma_{x}\leq c\frac{\nu(v)}{|v_{3}|}
by Lemma
2.1
, which imply
\hbar\nu^{-1}z_{1}\sigma_{x}+\tfrac{z_{1}}{|v_{3}|}\leq c\hbar+1
. It therefore follows that

\displaystyle\|\nu^{-\frac{1}{2}}z_{-\alpha}\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}z_{1}w_{-\gamma+\beta_{\gamma},\vartheta}

\displaystyle\partial_{x}g_{\sigma}\|^{2}_{A}\leq C\|\nu^{\frac{1}{2}}z_{-\alpha}\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}w_{-\gamma+\beta_{\gamma},\vartheta}g_{\sigma}\|^{2}_{A}

\displaystyle+C\|\nu^{-\frac{1}{2}}\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}z_{-\alpha}w_{-\gamma+\beta_{\gamma},\vartheta}(K_{\hbar}-\mathbf{D}_{\hbar})g_{\sigma}\|^{2}_{A}+C[\mathscr{A}^{A}_{\mathtt{cro}}(h_{\sigma})]^{2}\,.

Lemma
2.5
and Lemma
2.9
show that

\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|\nu^{-\frac{1}{2}}\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}z_{-\alpha}w_{-\gamma+\beta_{\gamma},\vartheta}(K_{\hbar}-\mathbf{D}_{\hbar})g_{\sigma}|^{2}\mathrm{d}v\leq C\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|\nu^{\frac{1}{2}}z_{-\alpha}\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}w_{-\gamma+\beta_{\gamma},\vartheta}g_{\sigma}|^{2}\mathrm{d}v\,.

Then, together with (
4.37
), one has

(4.38)

\displaystyle\|\nu^{-\frac{1}{2}}z_{-\alpha}\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}z_{1}w_{-\gamma+\beta_{\gamma},\vartheta}\partial_{x}g_{\sigma}\|^{2}_{A}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\|\nu^{\frac{1}{2}}z_{-\alpha}\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}w_{-\gamma+\beta_{\gamma},\vartheta}g_{\sigma}\|^{2}_{A}+C\|\nu^{-\frac{1}{2}}\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}z_{-\alpha}w_{-\gamma+\beta_{\gamma},\vartheta}h_{\sigma}\|^{2}_{A}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\|(\delta x+l)^{-\frac{m(1-\gamma)}{2(3-\gamma)}}\nu^{\frac{1}{2}}w_{-\gamma+\beta_{\gamma}-\mathbf{1}_{m<0}m(1-\gamma)/2,\vartheta}g_{\sigma}\|^{2}_{A}+C[\mathscr{A}^{A}_{\mathtt{cro}}(h_{\sigma})]^{2}+C[\mathscr{B}_{\mathtt{cro}}(\varphi_{A,\sigma})]^{2}

\displaystyle+C\alpha_{*}(\delta\hbar)^{-2}l^{-100}\mathscr{E}^{A}_{2}(g_{\sigma})+C\alpha_{*}(\delta\hbar)^{-2}l^{-\frac{125}{24(3-\gamma)}}\mathscr{E}_{\mathtt{NBE}}^{A}(g_{\sigma})+C_{l}\alpha_{*}\mathscr{A}_{\mathtt{NBE}}^{A}(h_{\sigma})\,.

Note that
(\delta\hbar)^{-2}l^{-100},(\delta\hbar)^{-2}l^{-\frac{125}{24(3-\gamma)}}\leq(\delta\hbar)^{-15}l^{-\frac{3(3+\gamma)}{32(3-\gamma)}}
for all
-3<\gamma\leq 1
. Then the bounds (
4.37
) and (
4.38
) conclude the estimate (
4.28
).

Furthermore, as the similar arguments in (
4.37
) and (
4.38
), one can easily knows that
{\vartriangle}g_{\sigma}
obeying the equation (
3.20
) satisfies the bound (
4.29
). Consequently, the proof of Lemma
4.4
is completed.
∎

4.5.
Close weighted
L^{\infty}_{x,v}
bound: Proof of Lemma
4.1

In this subsection, we will finish the proof of Lemma
4.1
based on Lemma
4.2
, Lemma
4.3
, Lemma
4.4
and Lemma
3.5
.

Proof of Lemma
4.1
.

First, it follows from the inequalities (
4.14
) in Lemma
4.2
and (
4.19
) in Lemma
4.3
that

(4.39)

\displaystyle\mathscr{E}^{A}_{\infty}(g_{\sigma})+[\![g_{\sigma}]\!]_{m,\beta_{*},\vartheta,\Sigma}\lesssim\mathscr{E}^{A}_{\mathtt{cro}}(g_{\sigma})+\mathscr{A}_{\infty}^{A}(h_{\sigma})+\mathscr{B}_{\infty}(\varphi_{A,\sigma})\,.

Together with (
4.28
) in Lemma
4.4
and (
4.39
), one then gains

(4.40)

\displaystyle\mathscr{E}^{A}_{\infty}(g_{\sigma})+[\![g_{\sigma}]\!]_{m,\beta_{*},\vartheta,\Sigma}+\mathscr{E}^{A}_{\mathtt{cro}}(g_{\sigma})

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\|(\delta x+l)^{-\frac{m(1-\gamma)}{2(3-\gamma)}}\nu^{\frac{1}{2}}w_{-\gamma+\beta_{\gamma}-\mathbf{1}_{m<0}m(1-\gamma)/2,\vartheta}g_{\sigma}\|_{A}

\displaystyle+\mathscr{A}^{A}_{\mathtt{cro}}(h_{\sigma})+\mathscr{B}_{\mathtt{cro}}(\varphi_{A,\sigma})+\mathscr{A}_{\infty}^{A}(h_{\sigma})+\mathscr{B}_{\infty}(\varphi_{A,\sigma})

\displaystyle+\alpha_{*}^{\frac{1}{2}}(\delta\hbar)^{-\frac{15}{2}}l^{-\frac{3(3+\gamma)}{64(3-\gamma)}}\big[\mathscr{E}^{A}_{2}(g_{\sigma})+\mathscr{E}_{\mathtt{NBE}}^{A}(g_{\sigma})\big]^{\frac{1}{2}}+C_{l}\alpha_{*}^{\frac{1}{2}}\big[\mathscr{A}_{\mathtt{NBE}}^{A}(h_{\sigma})\big]^{\frac{1}{2}}\,.

Notice that for
m\geq 1
and
\beta\geq-\gamma+\beta_{\gamma}
in the functional
\mathscr{E}^{A}_{2}(g_{\sigma})
,

(4.41)

\displaystyle\|(\delta x+l)^{-\frac{m(1-\gamma)}{2(3-\gamma)}}\nu^{\frac{1}{2}}w_{-\gamma+\beta_{\gamma}-\mathbf{1}_{m<0}m(1-\gamma)/2,\vartheta}g_{\sigma}\|_{A}\leq(\delta\hbar)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\big[\mathscr{E}^{A}_{2}(g_{\sigma})\big]^{\frac{1}{2}}\,.

Recalling that
\beta\geq 3(\beta_{\gamma}+\frac{1}{2})
is required in Lemma
3.5
, we take
\beta\geq\max\{3(\beta_{\gamma}+\frac{1}{2}),\beta_{\gamma}-\gamma\}=3(\beta_{\gamma}+\frac{1}{2})
for
-3<\gamma\leq 1
, where the last equality holds due to the definition of
\beta_{\gamma}
in (
1.31
).

Combining with the bounds (
4.40
), (
4.41
) and (
3.38
) in Lemma
3.5
, one easily sees that

(4.42)

\displaystyle\mathscr{E}^{A}_{\infty}(g_{\sigma})+[\![g_{\sigma}]\!]_{m,\beta_{*},\vartheta,\Sigma}+\mathscr{E}^{A}_{\mathtt{cro}}(g_{\sigma})+(\delta\hbar)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\big[\mathscr{E}^{A}_{2}(g_{\sigma})+\mathscr{E}_{\mathtt{NBE}}^{A}(g_{\sigma})\big]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle+\mathscr{A}^{A}_{\mathtt{cro}}(h_{\sigma})+\mathscr{B}_{\mathtt{cro}}(\varphi_{A,\sigma})+\mathscr{A}_{\infty}^{A}(h_{\sigma})+\mathscr{B}_{\infty}(\varphi_{A,\sigma})

\displaystyle+\alpha_{*}^{\frac{1}{2}}(\delta\hbar)^{-8}l^{-\frac{3(3+\gamma)}{64(3-\gamma)}}(\delta\hbar)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\big[\mathscr{E}^{A}_{2}(g_{\sigma})+\mathscr{E}_{\mathtt{NBE}}^{A}(g_{\sigma})\big]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle+C_{l}\big[\mathscr{A}_{2}^{A}(h_{\sigma})+\mathscr{A}_{\mathtt{NBE}}^{A}(h_{\sigma})\big]^{\frac{1}{2}}+C_{l}\big[\mathscr{B}_{2}(\varphi_{A,\sigma})+\mathscr{B}_{\mathtt{NBE}}(\varphi_{A,\sigma})\big]^{\frac{1}{2}}\,.

Under the assumption
l\geq O(1)(\delta\hbar)^{-\frac{512(3-\gamma)}{3(3+\gamma)}}
in Lemma
3.5
for sufficiently large
O(1)\gg 1
independent of
A,\delta,\hbar,l
, the coefficient
\alpha_{*}^{\frac{1}{2}}(\delta\hbar)^{-8}l^{-\frac{3(3+\gamma)}{64(3-\gamma)}}
is actually sufficiently small, so that the quantity
\alpha_{*}^{\frac{1}{2}}(\delta\hbar)^{-8}l^{-\frac{3(3+\gamma)}{64(3-\gamma)}}(\delta\hbar)^{-\frac{1}{2}}\big[\mathscr{E}^{A}_{2}(g_{\sigma})+\mathscr{E}_{\mathtt{NBE}}^{A}(g_{\sigma})\big]^{\frac{1}{2}}
in the right-hand side of (
4.42
) can be absorbed by that in the left-hand side of (
4.42
). Then the bound (
4.12
) holds.

Moreover, by the similar arguments in (
4.42
), we can also easily prove the bound (
4.13
) for the difference
{\vartriangle}g_{\sigma}
. Therefore, the proof of Lemma
4.1
is finished.
∎

5.
Existence of linear problem (
KL
): Proof of Theorem
1.1

In this section, we mainly justify the existence and uniqueness of the linear problem (
KL
). We first prove the existence result of the damped problem (
1.66
) based on the uniform-in-
A

L^{\infty}_{x,v}
solution to the equation (
CA-eq
) with
\varphi_{A}=0
constructed in Lemma
4.1
. Then by characterizing the structure of the vanishing sources set
\mathbb{VSS}_{\alpha_{*}}
defined in (
1.24
), one can remove the artificial damping
\mathbf{D}
in (
1.66
), which means the existence of the equation (
1.60
). At the end, together with the ODE problem (
1.59
), the existence of the equation (
KL
) can be obtained, hence, proof of Theorem
1.1
.

5.1.
Limits from (
CA-eq
) with
\varphi_{A}=0
to (
1.66
)

In this section, based on Lemma
4.1
, we will construct the existence of the problem (
1.66
) by taking limit
A\to+\infty
in the equation (
CA-eq
) with
\varphi_{A}=0
.

For small
\hbar>0
given in Lemma
4.1
, we initially define a Banach space

(5.1)

\displaystyle\mathbb{B}_{\infty}^{\hbar}:=\{f(x,v);\mathscr{E}^{\infty}(e^{\hbar\sigma}f)<\infty\}

with the norm

(5.2)

\displaystyle\|f\|_{\mathbb{B}_{\infty}^{\hbar}}=\mathscr{E}^{\infty}(e^{\hbar\sigma}f)\,,

where the functional
\mathscr{E}^{\infty}(\cdot)
is given in (
1.36
) with
A=\infty
.

Now we state the existence result on the problem (
1.66
) as follows.

Lemma 5.1
.

Assume that the parameters
\{\gamma,\alpha_{*},\beta,\beta_{*},\alpha,m,\delta,\hbar,\vartheta,l,\rho,T,T_{w},\mathfrak{u},u_{w}\}
satisfy the hypotheses (PH) given in Theorem
1.1
. Further assume that the source term
h(x,v)
in (
1.66
) satisfies

(5.3)

\displaystyle\mathscr{A}^{\infty}(e^{\hbar\sigma}h)<\infty\,,

where the functional
\mathscr{A}^{\infty}(\cdot)
is defined in (
1.42
) with
A=\infty
. Then the problem (
1.66
) admits a unique solution
g=g(x,v)\in\mathbb{B}_{\infty}^{\hbar}
such that

(5.4)

\displaystyle\|g\|_{\mathbb{B}_{\infty}^{\hbar}}\leq C\mathscr{A}^{\infty}(e^{\hbar\sigma}h)

for some constant
C>0
.

Proof of Lemma
5.1
.

We mainly consider the approximate problem (
CA-eq
) with
\varphi_{A}(v)=0
. Then

(5.5)

\displaystyle\mathscr{B}(e^{\hbar\sigma}\varphi_{A})=0

for all
A\geq 1
, where the functional
\mathscr{B}(\cdot)
is defined in (
1.42
). The conditions (
5.5
) and (
5.3
) tell us that for any
1\leq A<\infty
,

(5.6)

\displaystyle\mathscr{A}^{A}(e^{\hbar\sigma}h)+\mathscr{B}(e^{\hbar\sigma}\varphi_{A})\leq\mathfrak{C}_{\flat}:=\mathscr{A}^{\infty}(e^{\hbar\sigma}h)<\infty

Then Lemma
4.1
indicates that the problem (
CA-eq
) with
\varphi_{A}=0
admits a unique solution
g^{A}=g^{A}(x,v)
,
(x,v)\in(0,A)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}
satisfying

(5.7)

\displaystyle\mathscr{E}^{A}(e^{\hbar\sigma}g^{A})\leq C(\mathscr{A}^{A}(e^{\hbar\sigma}h)+\mathscr{B}(e^{\hbar\sigma}\varphi_{A}))\leq C\mathfrak{C}_{\flat}\,,

where
C>0
is independent of
A\geq 1
and the functional
\mathscr{E}^{A}(\cdot)
is given in (
1.36
).

We now extend
g^{A}(x,v)
as follows

(5.8)

\displaystyle\tilde{g}^{A}(x,v)=\mathbf{1}_{x\in(0,A)}g^{A}(x,v)\,,(x,v)\in(0,\infty)\times\mathbb{R}^{3}\,.

It is easy to see that
\tilde{g}^{A}(x,v)\in\mathbb{B}_{\infty}^{\hbar}
with

(5.9)

\displaystyle\|\tilde{g}^{A}\|_{\mathbb{B}_{\infty}^{\hbar}}=\mathscr{E}^{\infty}(e^{\hbar\sigma}\tilde{g}^{A})\leq C\mathfrak{C}_{\flat}\,.

Then there is a
g^{\prime}=g^{\prime}(x,v)\in\mathbb{B}_{\infty}^{\hbar}
such that

(5.10)

\displaystyle\tilde{g}^{A}(x,v)\to g^{\prime}(x,v)\quad\textrm{weakly in }\mathbb{B}_{\infty}^{\hbar}

as
A\to+\infty
(in the sense of subsequence, if necessary). Moreover,
g^{\prime}(x,v)
obeys

(5.11)

\displaystyle\|g^{\prime}\|_{\mathbb{B}_{\infty}^{\hbar}}=\mathscr{E}_{1}^{\infty}(e^{\hbar\sigma}g^{\prime})\leq C\mathfrak{C}_{\flat}\,.

For any
1\leq A_{1}<A_{2}<+\infty
, let
g^{A_{i}}(x,v)

(i=1,2)
be the solution to the problem

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} &v_{3}\partial_{x}g^{A_{i}}+\mathcal{L}g^{A_{i}}+\mathbf{D}g^{A_{i}}=h\,,\hskip 9.24994ptx\in(0,A_{i})\,,\\
&g^{A_{i}}(0,v)|_{v_{3}>0}=(1-\alpha_{*})g^{A_{i}}(0,R_{0}v)+\alpha_{*}\tfrac{M_{w}(v)}{\sqrt{\mathfrak{M}(v)}}\int_{v_{3}^{\prime}<0}(-v_{3}^{\prime})g^{A_{i}}(0,v^{\prime})\sqrt{\mathfrak{M}(v^{\prime})}\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\,,\\
&g^{A_{i}}(A_{i},v)|_{v_{3}<0}=0\,,\end{aligned}\right.

constructed in Lemma
4.1
. Then
g^{A_{i}}(x,v)

(i=1,2)
subject to the estimates

(5.12)

\displaystyle\mathscr{E}^{A_{i}}(e^{\hbar\sigma}g^{A_{i}})\leq C\mathscr{A}^{A_{i}}(e^{\hbar\sigma}h)\leq C\mathfrak{C}_{\flat}\,.

Let
\tilde{g}^{A_{i}}
be the extension of
g^{A_{i}}
as given in (
5.8
). Then

(5.13)

\displaystyle(\tilde{g}^{A_{1}}-\tilde{g}^{A_{2}})(x,v)=\mathbf{1}_{x\in(0,A_{1})}(g^{A_{1}}-g^{A_{2}})(x,v)-\mathbf{1}_{x\in[A_{1},A_{2})}g^{A_{2}}(x,v)\,.

It is easy to see that
g^{A_{1}}-g^{A_{2}}
obeys

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} &v_{3}\partial_{x}(\tilde{g}^{A_{1}}-\tilde{g}^{A_{2}})+\mathcal{L}(\tilde{g}^{A_{1}}-\tilde{g}^{A_{2}})=0\,,\ 0<x<A_{1}\,,\\
&(\tilde{g}^{A_{1}}-\tilde{g}^{A_{2}})(0,v)|_{v_{3}>0}=(1-\alpha_{*})(\tilde{g}^{A_{1}}-\tilde{g}^{A_{2}})(0,R_{0}v)+\alpha_{*}\tfrac{M_{w}(v)}{\sqrt{\mathfrak{M}(v)}}\int_{v_{3}^{\prime}<0}(-v_{3}^{\prime})(\tilde{g}^{A_{1}}-\tilde{g}^{A_{2}})(0,v^{\prime})\sqrt{\mathfrak{M}(v^{\prime})}\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\,,\\
&(\tilde{g}^{A_{1}}-\tilde{g}^{A_{2}})(A_{1},v)|_{v_{3}<0}=-g^{A_{2}}(A_{1},v)\,.\end{aligned}\right.

It thereby follows from the similar arguments in Lemma
4.1
that for any fixed
\hbar^{\prime}\in(0,\hbar)
,

(5.14)

\displaystyle\mathscr{E}^{A_{1}}(e^{\hbar^{\prime}\sigma}(g^{A_{1}}-g^{A_{2}}))\leq C\mathscr{B}(e^{\hbar^{\prime}\sigma(A_{1},v)}(-g^{A_{2}}(A_{1},v)))\leq C\mathscr{B}(e^{\hbar^{\prime}\sigma(A_{1},v)}g^{A_{2}}(A_{1},v))\,.

Lemma
2.1
shows that
\sigma(A_{1},v)\geq c(\delta A_{1}+l)^{\frac{2}{3-\gamma}}
uniformly in
v\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
, which indicates that

(5.15)

\displaystyle\sup_{v\in\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{-(\hbar-\hbar^{\prime})\sigma(A_{1},v)}\leq e^{-c(\hbar-\hbar^{\prime})(\delta A_{1}+l)^{\frac{2}{3-\gamma}}}\,.

It will be frequently used later. In the following, we focus on controlling the quantity
\mathscr{B}(e^{\hbar^{\prime}\sigma(A_{1},v)}g^{A_{2}}(A_{1},v))
. Recalling (
1.42
), one has

(5.16)

\displaystyle\mathscr{B}(\cdot)=\mathscr{B}_{\infty}(\cdot)+\mathscr{B}_{\mathtt{cro}}(\cdot)+\big[\mathscr{B}_{\mathtt{NBE}}(\cdot)\big]^{\frac{1}{2}}+\big[\mathscr{B}_{2}(\cdot)\big]^{\frac{1}{2}}\,.

Case 1. Control of
\mathscr{B}_{\infty}(e^{\hbar^{\prime}\sigma(A_{1},v)}g^{A_{2}}(A_{1},v))
.

By the definition of
\mathscr{B}_{\infty}(\cdot)
in (
1.44
), one has

\displaystyle\mathscr{B}_{\infty}(e^{\hbar^{\prime}\sigma(A_{1},v)}g^{A_{2}}(A_{1},v))=\|e^{\hbar^{\prime}\sigma(A_{1},v)}\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}(A_{1},v)w_{\beta,\vartheta}(v)g^{A_{2}}(A_{1},v)\|_{L^{\infty}_{v}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\sup_{v\in\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{-(\hbar-\hbar^{\prime})\sigma(A_{1},v)}\|e^{\hbar\sigma(A_{1},v)}\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}(A_{1},v)w_{\beta,\vartheta}(v)g^{A_{2}}(A_{1},v)\|_{L^{\infty}_{v}}

(5.17)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle e^{-c(\hbar-\hbar^{\prime})(\delta A_{1}+l)^{\frac{2}{3-\gamma}}}[\![e^{\hbar\sigma}g^{A_{2}}]\!]_{A_{2};m,\beta,\vartheta}=e^{-c(\hbar-\hbar^{\prime})(\delta A_{1}+l)^{\frac{2}{3-\gamma}}}\mathscr{E}_{\infty}^{A_{2}}(e^{\hbar\sigma}g^{A_{2}})\,,

where the inequality (
5.15
) is utilized, and
\mathscr{E}_{\infty}^{A_{2}}(\cdot)
is defined in (
1.37
).

Case 2. Control of
\big[\mathscr{B}_{2}(e^{\hbar^{\prime}\sigma(A_{1},v)}g^{A_{2}}(A_{1},v))\big]^{\frac{1}{2}}
.

By the definition of
\mathscr{B}_{2}(\cdot)
in (
1.44
), one has

\displaystyle\big[\mathscr{B}_{2}(e^{\hbar^{\prime}\sigma(A_{1},v)}g^{A_{2}}(A_{1},v))\big]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\||v_{3}|^{\frac{1}{2}}w_{\beta,\vartheta}e^{\hbar^{\prime}\sigma(A_{1},v)}g^{A_{2}}(A_{1},v)\|_{L^{2}_{\Sigma_{-}^{A_{1}}}}=\Big(\int_{v_{3}>0}|v_{3}|w_{\beta,\vartheta}^{2}e^{2\hbar^{\prime}\sigma(A_{1},v)}|g^{A_{2}}(A_{1},v)|^{2}\mathrm{d}v\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big(\int_{v_{3}>0}\Phi(A_{1},v)|z_{\alpha^{\prime}}(v)\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}(A_{1},v)w_{-\gamma,\vartheta}(v)e^{\hbar\sigma(A_{1},v)}g^{A_{2}}(A_{1},v)|^{2}\mathrm{d}v\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\,,

where
m\geq 1
,
\alpha^{\prime}\in(\tfrac{1}{2}-\mu_{\gamma},\frac{1}{2})
is given in Lemma
4.2
below, and

\displaystyle\Phi(A_{1},v)=|v_{3}|z_{-\alpha^{\prime}}^{2}(v)(1+|v|)^{2\beta+2\gamma}\sigma_{x}^{-m}(A_{1},v)e^{-2(\hbar-\hbar^{\prime})\sigma(A_{1},v)}\,.

Lemma
2.1
implies that for
m\geq 1
,

(5.18)

\displaystyle\sigma_{x}^{-m}(A_{1},v)\leq C(\delta A_{1}+l)^{\frac{m(1-\gamma)}{3-\gamma}}(1+|v-\mathfrak{u}|)^{m(1-\gamma)}\,.

It is also easy to verify that
\sigma(A_{1},v)\geq c^{\prime}(1+|v-\mathfrak{u}|^{2})
for some constant
c^{\prime}>0
, which means that

(5.19)

\displaystyle e^{-(\hbar-\hbar^{\prime})\sigma(A_{1},v)}\leq e^{-c^{\prime}(\hbar-\hbar^{\prime})}e^{-c^{\prime}(\hbar-\hbar^{\prime})|v-\mathfrak{u}|^{2}}\leq e^{-c^{\prime}(\hbar-\hbar^{\prime})|v-\mathfrak{u}|^{2}}\,.

Then the bounds (
5.15
), (
5.18
) and (
5.19
) show that

\displaystyle\Phi(A_{1},v)\leq

\displaystyle C|v_{3}|z_{-\alpha^{\prime}}^{2}(v)(1+|v|)^{2\beta+2\gamma+m(1-\gamma)}e^{-c^{\prime}(\hbar-\hbar^{\prime})|v-\mathfrak{u}|^{2}}(\delta A_{1}+l)^{\frac{m(1-\gamma)}{3-\gamma}}e^{-c(\hbar-\hbar^{\prime})(\delta A_{1}+l)^{\frac{2}{3-\gamma}}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle Ce^{-\frac{c}{2}(\hbar-\hbar^{\prime})(\delta A_{1}+l)^{\frac{2}{3-\gamma}}}

uniformly in
v\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
. It therefore follows that

\displaystyle\big[\mathscr{B}_{2}(e^{\hbar^{\prime}\sigma(A_{1},v)}g^{A_{2}}(A_{1},v))\big]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle Ce^{-\frac{c}{4}(\hbar-\hbar^{\prime})(\delta A_{1}+l)^{\frac{2}{3-\gamma}}}\Big(\int_{v_{3}>0}|z_{\alpha^{\prime}}(v)\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}(A_{1},v)w_{-\gamma,\vartheta}(v)e^{\hbar\sigma(A_{1},v)}g^{A_{2}}(A_{1},v)|^{2}\mathrm{d}v\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle Ce^{-\frac{c}{4}(\hbar-\hbar^{\prime})(\delta A_{1}+l)^{\frac{2}{3-\gamma}}}\|z_{\alpha^{\prime}}\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}w_{-\gamma,\vartheta}e^{\hbar\sigma}g^{A_{2}}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}\,.

From Lemma
4.3
below, it is derived that

\displaystyle\|z_{\alpha^{\prime}}\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}w_{-\gamma,\vartheta}e^{\hbar\sigma}g^{A_{2}}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\|\nu^{-\frac{1}{2}}z_{-\alpha}\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}z_{1}w_{-\gamma+\beta_{\gamma},\vartheta}\partial_{x}(e^{\hbar\sigma}g^{A_{2}})\|_{A_{2}}+C\|\nu^{\frac{1}{2}}z_{-\alpha}\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}w_{-\gamma+\beta_{\gamma},\vartheta}e^{\hbar\sigma}g^{A_{2}}\|_{A_{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle C\mathscr{E}_{\mathtt{cro}}^{A_{2}}(e^{\hbar\sigma}g^{A_{2}})\,,

where the functional
\mathscr{E}_{\mathtt{cro}}^{A_{2}}(\cdot)
is defined in (
1.38
). Then there hold

(5.20)

\displaystyle\big[\mathscr{B}_{2}(e^{\hbar^{\prime}\sigma(A_{1},v)}g^{A_{2}}(A_{1},v))\big]^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq Ce^{-\frac{c}{4}(\hbar-\hbar^{\prime})(\delta A_{1}+l)^{\frac{2}{3-\gamma}}}\mathscr{E}_{\mathtt{cro}}^{A_{2}}(e^{\hbar\sigma}g^{A_{2}})\,.

Case 3. Control of
\mathscr{B}_{\mathtt{cro}}(e^{\hbar^{\prime}\sigma(A_{1},v)}g^{A_{2}}(A_{1},v))+\big[\mathscr{B}_{\mathtt{NBE}}(e^{\hbar^{\prime}\sigma(A_{1},v)}g^{A_{2}}(A_{1},v))\big]^{\frac{1}{2}}
.

By employing the similar arguments of controlling the quantity
\big[\mathscr{B}_{2}(e^{\hbar^{\prime}\sigma(A_{1},v)}g^{A_{2}}(A_{1},v))\big]^{\frac{1}{2}}
, one obtains

(5.21)

\displaystyle\mathscr{B}_{\mathtt{cro}}(e^{\hbar^{\prime}\sigma(A_{1},v)}g^{A_{2}}(A_{1},v))+\big[\mathscr{B}_{\mathtt{NBE}}(e^{\hbar^{\prime}\sigma(A_{1},v)}g^{A_{2}}(A_{1},v))\big]^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle Ce^{-\frac{c}{4}(\hbar-\hbar^{\prime})(\delta A_{1}+l)^{\frac{2}{3-\gamma}}}\|z_{\alpha^{\prime}}\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}w_{-\gamma,\vartheta}e^{\hbar\sigma}g^{A_{2}}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle Ce^{-\frac{c}{4}(\hbar-\hbar^{\prime})(\delta A_{1}+l)^{\frac{2}{3-\gamma}}}\mathscr{E}_{\mathtt{cro}}^{A_{2}}(e^{\hbar\sigma}g^{A_{2}})\,.

As a consequence, the relations (
5.16
), (
5.17
), (
5.20
) and (
5.21
) reduce to

(5.22)

\displaystyle\mathscr{B}(e^{\hbar^{\prime}\sigma(A_{1},v)}g^{A_{2}}(A_{1},v))\leq Ce^{-\frac{c}{4}(\hbar-\hbar^{\prime})(\delta A_{1}+l)^{\frac{2}{3-\gamma}}}(\mathscr{E}_{\infty}^{A_{2}}(e^{\hbar\sigma}g^{A_{2}})+\mathscr{E}_{\mathtt{cro}}^{A_{2}}(e^{\hbar\sigma}g^{A_{2}}))

\displaystyle\leq Ce^{-\frac{c}{4}(\hbar-\hbar^{\prime})(\delta A_{1}+l)^{\frac{2}{3-\gamma}}}\mathscr{E}^{A_{2}}(e^{\hbar\sigma}g^{A_{2}})\leq C\mathfrak{C}_{\flat}e^{-\frac{c}{4}(\hbar-\hbar^{\prime})(\delta A_{1}+l)^{\frac{2}{3-\gamma}}}\,,

where the last inequality is derived from the bound (
5.12
). Collecting the estimates (
5.14
) and (
5.22
), one gains

(5.23)

\displaystyle\mathscr{E}^{\infty}(\mathbf{1}_{x\in(0,A_{1})}e^{\hbar^{\prime}\sigma}(g^{A_{1}}-g^{A_{2}}))=\mathscr{E}^{A_{1}}(e^{\hbar^{\prime}\sigma}(g^{A_{1}}-g^{A_{2}}))\leq C\mathfrak{C}_{\flat}e^{-\frac{c}{4}(\hbar-\hbar^{\prime})(\delta A_{1}+l)^{\frac{2}{3-\gamma}}}

for any
\hbar^{\prime}\in(0,\hbar)
.

Note that

\displaystyle\mathscr{E}^{\infty}(\mathbf{1}_{x\in[A_{1},A_{2})}e^{\hbar^{\prime}\sigma}g^{A_{2}})=\mathscr{E}^{A_{2}}(\mathbf{1}_{x\in[A_{1},A_{2})}e^{\hbar^{\prime}\sigma}g^{A_{2}})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathscr{E}^{A_{2}}(e^{\hbar\sigma}g^{A_{2}})\sup_{x>0,v\in\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathbf{1}_{x\in[A_{1},A_{2})}e^{-(\hbar-\hbar^{\prime})\sigma(x,v)}\leq C\mathfrak{C}_{\flat}\sup_{x>0,v\in\mathbb{R}^{3}}\mathbf{1}_{x\in[A_{1},A_{2})}e^{-(\hbar-\hbar^{\prime})\sigma(x,v)}\,,

where the last inequality is deduced from (
5.12
). Since
\sigma(x,v)\geq c(\delta x+l)^{\frac{2}{3-\gamma}}
by Lemma
2.1
, one has
\mathbf{1}_{x\in[A_{1},A_{2})}e^{-(\hbar-\hbar^{\prime})\sigma(x,v)}\leq\mathbf{1}_{x\in[A_{1},A_{2})}e^{-c(\hbar-\hbar^{\prime})(\delta x+l)^{\frac{2}{3-\gamma}}}\leq e^{-c(\hbar-\hbar^{\prime})(\delta A_{1}+l)^{\frac{2}{3-\gamma}}}
uniformly in
x
and
v
. Then

(5.24)

\displaystyle\mathscr{E}^{\infty}(\mathbf{1}_{x\in[A_{1},A_{2})}e^{\hbar^{\prime}\sigma}g^{A_{2}})\leq C\mathfrak{C}_{\flat}e^{-c(\hbar-\hbar^{\prime})(\delta A_{1}+l)^{\frac{2}{3-\gamma}}}\,.

Therefore, the decomposition (
5.13
) and the estimates (
5.23
)-(
5.24
) imply that for any fixed
1\leq A_{1}<A_{2}<\infty
and
\hbar^{\prime}\in(0,\hbar)
,

(5.25)

\displaystyle\mathscr{E}^{\infty}(e^{\hbar^{\prime}\sigma}(\tilde{g}^{A_{1}}-\tilde{g}^{A_{2}}))\leq

\displaystyle\mathscr{E}^{\infty}(\mathbf{1}_{x\in(0,A_{1})}e^{\hbar^{\prime}\sigma}(g^{A_{1}}-g^{A_{2}}))+\mathscr{E}^{\infty}(\mathbf{1}_{x\in[A_{1},A_{2})}e^{\hbar^{\prime}\sigma}g^{A_{2}})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\mathfrak{C}_{\flat}e^{-\frac{c}{4}(\hbar-\hbar^{\prime})(\delta A_{1}+l)^{\frac{2}{3-\gamma}}}\to 0

as
A_{1}\to+\infty
. Moreover, by (
5.9
),

(5.26)

\displaystyle\mathscr{E}^{\infty}(e^{\hbar^{\prime}\sigma}\tilde{g}^{A})\leq\|\tilde{g}^{A}\|_{\mathbb{B}_{\infty}^{\hbar}}=\mathscr{E}^{\infty}(e^{\hbar\sigma}\tilde{g}^{A})\leq C\mathfrak{C}_{\flat}

for any
\hbar^{\prime}\in(0,\hbar)
.

Denote by
\mathbb{B}_{\infty}^{\hbar^{\prime}}
be a Banach space defined as the same way of
\mathbb{B}_{\infty}^{\hbar}
in (
5.1
)-(
5.2
). It is easy to see that
\mathbb{B}_{\infty}^{\hbar}\subseteq\mathbb{B}_{\infty}^{\hbar^{\prime}}
, i.e.,
\|g\|_{\mathbb{B}_{\infty}^{\hbar^{\prime}}}\leq\|g\|_{\mathbb{B}_{\infty}^{\hbar}}
. Then the estimates (
5.25
) and (
5.26
) tell us that
\{\tilde{g}^{A}\}_{A>1}
is a bounded Cauchy sequence in
\mathbb{B}_{\infty}^{\hbar^{\prime}}
. As a result, there is a unique
g=g(x,v)\in\mathbb{B}_{\infty}^{\hbar^{\prime}}
such that

(5.27)

\displaystyle\tilde{g}^{A}(x,v)\to g(x,v)\quad\textrm{strongly in }\mathbb{B}_{\infty}^{\hbar^{\prime}}

as
A\to+\infty
. Combining with the convergences (
5.10
) and (
5.27
), the uniqueness of the limit shows that
g(x,v)=g^{\prime}(x,v)
. Moreover, the bound (
5.11
) infers that
g(x,v)
satisfies the bound

(5.28)

\displaystyle\|g\|_{\mathbb{B}_{\infty}^{\hbar}}\leq C\mathfrak{C}_{\flat}=C\mathscr{A}^{\infty}(e^{\hbar\sigma}h)\,.

Taking limit
A\to+\infty
in the mild solution form of (
CA-eq
) with
\varphi_{A}=0
, i.e.,

\displaystyle v_{3}\partial_{x}\tilde{g}^{A}+\mathcal{L}\tilde{g}^{A}+\mathbf{D}\tilde{g}^{A}=h\,,\ 0<x<A\,,

\displaystyle\tilde{g}^{A}(0,v)|_{v_{3}>0}=(1-\alpha_{*})\tilde{g}^{A}(0,R_{0}v)+\alpha_{*}\tfrac{M_{w}(v)}{\sqrt{\mathfrak{M}(v)}}\int_{v_{3}^{\prime}<0}(-v_{3}^{\prime})\tilde{g}^{A}(0,v^{\prime})\sqrt{\mathfrak{M}(v^{\prime})}\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\,,

we easily know that the limit
g(x,v)
of
\tilde{g}^{A}(x,v)
subjects to

(5.29)

\displaystyle v_{3}\partial_{x}g+\mathcal{L}g+\mathbf{D}g=h\,,x>0\,,

\displaystyle g(0,v)|_{v_{3}>0}=(1-\alpha_{*})g(0,R_{0}v)+\alpha_{*}\tfrac{M_{w}(v)}{\sqrt{\mathfrak{M}(v)}}\int_{v_{3}^{\prime}<0}(-v_{3}^{\prime})g(0,v^{\prime})\sqrt{\mathfrak{M}(v^{\prime})}\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}

in the sense of mild solution. Furthermore, the bounds (
5.18
) and (
5.28
) imply

(5.30)

\displaystyle|g(x,v)|\leq

\displaystyle e^{-\hbar\sigma(x,v)}\sigma_{x}^{-\frac{m}{2}}(x,v)w^{-1}_{\beta,\vartheta}(v)\|g\|_{\mathbb{B}_{\infty}^{\hbar}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\mathfrak{C}_{\flat}e^{-c\hbar(\delta x+l)^{\frac{2}{3-\gamma}}}\cdot C(\delta x+l)^{\frac{m(1-\gamma)}{2(3-\gamma)}}(1+|v-\mathfrak{u}|)^{-m(1-\gamma)}w^{-1}_{\beta,\vartheta}(v)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C^{\prime}\mathfrak{C}_{\flat}e^{-\frac{c}{2}\hbar(\delta x+l)^{\frac{2}{3-\gamma}}}\to 0

as
x\to+\infty
. Hence,
\lim_{x\to+\infty}g(x,v)=0
. Therefore, the function
g(x,v)
solves the problem (
1.66
).

At the end, we justify the uniqueness. Assume that
g_{i}(x,v)

(i=1,2)
are both the solutions to the problem (
1.66
) enjoying the bound (
5.28
). Then, for any fixed
A\geq 1
, the difference
g_{1}-g_{2}
obeys

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{aligned} &v_{3}\partial_{x}(g_{1}-g_{2})+\mathcal{L}(g_{1}-g_{2})+\mathbf{D}(g_{1}-g_{2})=0\,,\ 0<x<A\,,\\
&(g_{1}-g_{2})(0,v)|_{v_{3}>0}=(1-\alpha_{*})(g_{1}-g_{2})(0,R_{0}v)+\alpha_{*}\tfrac{M_{w}(v)}{\sqrt{\mathfrak{M}(v)}}\int_{v_{3}^{\prime}<0}(-v_{3}^{\prime})(g_{1}-g_{2})(0,v^{\prime})\sqrt{\mathfrak{M}(v^{\prime})}\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\,,\\
&(g_{1}-g_{2})(A,v)|_{v_{3}<0}=\phi_{A}(v):=\mathbf{1}_{v_{3}<0}(g_{1}-g_{2})(A,v)\,.\end{aligned}\right.

Following the similar arguments in Lemma
4.1
, one knows that

\displaystyle\mathscr{E}^{A}(e^{\hbar^{\prime}\sigma}(g_{1}-g_{2}))\leq C\mathscr{B}(e^{\hbar^{\prime}\sigma(A,v)}\phi_{A}(v))=C\mathscr{B}(e^{\hbar^{\prime}\sigma(A,v)}\mathbf{1}_{v_{3}<0}(g_{1}-g_{2})(A,v))

for any fixed
\hbar^{\prime}\in(0,\hbar)
. By employing the same arguments of (
5.22
), one has

\displaystyle\mathscr{B}(e^{\hbar^{\prime}\sigma(A,v)}\mathbf{1}_{v_{3}<0}(g_{1}-g_{2})(A,v))\leq

\displaystyle Ce^{-\frac{c}{4}(\hbar-\hbar^{\prime})(\delta A+l)^{\frac{2}{3-\gamma}}}\sum_{i=1,2}\mathscr{E}_{1}^{A}(e^{\hbar\sigma}g_{i})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\mathscr{A}^{\infty}(e^{\hbar\sigma}h)e^{-\frac{c}{4}(\hbar-\hbar^{\prime})(\delta A+l)^{\frac{2}{3-\gamma}}}\,,

where the last inequality is derived from (
5.28
). As a result,

\displaystyle\mathscr{E}^{\infty}(e^{\hbar^{\prime}\sigma}(g_{1}-g_{2}))=\lim_{A\to+\infty}\mathscr{E}^{A}(e^{\hbar^{\prime}\sigma}(g_{1}-g_{2}))\leq\lim_{A\to+\infty}\big[C\mathscr{A}^{\infty}(e^{\hbar\sigma}h)e^{-\frac{c}{4}(\hbar-\hbar^{\prime})(\delta A+l)^{\frac{2}{3-\gamma}}}\big]=0\,,

which means that
g_{1}=g_{2}
. Therefore, the uniqueness holds. The proof of Lemma
5.1
is finished.
∎

5.2.
Existence and uniqueness of (
KL
): Proof of Theorem
1.1

In this subsection, we mainly prove the existence and uniqueness of the linear problem (
KL
), i.e., justify Theorem
1.1
. As stated in Subsection
1.5
before, the problem (
KL
) can be decomposed as the problems (
1.59
) and (
1.60
). Because (
1.59
) admits a unique explicit solution (
1.62
), we only need to prove the existence and uniqueness of the problem (
1.60
) by applying the solution to (
1.66
) constructed in Lemma
5.1
. More precisely, by the equivalence between the problems (
1.66
) and (
KLd
) stated in Subsection
1.5
before, Lemma
5.1
actually implies the existence and uniqueness of the equation (
KLd
). Then by equivalently characterizing the structure of the vanishing sources set
\mathbb{VSS}_{\alpha_{*}}
defined in (
1.24
), one can remove the artificial damping
\mathbf{D}(\mathcal{I}-\mathcal{P}^{0})f
in (
KLd
), so that the problem (
KLd
) can be uniquely solved. Here the operator
\mathcal{P}^{0}
is given in (
1.20
).

Proof of Theorem
1.1
.

Lemma
5.1
implies that the problem (
1.66
) admits a unique mild solution
g(x,v)
with

\displaystyle\mathscr{E}^{\infty}(e^{\hbar\sigma}g)\lesssim\mathscr{A}^{\infty}(e^{\hbar\sigma}h)\,.

As the formal illustrations in Subsection
1.5
, the problems (
1.66
) and (
KLd
) are equivalent under the relations (
1.65
), i.e.,

\displaystyle g(x,v)=

\displaystyle f_{*}(x,v)-\Upsilon(x)\widetilde{f}_{b}(v)\,,

\displaystyle h(x,v)=

\displaystyle(\mathbb{I}-\mathbb{P})S(x,v)-v_{3}\partial_{x}\Upsilon(x)\widetilde{f}_{b}(v)-\Upsilon(x)(\mathcal{L}+\mathbf{D})\widetilde{f}_{b}(v)\,.

Then the problem (
KLd
) admits a unique solution
f_{*}(x,v)
enjoying

\displaystyle\mathscr{E}^{\infty}(e^{\hbar\sigma}

\displaystyle f_{*})\lesssim\mathscr{E}^{\infty}(e^{\hbar\sigma}g)+\mathscr{E}^{\infty}(e^{\hbar\sigma}\Upsilon(x)\widetilde{f}_{b}(v))\lesssim\mathscr{A}^{\infty}(e^{\hbar\sigma}h)+\mathscr{E}^{\infty}(e^{\hbar\sigma}\Upsilon(x)\widetilde{f}_{b}(v))

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\mathscr{A}^{\infty}(e^{\hbar\sigma}(\mathbb{I}-\mathbb{P})S)+\mathscr{A}^{\infty}(e^{\hbar\sigma}v_{3}\Upsilon^{\prime}(x)\widetilde{f}_{b}(v))+\mathscr{A}^{\infty}(e^{\hbar\sigma}\Upsilon(x)(\mathcal{L}+\mathbf{D})\widetilde{f}_{b}(v))+\mathscr{E}^{\infty}(e^{\hbar\sigma}\Upsilon(x)\widetilde{f}_{b}(v))\,.

By using the properties of
K
and
\mathbf{D}
in Subsection
2.3
and Subsection
2.4
, respectively, one can obtain that

\displaystyle\mathscr{A}^{\infty}(e^{\hbar\sigma}\Upsilon(x)(\mathcal{L}+\mathbf{D})\widetilde{f}_{b}(v))\lesssim\mathscr{A}^{\infty}(e^{\hbar\sigma}\Upsilon(x)\nu\widetilde{f}_{b}(v))\,.

Note that
\Upsilon(x)=\Upsilon^{\prime}(x)=0
for
x\geq 2
. Then one easily has

\displaystyle e^{\hbar\sigma}\Upsilon(x)+e^{\hbar\sigma}\Upsilon^{\prime}(x)\lesssim e^{3\hbar|v-\mathfrak{u}|^{2}}\,.

As a result, together with the definitions of
\mathscr{A}^{\infty}(\cdot)
and
\mathscr{E}^{\infty}(\cdot)
in (
1.42
) and (
1.36
), respectively, one derives that

\displaystyle\mathscr{A}^{\infty}(e^{\hbar\sigma}v_{3}\Upsilon^{\prime}(x)\widetilde{f}_{b}(v))+\mathscr{A}^{\infty}(e^{\hbar\sigma}v_{3}\Upsilon^{\prime}(x)\widetilde{f}_{b}(v))

\displaystyle+\mathscr{A}^{\infty}(e^{\hbar\sigma}\Upsilon(x)\nu\widetilde{f}_{b}(v))+\mathscr{E}^{\infty}(e^{\hbar\sigma}\Upsilon(x)\widetilde{f}_{b}(v))

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{f}_{b}\|_{\mathfrak{N}}+\sum_{\mathbf{c}\in\{0,\beta_{\gamma}+\frac{1}{2},2\beta_{\gamma}+1\}}\big(\|\mathcal{P}w_{1+\mathbf{c},3\hbar}\widetilde{f}_{b}\|_{L^{2}_{v}}+\|\nu^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{P}^{\perp}w_{1+\mathbf{c},3\hbar}\widetilde{f}_{b}\|_{L^{2}_{v}}\big)\,.

It is easy to see that for
\beta\geq 3(\beta_{\gamma}+\frac{1}{2})
,

\displaystyle\sum_{\mathbf{c}\in\{0,\beta_{\gamma}+\frac{1}{2},2\beta_{\gamma}+1\}}\big(\|\mathcal{P}w_{1+\mathbf{c},3\hbar}\widetilde{f}_{b}\|_{L^{2}_{v}}+\|\nu^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{P}^{\perp}w_{1+\mathbf{c},3\hbar}\widetilde{f}_{b}\|_{L^{2}_{v}}\big)\lesssim\|\widetilde{f}_{b}\|_{\mathfrak{N}}\,,

where the norm
\|\widetilde{f}_{b}\|_{\mathfrak{N}}
is defined in (
1.45
). Collecting the above estimates, one knows that the mild solution
f_{*}
to the problem (
KLd
) enjoys the bound

(5.31)

\displaystyle\mathscr{E}^{\infty}(e^{\hbar\sigma}f_{*})\lesssim\mathscr{A}^{\infty}(e^{\hbar\sigma}(\mathbb{I}-\mathbb{P})S)+\|\widetilde{f}_{b}\|_{\mathfrak{N}}\,.

Define

(5.32)
