\displaystyle f(x,v)=\mathcal{P}^{0}f(x,v)+f_{*}(x,v)

where
\mathcal{P}^{0}f(x,v)
is given in (
1.62
). Then
f(x,v)
is the unique solution to the damped problem (
1.50
). By the explicit expression of
\mathcal{P}^{0}f
in (
1.62
), one has

(5.33)

\displaystyle e^{\hbar\sigma(x,v)}\mathcal{P}^{0}f(x,v)=-e^{\hbar\sigma(x,v)}\int_{x}^{+\infty}e^{-\hbar\sigma(x^{\prime},v)}\tfrac{1}{v_{3}}e^{\hbar\sigma(x^{\prime},v)}\mathbb{P}S(x^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}\,.

If
(x,v)
satisfies
\delta x+l\leq 2(1+|v-\mathfrak{u}|)^{3-\gamma}
, by the definition of
\sigma(x,v)
in (
1.26
), the weight
e^{\hbar\sigma(x,v)}
can be bounded by
Ce^{c\hbar|v-\mathfrak{u}|^{2}}
. Due to the smallness of
\hbar>0
, the weight
e^{\hbar\sigma(x,v)}
can be absorbed by the exponential decay factors on
v
variable contained in
\tfrac{1}{v_{3}}\mathbb{P}S(x^{\prime},v)
. Then

\displaystyle|\mathbf{1}_{\delta x+l\leq 2(1+|v-\mathfrak{u}|)^{3-\gamma}}e^{\hbar\sigma(x,v)}\mathcal{P}^{0}f(x,v)|\lesssim\int_{x}^{+\infty}e^{-c(\delta x^{\prime}+l)^{\frac{2}{3-\gamma}}}\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}[\![e^{\hbar\sigma}S]\!]_{\infty;m,\beta_{*},\vartheta}\lesssim[\![e^{\hbar\sigma}S]\!]_{\infty;m,\beta_{*},\vartheta}

If
\delta x+l\geq 2(1+|v-\mathfrak{u}|)^{3-\gamma}
, one has
e^{\hbar\sigma(x,v)}=e^{5(\delta x+l)^{\frac{2}{3-\gamma}}}
and
e^{\hbar\sigma(x^{\prime},v)}=e^{5(\delta x^{\prime}+l)^{\frac{2}{3-\gamma}}}
for
x^{\prime}\geq x
. However, if we still dominate it by the quantity
[\![e^{\hbar\sigma}S]\!]_{\infty;m,\beta_{*},\vartheta}
, one then has

\displaystyle|\mathbf{1}_{\delta x+l\geq 2(1+|v-\mathfrak{u}|)^{3-\gamma}}e^{\hbar\sigma(x,v)}\mathcal{P}^{0}f(x,v)|\lesssim e^{5(\delta x+l)^{\frac{2}{3-\gamma}}}\int_{x}^{+\infty}e^{-5(\delta x^{\prime}+l)^{\frac{2}{3-\gamma}}}\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}[\![e^{\hbar\sigma}S]\!]_{\infty;m,\beta_{*},\vartheta}\,.

A direct analysis implies
\lim_{x\to+\infty}\tfrac{e^{5(\delta x+l)^{\frac{2}{3-\gamma}}}\int_{x}^{+\infty}e^{-5(\delta x^{\prime}+l)^{\frac{2}{3-\gamma}}}\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}}{\frac{3-\gamma}{10\delta}(\delta x+l)^{\frac{1-\gamma}{3-\gamma}}}=1
, which means that the function
e^{5(\delta x+l)^{\frac{2}{3-\gamma}}}\int_{x}^{+\infty}e^{-5(\delta x^{\prime}+l)^{\frac{2}{3-\gamma}}}\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}
grow with the rate
\frac{3-\gamma}{10\delta}(\delta x+l)^{\frac{1-\gamma}{3-\gamma}}
as
x\to+\infty
. In other words, the quantity
|\mathbf{1}_{\delta x+l\geq 2(1+|v-\mathfrak{u}|)^{3-\gamma}}e^{\hbar\sigma(x,v)}\mathcal{P}^{0}f(x,v)|
can only be bounded by

\displaystyle|\mathbf{1}_{\delta x+l\geq 2(1+|v-\mathfrak{u}|)^{3-\gamma}}e^{\hbar\sigma(x,v)}\mathcal{P}^{0}f(x,v)|\lesssim[\![(\delta x+l)^{\frac{1-\gamma}{3-\gamma}}e^{\hbar\sigma}\mathbb{P}S]\!]_{\infty;m,\beta_{*},\vartheta}\,.

In summary, one has

\displaystyle\mathscr{E}^{\infty}_{\infty}(e^{\hbar\sigma}\mathcal{P}^{0}f)=\mathscr{E}^{\infty}_{\infty}(e^{\hbar\sigma}\int_{x}^{+\infty}\tfrac{1}{v_{3}}\mathbb{P}S(x^{\prime},v)\mathrm{d}x^{\prime})\lesssim[\![(\delta x+l)^{\frac{1-\gamma}{3-\gamma}}e^{\hbar\sigma}\mathbb{P}S]\!]_{\infty;m,\beta_{*},\vartheta}\,.

From the similar arguments above, one can also derive that

\displaystyle\mathscr{E}_{X}^{\infty}(e^{\hbar\sigma}\mathcal{P}^{0}f)\lesssim\mathscr{E}_{X}^{\infty}((\delta x+l)^{\frac{1-\gamma}{3-\gamma}}e^{\hbar\sigma}S)

for
X=\mathtt{cro},\mathtt{NBE}
and
2
. Recalling the definition of
\mathscr{E}^{\infty}(\cdot)
in (
1.36
), one gains

(5.34)

\displaystyle\mathscr{E}^{\infty}(e^{\hbar\sigma}\mathcal{P}^{0}f)\lesssim\mathscr{E}^{\infty}((\delta x+l)^{\frac{1-\gamma}{3-\gamma}}e^{\hbar\sigma}S)\,.

Moreover, together with the similar arguments in (
5.34
), it also can be derived from the definition of
\widetilde{f}_{b}
in (
1.61
) that

(5.35)

\displaystyle\|\widetilde{f}_{b}\|_{\mathfrak{N}}\leq\|f_{b}\|_{\mathfrak{N}}+\mathscr{E}^{\infty}((\delta x+l)^{\frac{1-\gamma}{3-\gamma}}e^{\hbar\sigma}S)\,.

Note that by the definition of
\mathscr{A}^{\infty}(e^{\hbar\sigma}(\mathbb{I}-\mathbb{P})S)
in (
1.42
),

(5.36)

\displaystyle\mathscr{A}^{\infty}(e^{\hbar\sigma}(\mathbb{I}-\mathbb{P})S)\lesssim\mathscr{E}^{\infty}(e^{\hbar\sigma}(\mathbb{I}-\mathbb{P})S)\lesssim\mathscr{E}^{\infty}((\delta x+l)^{\frac{1-\gamma}{3-\gamma}}e^{\hbar\sigma}S)\,.

Therefore, the relations (
5.31
)-(
5.32
)-(
5.34
)-(
5.35
)-(
5.36
) indicate that the unique solution
f(x,v)
to (
KL
) enjoys the bound

\displaystyle\mathscr{E}^{\infty}(e^{\hbar\sigma}f)\lesssim\mathscr{E}^{\infty}((\delta x+l)^{\frac{1-\gamma}{3-\gamma}}e^{\hbar\sigma}S)+\|{f}_{b}\|_{\mathfrak{N}}\,.

Namely, if the source term
(S,f_{b})\in\mathfrak{X}_{\gamma}^{\infty}
(defined in (
1.47
)), the solution operator
\mathbb{I}_{\gamma}
introduced in Remark
1.3
is well-defined.

Next we prove the existence of (
KL
). Note that the solution
f_{*}
to (
KLd
) is exactly a solution to (
1.60
) if and only if
\mathbf{D}f_{*}(x,v)=0
for all
x\geq 0
and
v\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
. Hence
\mathcal{P}^{+}v_{3}f_{*}=0
and
\mathcal{P}^{0}f_{*}=0
.

Now we remove the artificial damping
\mathbf{D}f_{*}
in (
KLd
), i.e.,

{\small\left\{\begin{aligned} &v_{3}\partial_{x}f_{*}+\mathcal{L}f_{*}+\mathbf{D}f_{*}=(\mathbb{I}-\mathbb{P})S\,,\\
&f_{*}(0,v)|_{v_{3}>0}=(1-\alpha_{*})f_{*}(0,R_{0}v)+\alpha_{*}\mathcal{D}_{w}f_{*}(0,v)+\widetilde{f}_{b}(v)\,,\ \lim_{x\to+\infty}f_{*}(x,v)=0\end{aligned}\right.}

in certain equivalent forms at
x=0
.

Multiplying (
KLd
) by
\psi_{3}^{*}(v)
and integrating over
v\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
, one has

\displaystyle\tfrac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}x}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}v_{3}\psi_{3}^{*}f_{*}\mathrm{d}v=-\ss_{+}(\delta x+l)^{-\frac{1-\gamma}{3-\gamma}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}v_{3}\psi_{3}^{*}f_{*}\mathrm{d}v\,,

which means that
\tfrac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}x}\Big(e^{\frac{\ss_{+}(3-\gamma)}{2\delta}(\delta x+l)^{\frac{2}{3-\gamma}}}\mathcal{P}^{+}v_{3}f_{*}\Big)=0
. Consequently,

(5.37)

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}^{+}v_{3}f_{*}(x,v)=0(\forall x\geq 0)\ \ \textrm{if and only if }\ \mathcal{P}^{+}v_{3}f_{*}(0,v)=0\,.

Multiplying by
(\widehat{\mathbb{B}}_{3},\widehat{\mathbb{A}}_{13},\widehat{\mathbb{A}}_{23})^{\top}
and integrating over
v\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
, one has

\displaystyle\tfrac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}x}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}v_{3}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\widehat{\mathbb{B}}_{3}\\
\widehat{\mathbb{A}}_{13}\\
\widehat{\mathbb{A}}_{23}\end{array}\right)f_{*}(x,v)\mathrm{d}v=-\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\widehat{\mathbb{B}}_{3}\\
\widehat{\mathbb{A}}_{13}\\
\widehat{\mathbb{A}}_{23}\end{array}\right)\mathcal{L}f_{*}(x,v)\mathrm{d}v\,,

where we have used the facts
\widehat{\mathbb{A}}_{13},\widehat{\mathbb{A}}_{23},\widehat{\mathbb{B}}_{3}\in\mathrm{Null}^{\perp}(\mathcal{L})
and
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}(\widehat{\mathbb{B}}_{3},\widehat{\mathbb{A}}_{13},\widehat{\mathbb{A}}_{23})^{\top}(\mathbb{I}-\mathbb{P})S\mathrm{d}v=0
. Here the operator
\mathbb{P}
is defined in (
1.21
). Notice that by (
1.16
)-(
1.18
)-(
1.19
),

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\widehat{\mathbb{B}}_{3}\\
\widehat{\mathbb{A}}_{13}\\
\widehat{\mathbb{A}}_{23}\end{array}\right)\mathcal{L}f_{*}(x,v)\mathrm{d}v=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}{\mathbb{B}}_{3}\\
{\mathbb{A}}_{13}\\
{\mathbb{A}}_{23}\end{array}\right)f_{*}(x,v)\mathrm{d}v=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}v_{3}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\tfrac{\sqrt{\rho}}{2\sqrt{T}}\psi_{0}^{*}\\
\sqrt{\tfrac{\rho}{T}}\psi_{1}^{*}\\
\sqrt{\tfrac{\rho}{T}}\psi_{2}^{*}\end{array}\right)f_{*}(x,v)\mathrm{d}v\,.

For notational simplicity, we define

\displaystyle\mathfrak{F}(x)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}v_{3}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\widehat{\mathbb{B}}_{3}\\
\widehat{\mathbb{A}}_{13}\\
\widehat{\mathbb{A}}_{23}\end{array}\right)f_{*}(x,v)\mathrm{d}v\,,\ \quad\mathfrak{G}(x)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}v_{3}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\psi_{0}^{*}\\
\psi_{1}^{*}\\
\psi_{2}^{*}\end{array}\right)f_{*}(x,v)\mathrm{d}v\,.

By the fact
\lim_{x\to+\infty}f_{*}(x,v)=0
one knows that
\lim_{x\to+\infty}\mathfrak{F}(x)=0
. As a result,

(5.38)

\displaystyle\tfrac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}x}\mathfrak{F}(x)=-\mathrm{diag}(\tfrac{\sqrt{\rho}}{2\sqrt{T}},\sqrt{\tfrac{\rho}{T}},\sqrt{\tfrac{\rho}{T}})\mathfrak{G}(x)\,,\ \ \lim_{x\to+\infty}\mathfrak{F}(x)=0_{3}\,,

where
0_{n}=(\underbrace{0,\cdots,0}_{n})^{\top}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
. We then multiply (
KLd
) by
(\tfrac{\sqrt{\rho}}{2\sqrt{T}}\psi_{0}^{*},\sqrt{\tfrac{\rho}{T}}\psi_{1}^{*},\sqrt{\tfrac{\rho}{T}}\psi_{2}^{*})^{\top}
and integrate the resultant over
v\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
. It therefore holds

(5.39)

\displaystyle\tfrac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}x}\big[\mathrm{diag}(\tfrac{\sqrt{\rho}}{2\sqrt{T}},\sqrt{\tfrac{\rho}{T}},\sqrt{\tfrac{\rho}{T}})\mathfrak{G}(x)\big]=-(\delta x+l)^{-\frac{1-\gamma}{3-\gamma}}\mathrm{diag}(\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2})\mathfrak{F}(x)\,,

where

\displaystyle\lambda_{0}=\tfrac{\sqrt{\rho}}{2\sqrt{T}}\ss_{0}(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\widehat{\mathbb{B}}_{3}\mathbb{B}_{3}\mathrm{d}v)^{-1}>0\,,\ \lambda_{i}=\sqrt{\tfrac{\rho}{T}}\ss_{0}(\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\widehat{\mathbb{A}}_{i3}\mathbb{A}_{i3}\mathrm{d}v)^{-1}>0\ (i=1,2)\,.

Then the equations (
5.38
) and (
5.39
) are equivalent to

(5.40)

\displaystyle\tfrac{\mathrm{d}^{2}}{\mathrm{d}x^{2}}\mathfrak{F}(x)=(\delta x+l)^{-\frac{1-\gamma}{3-\gamma}}\mathrm{diag}(\lambda_{0},\lambda_{1},\lambda_{2})\mathfrak{F}(x)\,,\ \ \lim_{x\to+\infty}\mathfrak{F}(x)=0_{3}\,,

which is a second order ODE system.

We then claim that

(5.41)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{F}(x)=0\ (\forall x\geq 0)\ \ \textrm{if and only if }\ \mathfrak{F}(0)=0_{3}\,.

Indeed, we first rewrite the equation (
5.40
) in the components form

(5.42)

\displaystyle\tfrac{\mathrm{d}^{2}}{\mathrm{d}x^{2}}\mathfrak{F}_{i}(x)=\lambda_{i}(\delta x+l)^{-\frac{1-\gamma}{3-\gamma}}\mathfrak{F}_{i}(x)\,,\ \ \lim_{x\to+\infty}\mathfrak{F}_{i}(x)=0

for
i=0,1,2
, where

\displaystyle\mathfrak{F}_{0}(x)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}v_{3}\widehat{\mathbb{B}}_{3}f_{*}(x,v)\mathrm{d}v\,,\ \mathfrak{F}_{i}(x)=\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}v_{3}\widehat{\mathbb{A}}_{i3}f_{*}(x,v)\mathrm{d}v\ \ (i=1,2)\,.

Now we employ the so-called
Freezing Point Method
to complete the proof of claim (
5.41
). For any fixed
x_{0}\geq 0
, define

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}(x_{0})=\lambda_{i}^{\frac{1}{2}}(\delta x_{0}+l)^{-\frac{1-\gamma}{2(3-\gamma)}}>0\,,\ \mathfrak{g}_{i}(x)=\lambda_{i}(\delta x+l)^{-\frac{1-\gamma}{3-\gamma}}\mathfrak{F}_{i}(x)-\lambda_{i}(\delta x_{0}+l)^{-\frac{1-\gamma}{3-\gamma}}\mathfrak{F}_{i}(x)

for
i=0,1,2
. Then the component equations (
5.42
) for
\mathfrak{F}(x)
can be reformulated as

(5.43)

\displaystyle\tfrac{\mathrm{d}^{2}}{\mathrm{d}x^{2}}\mathfrak{F}_{i}(x)-\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}^{2}(x_{0})\mathfrak{F}_{i}(x)=\mathfrak{g}_{i}(x)\,,\ \ \lim_{x\to+\infty}\mathfrak{F}_{i}(x)=0

Note that the general solution of the second order linear ODE
\tfrac{\mathrm{d}^{2}}{\mathrm{d}x^{2}}\mathfrak{F}_{i}(x)-\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}^{2}(x_{0})\mathfrak{F}_{i}(x)=0
is
\mathfrak{F}_{i}(x)=C_{1}e^{-\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}(x_{0})x}+C_{2}e^{\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}(x_{0})x}
, where
C_{1},C_{2}
are two arbitrary constants. Then one can set that the solution of (
5.43
) admits the form

\displaystyle\mathfrak{F}_{i}(x)=C_{1}(x)e^{-\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}(x_{0})x}+C_{2}(x)e^{\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}(x_{0})x}\,,

where
C_{1}(x)
and
C_{2}(x)
are two functions to be determined later. Substituting the above form into (
5.43
), one derives from a direct calculation that

\displaystyle-\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}(x_{0})e^{-\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}(x_{0})x}C_{1}^{\prime}(x)+\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}(x_{0})e^{\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}(x_{0})x}C_{2}^{\prime}(x)+\big[e^{-\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}(x_{0})x}C_{1}^{\prime}(x)+e^{\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}(x_{0})x}C_{2}^{\prime}(x)\big]^{\prime}=\mathfrak{g}_{i}(x)\,.

One then takes
C_{1}(x)
and
C_{2}(x)
satisfying

\displaystyle e^{-\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}(x_{0})x}C_{1}^{\prime}(x)+e^{\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}(x_{0})x}C_{2}^{\prime}(x)=0\,,

\displaystyle-\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}(x_{0})e^{-\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}(x_{0})x}C_{1}^{\prime}(x)+\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}(x_{0})e^{\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}(x_{0})x}C_{2}^{\prime}(x)=\mathfrak{g}_{i}(x)\,,

which implies that

\displaystyle C_{1}^{\prime}(x)=-\tfrac{1}{2\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}(x_{0})}e^{\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}(x_{0})x}\mathfrak{g}_{i}(x)\,,\ \ C_{2}^{\prime}(x)=\tfrac{1}{2\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}(x_{0})}e^{-\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}(x_{0})x}\mathfrak{g}_{i}(x)\,.

Integrating the above two equations from
x_{0}
to
x
, one gains

\displaystyle C_{1}(x)=C_{1}(x_{0})-\tfrac{1}{2\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}(x_{0})}\int_{x_{0}}^{x}e^{\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}(x_{0})y}\mathfrak{g}_{i}(y)\mathrm{d}y\,,\ C_{2}(x)=C_{2}(x_{0})+\tfrac{1}{2\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}(x_{0})}\int_{x_{0}}^{x}e^{-\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}(x_{0})y}\mathfrak{g}_{i}(y)\mathrm{d}y\,.

Consequently,

\displaystyle\mathfrak{F}_{i}(x)=\Big[C_{1}(x_{0})-\tfrac{1}{2\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}(x_{0})}\int_{x_{0}}^{x}e^{\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}(x_{0})y}\mathfrak{g}_{i}(y)\mathrm{d}y\Big]e^{-\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}(x_{0})x}+\Big[C_{2}(x_{0})+\tfrac{1}{2\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}(x_{0})}\int_{x_{0}}^{x}e^{-\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}(x_{0})y}\mathfrak{g}_{i}(y)\mathrm{d}y\Big]e^{\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}(x_{0})x}\,.

Due to the far-field condition
\lim_{x\to+\infty}\mathfrak{F}_{i}(x)=0
, it must hold

\displaystyle\mathfrak{F}_{i}(x)=\Big[C_{1}(x_{0})-\tfrac{1}{2\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}(x_{0})}\int_{x_{0}}^{x}e^{\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}(x_{0})y}\mathfrak{g}_{i}(y)\mathrm{d}y\Big]e^{-\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}(x_{0})x}\,.

which means that

(5.44)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{F}_{i}(x_{0})=C_{1}(x_{0})e^{-\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}(x_{0})x_{0}}\,.

Moreover,

\displaystyle\tfrac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}x}\mathfrak{F}_{i}(x)=-\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}(x_{0})\Big[C_{1}(x_{0})-\tfrac{1}{2\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}(x_{0})}\int_{x_{0}}^{x}e^{\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}(x_{0})y}\mathfrak{g}_{i}(y)\mathrm{d}y\Big]e^{-\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}(x_{0})x}-\tfrac{1}{2\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}(x_{0})}\mathfrak{g}_{i}(x)\,.

Note that
\mathfrak{g}_{i}(x_{0})=0
. One then has

(5.45)

\displaystyle\tfrac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}x}\mathfrak{F}_{i}(x_{0})=-\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}(x_{0})C_{1}(x_{0})e^{-\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}(x_{0})x_{0}}\,.

Due to the arbitrariness of
x_{0}\geq 0
, the relations (
5.44
) and (
5.45
) give us

\displaystyle\tfrac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}x}\mathfrak{F}_{i}(x)=-\boldsymbol{\theta}_{i}(x)\mathfrak{F}_{i}(x)=-\lambda_{i}(\delta x+l)^{-\frac{1-\gamma}{3-\gamma}}\mathfrak{F}_{i}(x)

for all
x\geq 0
, which is equivalent to
\tfrac{\mathrm{d}}{\mathrm{d}x}\Big(e^{\frac{\lambda_{i}(3-\gamma)}{2\delta}(\delta x+l)^{\frac{2}{3-\gamma}}}\mathfrak{F}_{i}(x)\Big)=0
. As a result,
\mathfrak{F}_{i}(x)=0
for all
x\geq 0
if and only if
\mathfrak{F}_{i}(0)=0
. The claim (
5.41
) is therefore valid.

Note that
\mathcal{P}^{0}f_{*}=0
is equivalent to
\mathfrak{F}(x)=0
for all
x\geq 0
. As a consequence, by (
5.37
) and (
5.41
), we have proved that
\mathbf{D}f_{*}(x,v)=0
for all
x\geq 0
and
v\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
if and only if

(5.46)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}v_{3}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\psi_{3}^{*}\\
\widehat{\mathbb{B}}_{3}\\
\widehat{\mathbb{A}}_{13}\\
\widehat{\mathbb{A}}_{23}\end{array}\right)f_{*}(0,v)\mathrm{d}v=0_{4}\,.

By using the relations (
5.32
) and (
1.62
), the solvability conditions (
5.46
) can be equivalently expressed by the form (
1.51
) in Remark
1.3
. Then the vanishing sources set
\mathbb{VSS}_{\alpha_{*}}
can be re-characterized by

\displaystyle\mathbb{VSS}_{\alpha_{*}}=\Big\{

\displaystyle(S,f_{b});S\in\mathrm{Null}^{\perp}(\mathcal{L}),\mathbb{I}_{\gamma}(S,f_{b})=f,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\psi_{3}^{*}\\
\widehat{\mathbb{B}}_{3}\\
\widehat{\mathbb{A}}_{13}\\
\widehat{\mathbb{A}}_{23}\end{array}\right)\Big(v_{3}f(0,v)+\int_{0}^{\infty}S(z,v)\mathrm{d}z\Big)\mathrm{d}v=0_{4}\Big\}\,.

Consequently, if
(S,f_{b})\in\mathbb{VSS}_{\alpha_{*}}\cap\mathfrak{X}_{\gamma}^{\infty}
, the problem (
KL
) admits a unique solution
f(x,v)
enjoying the bound (
1.48
) in Theorem
1.1
.

Because the four functions
\psi_{3}^{*}
,
\widehat{\mathbb{B}}_{3}
,
\widehat{\mathbb{A}}_{13}
,
\widehat{\mathbb{A}}_{23}
are linearly independent, the space
\mathbb{VSS}_{\alpha_{*}}\cap\mathfrak{X}_{\gamma}^{\infty}
is the subspace of
\mathfrak{X}_{\gamma}^{\infty}
with codimension 4. The proof of Theorem
1.1
is therefore finished.
∎

6.
Nonlinear problem (
KL-NL
): Proof of Theorem
1.2

In this section, we devote to investigating the nonlinear problem (
KL-NL
) near the Maxwellian
\mathfrak{M}(v)
by employing the linear theory constructed in Theorem
1.1
. It is equivalent to study the equation (
1.53
). The key point is to dominate the quantity
\mathscr{E}^{\infty}((\delta x+l)^{\frac{1-\gamma}{3-\gamma}}e^{\hbar\sigma}\Gamma(f,g))
, where the functional
\mathscr{E}^{\infty}(\cdot)
is given in (
1.36
).

Notice that
\mathscr{E}^{\infty}((\delta x+l)^{\frac{1-\gamma}{3-\gamma}}e^{\hbar\sigma}\Gamma(f,g))
is composed of the weighted
L^{\infty}_{x,v}
-norms and
L^{2}_{x,v}
-norms of
e^{\hbar\sigma}\Gamma(f,g)
. Concerning the weights involved in
\mathscr{E}^{\infty}((\delta x+l)^{\frac{1-\gamma}{3-\gamma}}e^{\hbar\sigma}\Gamma(f,g))
, one has

(6.1)

\displaystyle e^{\hbar\sigma}\Gamma(f,g)=e^{\hbar\sigma}\Gamma(e^{-\hbar\sigma}e^{\hbar\sigma}f,e^{-\hbar\sigma}e^{\hbar\sigma}g)\,.

By the properties of
\sigma
in Lemma
2.1
, it holds
e^{\hbar\sigma}\geq e^{c(\delta x+l)^{\frac{2}{3-\gamma}}}e^{c|v|^{2}}
. As shown in (
6.1
), loosely speaking, one of
e^{-\hbar\sigma}
can absorb the factor
e^{\hbar\sigma}
out of
\Gamma
, and the other decay factor
e^{-\hbar\sigma}\leq e^{-c(\delta x+l)^{\frac{2}{3-\gamma}}}e^{-c|v|^{2}}
can be used to adjust the required weights.

It is easy to see that
\Gamma(f,g)
can be pointwise bounded by the
L^{\infty}_{x,v}
-norms of
f
and
g
. By the similar arguments in Lemma 3 of
[
23
]
, the weighted
L^{2}_{x,v}
-norms of
e^{\hbar\sigma}\Gamma(f,g)
can be bounded by the quantity with form
\|w_{1}e^{\hbar\sigma}f\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\|w_{2}e^{\hbar\sigma}g\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}+\|w_{1}e^{\hbar\sigma}g\|_{L^{2}_{x,v}}\|w_{2}e^{\hbar\sigma}f\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}
, where
w_{1},w_{2}
are some required weights. As a result, we can establish the following lemma.

Lemma 6.1
.

For arbitrary functions
f(x,v)
and
g(x,v)
, there holds

(6.2)

\displaystyle\mathscr{E}^{\infty}((\delta x+l)^{\frac{1-\gamma}{3-\gamma}}e^{\hbar\sigma}\Gamma(f,g))\leq C\mathscr{E}^{\infty}(e^{\hbar\sigma}f)\mathscr{E}^{\infty}(e^{\hbar\sigma}g)\,,

where the functionals
\mathscr{E}^{\infty}(\cdot)
is defined in (
1.36
).

The proof of Lemma
6.1
can be finished by applying the properties of the weight
\sigma(x,v)
and employing the similar arguments in Lemma 3 of
[
23
]
. For simplicity, we omit the details here.

Based on Lemma
6.1
, we will study the nonlinear problem (
1.53
) (equivalently (
KL-NL
)) by employing the iterative approach, hence, prove Theorem
1.2
.

Proof of Theorem
1.2
.

As similar as in investigating the linear problem, the nonlinear problem (
1.53
) can be decomposed as

(6.3)

{\small\left\{\begin{aligned} &v_{3}\partial_{x}f_{1}+\mathcal{L}f_{1}=(\mathbb{I}-\mathbb{P})\Gamma(f,f)+(\mathbb{I}-\mathbb{P})\widehat{h}\,,\ v_{3}\partial_{x}f_{2}=\mathbb{P}\Gamma(f,f)+\mathbb{P}\widehat{h}\,,\\
&f=f_{1}+f_{2}\,,\ f_{1}=(\mathcal{I}-\mathcal{P}^{0})f\,,\ f_{2}=\mathcal{P}^{0}f\,,\\
&f_{1}(0,v)|_{v_{3}>0}=(1-\alpha_{*})f(0,R_{0}v)+\alpha_{*}\mathcal{D}_{w}f_{1}(0,v)+\widetilde{f}_{b}(\widehat{f}_{b},f_{2})\,,\\
&\lim_{x\to+\infty}f_{1}(x,v)=\lim_{x\to+\infty}f_{2}(x,v)=0\,,\end{aligned}\right.}

where the operators
\mathcal{P}^{0}
and
\mathbb{P}
are respectively defined in (
1.20
) and (
1.21
), and

(6.4)

\displaystyle\widetilde{f}_{b}(\widehat{f}_{b},f_{2})(v)=\widehat{f}_{b}(v)-f_{2}(0,v)\mathbf{1}_{v_{3}>0}+(1-\alpha_{*})f_{2}(0,R_{0}v)\mathbf{1}_{v_{3}>0}+\alpha_{*}\mathcal{D}_{2}f_{2}(0,v)\mathbf{1}_{v_{3}>0}\,.

As inspired by the linear theory, we first consider the artificial damped nonlinear problem (
1.58
), i.e.,

{\small\left\{\begin{aligned} &v_{3}\partial_{x}f_{1}+\mathcal{L}f_{1}+\mathbf{D}f_{1}=(\mathbb{I}-\mathbb{P})\Gamma(f,f)+(\mathbb{I}-\mathbb{P})\widehat{h}\,,\\
&v_{3}\partial_{x}f_{2}=\mathbb{P}\Gamma(f,f)+\mathbb{P}\widehat{h}\,,f=f_{1}+f_{2}\,,\\
&f_{1}(0,v)|_{v_{3}>0}=(1-\alpha_{*})f(0,R_{0}v)+\alpha_{*}\mathcal{D}_{w}f_{1}(0,v)+\widetilde{f}_{b}(\widehat{f}_{b},f_{2})\,,\\
&\lim_{x\to+\infty}f_{1}(x,v)=\lim_{x\to+\infty}f_{2}(x,v)=0\,,\end{aligned}\right.}

where the artificial damping operator
\mathbf{D}
is defined in (
1.63
).

Note that
f_{1}
and
f_{2}
are coupled each other in the system (
1.58
). In order to study the existence and uniqueness of the damped nonlinear problem (
1.58
), we design the following iteration scheme, which decouples the functions
f_{1}
and
f_{2}
:

(6.5)

{\small\left\{\begin{aligned} &v_{3}\partial_{x}f^{i+1}_{1}+\mathcal{L}f^{i+1}_{1}+\mathbf{D}f^{i+1}_{1}=(\mathbb{I}-\mathbb{P})\Gamma(f^{i},f^{i})+(\mathbb{I}-\mathbb{P})\widehat{h}\,,\ x>0\,,v\in\mathbb{R}^{3}\,,\\
&v_{3}\partial_{x}f^{i+1}_{2}=\mathbb{P}\Gamma(f^{i},f^{i})+\mathbb{P}\widehat{h}\,,\ x>0\,,v\in\mathbb{R}^{3}\,,\ f^{i+1}=f^{i+1}_{1}+f^{i+1}_{2}\,,\\
&f^{i+1}_{1}(0,v)|_{v_{3}>0}=(1-\alpha_{*})f^{i+1}_{1}(0,R_{0}v)+\alpha_{*}\mathcal{D}_{w}f^{i+1}_{1}(0,v)+\widetilde{f}_{b}(\widehat{f}_{b},f^{i+1}_{2})\,,\\
&\lim_{x\to+\infty}f^{i+1}_{1}(x,v)=\lim_{x\to+\infty}f^{i+1}_{2}(x,v)=0\,,\end{aligned}\right.}

which starts from
f^{0}_{1}(x,v)=f^{0}_{2}(x,v)=0
.

We first iteratively solve
f^{i+1}_{2}(x,v)
, which are a sequence of ODE equations. By (
5.34
), one has

(6.6)

\displaystyle\mathscr{E}^{\infty}(e^{\hbar\sigma}f^{i+1}_{2})\lesssim

\displaystyle\mathscr{E}^{\infty}((\delta x+l)^{\frac{1-\gamma}{3-\gamma}}e^{\hbar\sigma}(\Gamma(f^{i},f^{i})+\widehat{h}))

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\mathscr{E}^{\infty}((\delta x+l)^{\frac{1-\gamma}{3-\gamma}}e^{\hbar\sigma}\Gamma(f^{i},f^{i}))+\mathscr{E}^{\infty}((\delta x+l)^{\frac{1-\gamma}{3-\gamma}}e^{\hbar\sigma}\widehat{h})

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\big[\mathscr{E}^{\infty}(e^{\hbar\sigma}f^{i}_{1})+\mathscr{E}^{\infty}(e^{\hbar\sigma}f^{i}_{2})\big]^{2}+\mathscr{E}^{\infty}((\delta x+l)^{\frac{1-\gamma}{3-\gamma}}e^{\hbar\sigma}\widehat{h})\,,

where the last inequality is derived from (
6.2
) in Lemma
6.1
.

We then iteratively solve
f^{i+1}_{1}(x,v)
, which subject to the linear problem with the same type of (
KLd
). By (
5.31
), one has

(6.7)

\displaystyle\mathscr{E}^{\infty}(e^{\hbar\sigma}f^{i+1}_{1})\lesssim

\displaystyle\mathscr{A}^{\infty}(e^{\hbar\sigma}(\mathbb{I}-\mathbb{P})(\Gamma(f^{i},f^{i})+\widehat{h}))+\|\widetilde{f}_{b}(\widehat{f}_{b},f^{i+1}_{2})\|_{\mathfrak{N}}

\displaystyle\lesssim

\displaystyle\big[\mathscr{E}^{\infty}(e^{\hbar\sigma}f^{i}_{1})+\mathscr{E}^{\infty}(e^{\hbar\sigma}f^{i}_{2})\big]^{2}+\mathscr{E}^{\infty}((\delta x+l)^{\frac{1-\gamma}{3-\gamma}}e^{\hbar\sigma}\widehat{h})+\|\widehat{f}_{b}\|_{\mathfrak{N}}\,,

where the last inequality is derived from the similar arguments in (
5.35
), (
5.36
) and (
6.6
).

Then the bounds (
6.6
) and (
6.7
) indicate that

(6.8)

\displaystyle\mathscr{E}^{\infty}(e^{\hbar\sigma}f^{i+1}_{1})+\mathscr{E}^{\infty}(e^{\hbar\sigma}f^{i+1}_{2})\leq C_{0}\big[\mathscr{E}^{\infty}(e^{\hbar\sigma}f^{i}_{1})+\mathscr{E}^{\infty}(e^{\hbar\sigma}f^{i}_{2})\big]^{2}+C_{0}\varsigma

for some constant
C_{0}>0
, where the quantity
\varsigma
is defined in (
1.56
), i.e.,
\varsigma=\mathscr{E}^{\infty}((\delta x+l)^{\frac{1-\gamma}{3-\gamma}}e^{\hbar\sigma}\widehat{h})+\|\widehat{f}_{b}\|_{\mathfrak{N}}
.

Now we assert that there is a small
\varsigma_{0}>0
such that if
\varsigma\leq\varsigma_{0}
, then for all
i\geq 1
,

(6.9)

\displaystyle\mathscr{E}^{\infty}(e^{\hbar\sigma}f^{i}_{1})+\mathscr{E}^{\infty}(e^{\hbar\sigma}f^{i}_{2})\leq 2C_{0}\varsigma\,.

Note that
f^{0}_{1}=f^{0}_{2}=0
. The bound (
6.8
) shows that
\mathscr{E}^{\infty}(e^{\hbar\sigma}f^{1}_{1})+\mathscr{E}^{\infty}(e^{\hbar\sigma}f^{1}_{2})\leq C_{0}\varsigma<2C_{0}\varsigma
, i.e., the claim (
6.9
) holds for
i=1
. Assume that the claim (
6.9
) holds for
1,2,\cdots,i
. Then the case
i+1
can be carried out by (
6.8
) that
\mathscr{E}^{\infty}(e^{\hbar\sigma}f^{i+1}_{1})+\mathscr{E}^{\infty}(e^{\hbar\sigma}f^{i+1}_{2})\leq C(2C_{0}\varsigma)^{2}+C_{0}\varsigma=(4CC_{0}\varsigma+1)C_{0}\varsigma
. Take
\varsigma_{0}>0
small such that
4CC_{0}\varsigma_{0}\leq 1
. Then if
\varsigma\leq\varsigma_{0}
, one has
\mathscr{E}^{\infty}(e^{\hbar\sigma}f^{i+1}_{1})+\mathscr{E}^{\infty}(e^{\hbar\sigma}f^{i+1}_{2})\leq(4CC_{0}\varsigma+1)C_{0}\varsigma\leq 2C_{0}\varsigma
. Therefore, the Induction Principle concludes the claim (
6.9
), which indicates that
\{f^{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
is bounded in the Banach space
\mathbb{B}_{\hbar}^{\infty}
defined in (
5.1
).

Next we will show that
\{f^{i}\}_{i\geq 1}
is a Cauchy sequence in
\mathbb{B}_{\hbar}^{\infty}
. Observe that
f^{i+1}_{1}-f^{i}_{1}
and
f^{i+1}_{2}-f^{i}_{2}
subject to

{\small\left\{\begin{aligned} &v_{3}\partial_{x}(f^{i+1}_{1}-f^{i}_{1})+\mathcal{L}(f^{i+1}_{1}-f^{i}_{1})+\mathbf{D}(f^{i+1}_{1}-f^{i}_{1})\\
&\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt=(\mathbb{I}-\mathbb{P})\Gamma(f^{i}-f^{i-1},f^{i})+(\mathbb{I}-\mathbb{P})\Gamma(f^{i-1},f^{i}-f^{i-1})\,,\ x>0\,,v\in\mathbb{R}^{3}\,,\\
&v_{3}\partial_{x}(f^{i+1}_{2}-f^{i}_{2})=\mathbb{P}\Gamma(f^{i}-f^{i-1},f^{i})+\mathbb{P}\Gamma(f^{i-1},f^{i}-f^{i-1})\,,\ x>0\,,v\in\mathbb{R}^{3}\,,\\
&f^{i+1}-f^{i}=(f^{i+1}_{1}-f^{i}_{1})+(f^{i+1}_{2}-f^{i}_{2})\,,\\
&(f^{i+1}_{1}-f^{i}_{1})(0,v)|_{v_{3}>0}=(1-\alpha_{*})(f^{i+1}_{1}-f^{i}_{1})(0,R_{0}v)+\alpha_{*}\mathcal{D}_{w}(f^{i+1}_{1}-f^{i}_{1})(0,v)+\widetilde{f}_{b}(0,f^{i+1}_{2}-f^{i}_{2})\,,\\
&\lim_{x\to+\infty}(f^{i+1}_{1}-f^{i}_{1})(x,v)=\lim_{x\to+\infty}(f^{i+1}_{2}-f^{i}_{2})(x,v)=0\,.\end{aligned}\right.}

By the similar arguments in (
6.8
), one has

\displaystyle\mathscr{E}^{\infty}(e^{\hbar\sigma}(f^{i+1}_{1}-f^{i}_{1}))+\mathscr{E}^{\infty}(e^{\hbar\sigma}(f^{i+1}_{2}-f^{i}_{2}))

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\big[\mathscr{E}^{\infty}(e^{\hbar\sigma}f^{i})+\mathscr{E}^{\infty}(e^{\hbar\sigma}f^{i-1})\big]\big[\mathscr{E}^{\infty}(e^{\hbar\sigma}(f^{i}_{1}-f^{i-1}_{2}))+\mathscr{E}^{\infty}(e^{\hbar\sigma}(f^{i}_{2}-f^{i-1}_{2}))\big]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 4CC_{0}\varsigma\big[\mathscr{E}^{\infty}(e^{\hbar\sigma}(f^{i}_{1}-f^{i-1}_{2}))+\mathscr{E}^{\infty}(e^{\hbar\sigma}(f^{i}_{2}-f^{i-1}_{2}))\big]\,,

where the last inequality is implied by (
6.9
). As a consequence,

(6.10)

\displaystyle\mathscr{E}^{\infty}(e^{\hbar\sigma}(f^{i+1}-f^{i}))\leq 4CC_{0}^{2}\varsigma\mathscr{E}^{\infty}(e^{\hbar\sigma}(f^{i}-f^{i-1}))\leq\tfrac{1}{2}\mathscr{E}^{\infty}(e^{\hbar\sigma}(f^{i}-f^{i-1}))

by further taking small
\varsigma_{0}>0
such that
4CC_{0}^{2}\varsigma\leq 4CC_{0}^{2}\varsigma_{0}\leq\frac{1}{2}
.

Note that
f^{0}_{1}=f^{0}_{2}=0
. It follows from iterating (
6.10
) and employing (
6.9
) that

(6.11)

\displaystyle\mathscr{E}^{\infty}(e^{\hbar\sigma}(f^{i+1}_{1}-f^{i}_{1}))+\mathscr{E}^{\infty}(e^{\hbar\sigma}(f^{i+1}_{2}-f^{i}_{2}))

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(\tfrac{1}{2})^{i}\big[\mathscr{E}^{\infty}(e^{\hbar\sigma}f^{1}_{1})+\mathscr{E}^{\infty}(e^{\hbar\sigma}f^{1}_{2})\big]\leq 2C_{0}\varsigma(\tfrac{1}{2})^{i}\to 0

as
i\to+\infty
. Consequently, (
6.9
) and (
6.11
) tell us that
\{f^{i}_{1}\}_{i\geq 1}
and
\{f^{i}_{2}\}_{i\geq 1}
are both bounded Cauchy sequence in
\mathbb{B}_{\hbar}^{\infty}
. Then there is a unique pair of
(f_{1},f_{2})(x,v)\in\mathbb{B}_{\hbar}^{\infty}
such that

\displaystyle(f^{i}_{1},f^{i}_{2})(x,v)\to(f_{1},f_{2})(x,v)\quad\textrm{strongly in }\mathbb{B}_{\hbar}^{\infty}\,.

Passing the limit
i\to+\infty
in the mild formation of the iterative scheme (
6.5
), one knows that
(f_{1},f_{2})(x,v)
solves the damped nonlinear problem (
1.58
). Moreover,
(f_{1},f_{2})(x,v)
enjoys the estimate
\mathscr{E}^{\infty}(e^{\hbar\sigma}f_{1})+\mathscr{E}^{\infty}(e^{\hbar\sigma}f_{2})\leq 2C_{0}\varsigma
. Moreover, the uniqueness of (
1.58
) can be obtained by similar arguments in (
6.10
). As a result, for any
(\widehat{h},\widehat{f}_{b})\in\mathfrak{X}_{\gamma}^{\varsigma_{0}}
, the solution operator
\mathcal{I}^{\gamma}
to the damped nonlinear problem (
1.58
) by

(6.12)

\displaystyle\mathcal{I}^{\gamma}(\widehat{h},\widehat{f}_{b})=f

given in Remark
1.4
is well-defined, where the space
\mathfrak{X}_{\gamma}^{\varsigma_{0}}
is defined in (
1.56
).

Now we prove the existence of the nonlinear problem (
KL-NL
) (equivalently (
6.3
)) by removing the artificial damping term
\mathbf{D}f_{1}
in (
6.3
). By the similar derivations of (
5.46
), one knows that
\mathbf{D}f_{1}(x,v)=0
for any
x\geq 0
and
v\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
if and only if

(6.13)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}v_{3}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\psi_{3}^{*}\\
\widehat{\mathbb{B}}_{3}\\
\widehat{\mathbb{A}}_{13}\\
\widehat{\mathbb{A}}_{23}\end{array}\right)f_{1}(0,v)\mathrm{d}v=0_{4}\,.

Note that
f_{1}=f-f_{2}
and
f_{2}(0,v)=-\frac{1}{v_{3}}\int_{0}^{\infty}\big[\mathbb{P}\Gamma(f,f)+\mathbb{P}\widehat{h}\big](z,v)\mathrm{d}z
. Together with the facts
\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}X\cdot(\mathbb{I}-\mathbb{P})[\Gamma(f,f)+\widehat{h}]\mathrm{d}v=0
for
X=\psi_{3}^{*},\widehat{\mathbb{B}}_{3},\widehat{\mathbb{A}}_{13},\widehat{\mathbb{A}}_{23}
, the the conditions (
6.13
) is equivalent to

(6.14)

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\psi_{3}^{*}\\
\widehat{\mathbb{B}}_{3}\\
\widehat{\mathbb{A}}_{13}\\
\widehat{\mathbb{A}}_{23}\end{array}\right)\Big(v_{3}f(0,v)+\int_{0}^{\infty}\big[\Gamma(f,f)+\widehat{h}\big](z,v)\mathrm{d}z\Big)\mathrm{d}v=0_{4}\,.

Then the vanishing sources set

(6.15)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathbb{VSS}}_{\alpha_{*}}=\big\{(\widehat{h},\widehat{f}_{b});\widehat{h}\in\mathrm{Null}^{\perp},\mathcal{I}^{\gamma}(\widehat{h},\widehat{f}_{b})=f\textrm{ satisfies \eqref{Solvb-NL}}\big\}\,.

Consequently,
\mathcal{I}^{\gamma}(\widehat{h},\widehat{f}_{b})=f
is the solution to the nonlinear problem (
KL-NL
) if the source terms
(\widehat{h},\widehat{f}_{b})\in\widetilde{\mathbb{VSS}}_{\alpha_{*}}\cap{\mathfrak{X}}_{\gamma}^{\varsigma_{0}}
.

Since the functions
\psi_{3}^{*},\widehat{\mathbb{B}}_{3},\widehat{\mathbb{A}}_{13},\widehat{\mathbb{A}}_{23}
are linearly independent in
L^{2}_{v}
,
\widetilde{\mathbb{VSS}}_{\alpha_{*}}\cap{\mathfrak{X}}_{\gamma}^{\varsigma_{0}}
is the subspace of
{\mathfrak{X}}_{\gamma}^{\varsigma_{0}}
with codimension 4. The proof of Theorem
1.2
is therefore finished.
∎

7.
Bounds for operators
Y_{A}
,
Z
and
U
: Proof of Lemma
2.2

In this section, we mainly aim at verifying the proof of Lemma
2.2
.

Proof of Lemma
2.2
.

The proof of this lemma will be divided into three steps as follows.

Step 1.
L^{\infty}_{x,v}
bound for the operator
Y_{A}
.

We now show the inequality (
2.6
).

If
v_{3}>0
, Lemma
2.1
indicates that
0<v_{3}\sigma_{x}\leq c\nu(v)
and
|\sigma_{xx}v_{3}|\leq\delta\sigma_{x}\nu(v)
, which mean that

(7.1)

\displaystyle\kappa(x,v)=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{x}\big[-\tfrac{m\sigma_{xx}(y,v)}{2\sigma_{x}(y,v)}-\hbar\sigma_{x}(y,v)+\tfrac{\nu(v)}{v_{3}}\big]\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{x}(1-c\hbar-\tfrac{|m|}{2}\delta)\tfrac{\nu(v)}{v_{3}}\mathrm{d}y=(1-c\hbar-\tfrac{|m|}{2}\delta)\tfrac{\nu(v)}{v_{3}}x\geq 0\,,

provided that
c_{\hbar,\delta}:=1-c\hbar-\tfrac{|m|}{2}\delta>0
. Actually, one can take sufficiently small
\hbar,\delta>0
such that
c_{\hbar,\delta}>\frac{1}{2}
. Then one has

(7.2)

\displaystyle e^{-\kappa(x,v)}\leq 1

for
1-c\hbar-\tfrac{|m|}{2}\delta>0
. Moreover,
(R_{0}v)_{3}=-v_{3}<0
, one similarly knows that

(7.3)

\displaystyle\kappa(A,R_{0}v)=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{A}\big[-\tfrac{m\sigma_{xx}(y,R_{0}v)}{2\sigma_{x}(y,R_{0}v)}-\hbar\sigma_{x}(y,R_{0}v)+\tfrac{\nu(R_{0}v)}{(R_{0}v)_{3}}\big]\mathrm{d}y

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{A}(1-c\hbar-\tfrac{|m|}{2}\delta)\tfrac{\nu(v)}{v_{3}}\mathrm{d}y=-(1-c\hbar-\tfrac{|m|}{2}\delta)\tfrac{\nu(v)}{v_{3}}A\leq 0\,,

which means that
e^{\kappa(A,R_{0}v)}\leq 1
. As a result, for
v_{3}>0
,

(7.4)

\displaystyle e^{{\kappa}(A,R_{0}v)-{\kappa}(x,v)}\leq 1\,.

Here
{\kappa}(x,v)
is defined in (
2.1
). It thereby holds

(7.5)

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{1}_{v_{3}>0}e^{{\kappa}(A,R_{0}v)-{\kappa}(x,v)}f(A,R_{0}v)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\leq\|f(A,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}_{v}}\,.

Moreover, by the similar arguments in (
7.4
), one easily obtains
e^{{\kappa}(A,v^{\prime})-{\kappa}(x,v)}\leq 1
for
v_{3}^{\prime}<0
and
v_{3}>0
. Recall the definition of
M_{\sigma}
in (
2.2
), i.e.,
\mathfrak{M}_{\sigma}(v)=\mathfrak{M}(v)e^{-2\hbar\sigma(0,v)}
. Based on Lemma
2.1
, it is easy to verify that

\displaystyle|v_{3}^{\prime}|\sigma_{x}^{-\frac{m}{2}}(0,v^{\prime})\sqrt{\mathfrak{M}_{\sigma}(v^{\prime})}\leq C\mathfrak{M}^{\frac{1}{4}}(v^{\prime})

for some universal constant
C>0
. Now we deal with the factor
\frac{M_{w}(v)}{\sqrt{\mathfrak{M}_{\sigma}(v)}}\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}(0,v)
. Lemma
2.1
tells us

0\leq\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}(0,v)e^{\hbar\sigma(0,v)}\leq c_{1}(1+|v-\mathfrak{u}|)^{(1-\gamma)|m|}e^{C\hbar|v-\mathfrak{u}|^{2}}\,.

Then

\displaystyle\tfrac{M_{w}(v)}{\sqrt{\mathfrak{M}_{\sigma}(v)}}\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}(0,v)=

\displaystyle\tfrac{\sqrt{2\pi}T^{\frac{3}{2}}}{\rho T_{w}^{2}}\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}(0,v)e^{\hbar\sigma(0,v)}\mathrm{exp}\{-(\tfrac{1}{2T_{w}}-\tfrac{1}{4T})|v-\mathfrak{u}|^{2}-\tfrac{1}{T_{w}}(\mathfrak{u}-u_{w})\cdot(v-\mathfrak{u})-\tfrac{|\mathfrak{u}-u_{w}|^{2}}{2T_{w}}\}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C(1+|v-\mathfrak{u}|)^{(1-\gamma)|m|}e^{c_{1}\hbar|v-\mathfrak{u}|^{2}}\mathrm{exp}\{-\tfrac{1}{2}(\tfrac{1}{2T_{w}}-\tfrac{1}{4T})|v-\mathfrak{u}|^{2}\}

with the assumption (
1.46
), i,e.,
0<T_{w}<2T
. By taking small
\hbar>0
such that
c_{1}\hbar<\tfrac{1}{2}(\tfrac{1}{2T_{w}}-\tfrac{1}{4T})
, it thereby infers
\tfrac{M_{w}(v)}{\sqrt{\mathfrak{M}_{\sigma}(v)}}\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}(0,v)\leq C
uniformly in
v\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
. We then have shown that

(7.6)

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{1}_{v_{3}>0}\tfrac{M_{w}(v)}{\sqrt{\mathfrak{M}_{\sigma}(v)}}\int_{v_{3}^{\prime}<0}(-v_{3}^{\prime})\tfrac{\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}(0,v)}{\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}(0,v^{\prime})}e^{{\kappa}(A,v^{\prime})-{\kappa}(x,v)}f(A,v^{\prime})\sqrt{\mathfrak{M}_{\sigma}(v^{\prime})}\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\|\int_{v_{3}^{\prime}<0}|f(A,v^{\prime})|\mathfrak{M}^{\frac{1}{4}}(v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\leq C\|f(A,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}_{v}}\,,

where the fact
\int_{v_{3}^{\prime}<0}\mathfrak{M}^{\frac{1}{4}}(v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\leq C
has been used.

If
v_{3}<0
,

(7.7)

\displaystyle\kappa(A,v)-\kappa(x,v)=\int_{x}^{A}[-\tfrac{m\sigma_{xx}(y,v)}{2\sigma_{x}(y,v)}-\hbar\sigma_{x}(y,v)-\tfrac{\nu(v)}{|v_{3}|}]\mathrm{d}y\leq-(1-c\hbar-\tfrac{m}{2}\delta)\tfrac{\nu(v)}{|v_{3}|}(A-x)\leq 0\,,

which means that

(7.8)

\displaystyle e^{\kappa(A,v)-\kappa(x,v)}\leq e^{-(1-c\hbar-\frac{m}{2}\delta)\tfrac{\nu(v)}{|v_{3}|}(A-x)}\leq 1\,.

Consequently, there holds

(7.9)

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{1}_{v_{3}<0}e^{{\kappa}(A,v)-{\kappa}(x,v)}f(A,v)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\leq\|f(A,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}_{v}}\,.

Recalling the definition of the operator
Y_{A}(f)
in (
2.3
), the estimates (
7.5
), (
7.6
) and (
7.9
) infer that

\displaystyle\|Y_{A}(f)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\leq C\|f(A,\cdot)\|_{L^{\infty}_{v}}

for some constant
C>0
independent of
A
,
\hbar
. Namely the bound (
2.6
) holds.

Step 2.
L^{\infty}_{x,v}
bound for the operator
Z
.

Now we prove the inequalities (
2.7
).

If
v_{3}>0
, then (
1.11
) and
\sigma_{x}>0
derived from Lemma
2.1
show that for
v_{3}>0
,

(7.10)

\displaystyle\kappa(x^{\prime},R_{0}v)=\int_{0}^{x^{\prime}}\big[-\tfrac{m\sigma_{xx}(y,R_{0}v)}{2\sigma_{x}(y,R_{0}v)}-\hbar\sigma_{x}(y,R_{0}v)+\tfrac{\nu(R_{0}v)}{(R_{0}v)_{3}}\big]\mathrm{d}y\leq-(1-\tfrac{\delta}{2})\tfrac{\nu(v)}{v_{3}}x^{\prime}<0\,.

Together with (
7.1
), one has
e^{-\kappa(x,v)+\kappa(x^{\prime},R_{0}v)}\leq e^{-c_{\hbar,\delta}\frac{\nu(v)}{v_{3}}x}e^{-(1-\tfrac{\delta}{2})\frac{\nu(v)}{v_{3}}x^{\prime}}
for
v_{3}>0
. Then there hold

\displaystyle(1-\tfrac{1}{n})|\int_{0}^{A}e^{-[{\kappa}(x,v)-{\kappa}(x^{\prime},R_{0}v)]}\tfrac{1}{v_{3}}f(x^{\prime},R_{0}v)\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}|

(7.11)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(1-\tfrac{1}{n})\mathbf{1}_{v_{3}>0}e^{-c_{\hbar,\delta}\frac{\nu(v)}{v_{3}}x}\int_{0}^{A}e^{-(1-\tfrac{\delta}{2})\frac{\nu(v)}{v_{3}}x^{\prime}}\tfrac{\nu(v)}{v_{3}}|\nu^{-1}(v)f(x^{\prime},R_{0}v)|\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}

for small
\hbar>0
. Note that the right-hand side of the bound (
7.11
) can be bounded by

\displaystyle(1-\tfrac{1}{n})\int_{0}^{A}e^{-(1-\tfrac{\delta}{2})\frac{\nu(v)}{v_{3}}x^{\prime}}\tfrac{\nu(v)}{v_{3}}\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}\|\nu^{-1}(v)f(x,R_{0}v)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}=(1-\tfrac{\delta}{2})^{-1}(1-\tfrac{1}{n})\nu^{-1}(v)\|f(\cdot,R_{0}v)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}}\,,

which implies

(7.12)

\displaystyle(1-\tfrac{1}{n})\|\int_{0}^{A}e^{-[{\kappa}(x,v)-{\kappa}(x^{\prime},R_{0}v)]}\tfrac{1}{v_{3}}f(x^{\prime},R_{0}v)\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\leq(1-\tfrac{\delta}{2})^{-1}(1-\tfrac{1}{n})\|\nu^{-1}f\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\,.

Now we control the quantity

\displaystyle\mathfrak{Q}(f)=\tfrac{M_{w}(v)}{\sqrt{\mathfrak{M}_{\sigma}(v)}}\int_{v_{3}^{\prime}<0}\int_{0}^{A}(-v_{3}^{\prime})\tfrac{\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}(0,v)}{\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}(0,v^{\prime})}e^{-[{\kappa}(x,v)-{\kappa}(x^{\prime},v^{\prime})]}\tfrac{1}{v_{3}^{\prime}}f(x^{\prime},v^{\prime})\sqrt{\mathfrak{M}_{\sigma}(v^{\prime})}\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\,.

The inequality (
7.1
) implies
\mathbf{1}_{v_{3}>0}e^{-\kappa(x,v)}\leq\mathbf{1}_{v_{3}>0}e^{-c_{\hbar,\delta}\frac{\nu(v)}{v_{3}}x}\leq 1
. By the similar arguments in (
7.3
), one has
\mathbf{1}_{v_{3}^{\prime}<0}e^{\kappa(x^{\prime},v^{\prime})}\leq\mathbf{1}_{v_{3}^{\prime}<0}e^{-c_{\hbar,\delta}\frac{\nu(v^{\prime})}{|v_{3}^{\prime}|}x^{\prime}}
. It also follows from the similar arguments of (
7.6
) that
\tfrac{M_{w}(v)}{\sqrt{\mathfrak{M}_{\sigma}(v)}}\tfrac{\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}(0,v)}{\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}(0,v^{\prime})}\sqrt{\mathfrak{M}_{\sigma}(v^{\prime})}\leq C\mathfrak{M}^{\frac{1}{4}}(v^{\prime})
uniformly in
v\in\mathbb{R}^{3}
for sufficiently small
\hbar>0
, where the constant
C>0
is independent of
A,n,\hbar,l
and
\delta
. As a consequence,

\displaystyle\|\mathfrak{Q}(f)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\leq

\displaystyle C\|\nu^{-1}f\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\int_{v_{3}^{\prime}<0}\int_{0}^{A}\tfrac{\nu(v^{\prime})}{|v_{3}^{\prime}|}e^{-c_{\hbar,\delta}\frac{\nu(v^{\prime})}{|v_{3}^{\prime}|}x^{\prime}}|v_{3}^{\prime}|\mathfrak{M}^{\frac{1}{4}}(v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tfrac{C}{c_{\hbar,\delta}}\|\nu^{-1}f\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\int_{v_{3}^{\prime}<0}(-e^{-c_{\hbar,\delta}\tfrac{\nu(v^{\prime})}{|v_{3}^{\prime}|}x^{\prime}})\big|_{0}^{A}|v_{3}^{\prime}|\mathfrak{M}^{\frac{1}{4}}(v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}

(7.13)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\tfrac{C}{c_{\hbar,\delta}}\|\nu^{-1}f\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\int_{v_{3}^{\prime}<0}|v_{3}^{\prime}|\mathfrak{M}^{\frac{1}{4}}(v^{\prime})\mathrm{d}v^{\prime}\leq C\|\nu^{-1}f\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}

with small
\hbar>0
and assumption (
1.46
). Therefore, plugging the estimates (
7.12
) and (
7.13
) into the expression (
2.4
) of the operator
Z(f)
, one gains
\|Z(f)\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\leq C\|\nu^{-1}f\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}
, hence the bound (
2.7
) holds.

Step 3.
L^{\infty}_{x,v}
bound for the operator
U
.

We now justify the bound (
2.8
). By the definition of
{\kappa}(x,v)
in (
2.1
), one has

\displaystyle\mathbf{1}_{x^{\prime}\in(0,x)}\mathbf{1}_{v_{3}>0}e^{-[{\kappa}(x,v)-{\kappa}(x^{\prime},v)]}=e^{-\int_{x^{\prime}}^{x}[-\frac{m\sigma_{xx}(y,v)}{2\sigma_{x}(y,v)}-\hbar\sigma_{x}(y,v)+\frac{\nu(v)}{v_{3}}]\mathrm{d}y}\,.

By the similar arguments in (
7.1
), one has

\displaystyle\mathbf{1}_{x^{\prime}\in(0,x)}\mathbf{1}_{v_{3}>0}e^{-[{\kappa}(x,v)-{\kappa}(x^{\prime},v)]}\leq\mathbf{1}_{x^{\prime}\in(0,x)}\mathbf{1}_{v_{3}>0}e^{-c_{\hbar,\delta}\frac{\nu(v)}{v_{3}}(x-x^{\prime})}\,.

Together with the definition of the operator
U
in (
2.5
), it infers that

\displaystyle|\mathbf{1}_{v_{3}>0}U(f)|\leq

\displaystyle C\int_{0}^{x}\tfrac{\nu(v)}{v_{3}}e^{-\frac{1}{2}c_{\hbar,\delta}\frac{\nu(v)}{v_{3}}(x-x^{\prime})}|\nu^{-1}f(x^{\prime}v)|\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\|\nu^{-1}f\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\int_{0}^{x}\tfrac{\nu(v)}{v_{3}}e^{-c_{\hbar,\delta}\frac{\nu(v)}{v_{3}}(x-x^{\prime})}\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}

(7.14)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tfrac{C}{c_{\hbar,\delta}}\|\nu^{-1}f\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}(1-e^{-\frac{1}{2}c_{\hbar,\delta}\frac{\nu(v)}{v_{3}}x})\leq\tfrac{2C}{c_{\hbar,\delta}}\|\nu^{-1}f\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\,.

We then consider the case
v_{3}<0
. Combining with the definition of
{\kappa}(x,v)
in (
2.1
) and the inequality (
7.7
), one derives

\displaystyle\mathbf{1}_{x^{\prime}\in(x,A)}\mathbf{1}_{v_{3}<0}e^{-[{\kappa}(x,v)-{\kappa}(x^{\prime},v)]}=

\displaystyle\mathbf{1}_{x^{\prime}\in(x,A)}\mathbf{1}_{v_{3}<0}e^{\int^{x^{\prime}}_{x}[-\frac{m\sigma_{xx}(y,v)}{2\sigma_{x}(y,v)}-\hbar\sigma_{x}(y,v)-\frac{\nu(v)}{|v_{3}|}]\mathrm{d}y}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\mathbf{1}_{x^{\prime}\in(x,A)}\mathbf{1}_{v_{3}<0}e^{-c_{\hbar,\delta}\frac{\nu(v)}{|v_{3}|}(x^{\prime}-x)}\,.

Then

(7.15)

\displaystyle|\mathbf{1}_{v_{3}<0}U(f)|\leq

\displaystyle\int_{x}^{A}e^{-c_{\hbar,\delta}\frac{\nu(v)}{|v_{3}|}(x^{\prime}-x)}\tfrac{\nu(v)}{|v_{3}|}|\nu^{-1}(v)f(x^{\prime},v)|\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|\nu^{-1}f\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\int_{x}^{A}e^{-c_{\hbar,\delta}\frac{\nu(v)}{|v_{3}|}(x^{\prime}-x)}\tfrac{\nu(v)}{|v_{3}|}\mathrm{d}x^{\prime}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{c_{\hbar,\delta}}\|\nu^{-1}f\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}(1-e^{-c_{\hbar,\delta}\frac{\nu(v)}{|v_{3}|}(A-x)})\leq\tfrac{1}{c_{\hbar,\delta}}\|\nu^{-1}f\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\,.

Then the bounds (
7.14
) and (
7.15
) conclude the estimate (
2.8
). The proof of Lemma
2.2
is therefore completed.
∎

8.
Properties of the operator
K
: Proof of Lemma
2.3
-
2.4
-
2.5

In this section, we aim at investigating the properties of the operator
K
.

8.1.
L^{\infty}_{x,v}
property of
K
: Proof of Lemma
2.3

In this subsection, we will prove the
L^{\infty}_{x,v}
property of
K
, i.e., proof of Lemma
2.3
.

Before proving Lemma
2.3
, we need to analyze the factor
\sigma_{x}
in the bound (
2.9
). By the definition of
\sigma
in (
1.26
), it infers

(8.1)

\sigma_{x}(x,v)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\delta(1+|v-\mathfrak{u}|)^{-1+\gamma}\,,&(x,v)\in\Omega_{1}\,,\\
\delta\big(c_{1}(1+|v-\mathfrak{u}|)^{-1+\gamma}+c_{2}(\delta x+l)^{-\frac{1-\gamma}{3-\gamma}}\big)\,,&(x,v)\in\Omega_{2}\,,\\
\tfrac{10\delta}{3-\gamma}(\delta x+l)^{-\frac{1-\gamma}{3-\gamma}}\,,&(x,v)\in\Omega_{3}\,,\end{array}\right.

where
c_{1}
and
c_{2}
are positive functions depending on
(x,v)
and
\Upsilon
with
c_{1}+c_{2}
admiting a uniform lower bound. Also
\Omega_{i}
(
i=1,2,3
) are defined as follows.

(8.2)

\displaystyle\Omega_{1}=\{(x,v):\delta x+l\leq(1+|v-\mathfrak{u}|)^{3-\gamma}\}\,,

\displaystyle\Omega_{2}=\{(x,v):(1+|v-\mathfrak{u}|)^{3-\gamma}<\delta x+l<2(1+|v-\mathfrak{u}|)^{3-\gamma}\}\,,

\displaystyle\Omega_{3}=\{(x,v):\delta x+l\geq 2(1+|v-\mathfrak{u}|)^{3-\gamma}\}\,.

Lemma 8.1
.

Let
-3<\gamma\leq 1
. Denote by

(8.3)

\displaystyle P_{\sigma}=\tfrac{\sigma_{x}(x,v)}{\sigma_{x}(x,v_{*})}\,,\quad P_{v}=\tfrac{(1+|v-\mathfrak{u}|)^{-1+\gamma}}{(1+|v_{*}-\mathfrak{u}|)^{-1+\gamma}}\,.

Then the following results hold:

(x,v)

(x,v_{*})
P_{\sigma}
\Omega_{1}
\Omega_{2}
\Omega_{3}
\Omega_{1}
\Omega_{2}
\Omega_{3}
P_{\sigma}=P_{v}
P_{\sigma}\thicksim P_{v}
P_{v}\lesssim P_{\sigma}\lesssim 1
P_{\sigma}\thicksim P_{v}
P_{\sigma}\thicksim P_{v}
P_{\sigma}\thicksim 1
1\lesssim P_{\sigma}\lesssim P_{v}
P_{\sigma}\thicksim 1
P_{\sigma}=1

Proof.

Based on the expression
\sigma_{x}
in (
8.1
) and the definitions of
\Omega_{1}
(
i=1,2,3
) in (
8.2
), the results in Lemma
8.1
will be proved case by case.

Case 1.
(x,v)\in\Omega_{1}
,
(x,v_{*})\in\Omega_{1}
.

By (
8.1
), one has
\sigma_{x}(x,v)=\delta(1+|v-\mathfrak{u}|)^{-1+\gamma}
and
\sigma_{x}(x,v_{*})=\delta(1+|v_{*}-\mathfrak{u}|)^{-1+\gamma}
. Then

\displaystyle P_{\sigma}=\tfrac{\sigma_{x}(x,v)}{\sigma_{x}(x,v_{*})}=\tfrac{(1+|v-\mathfrak{u}|)^{-1+\gamma}}{(1+|v_{*}-\mathfrak{u}|)^{-1+\gamma}}=P_{v}\,.

Case 2.
(x,v)\in\Omega_{1}
,
(x,v_{*})\in\Omega_{2}
.

Due to
(x,v_{*})\in\Omega_{2}
, Lemma
2.1
and the definition of
\Omega_{2}
in (
8.2
) show that

\displaystyle\sigma_{x}(x,v_{*})\thicksim(\delta x+l)^{-\frac{1-\gamma}{3-\gamma}}\thicksim(1+|v_{*}-\mathfrak{u}|)^{-1+\gamma}\,.

Together
\sigma_{x}(x,v)=\delta(1+|v-\mathfrak{u}|)^{-1+\gamma}
, it follows that

\displaystyle P_{\sigma}=\tfrac{\sigma_{x}(x,v)}{\sigma_{x}(x,v_{*})}\thicksim\tfrac{(1+|v-\mathfrak{u}|)^{-1+\gamma}}{(1+|v_{*}-\mathfrak{u}|)^{-1+\gamma}}=P_{v}\,.

Case 3.
(x,v)\in\Omega_{1}
,
(x,v_{*})\in\Omega_{3}
.

By (
8.1
), one has
\sigma_{x}(x,v_{*})=\tfrac{10\delta}{3-\gamma}(\delta x+l)^{-\frac{1-\gamma}{3-\gamma}}
. Then

\displaystyle\tfrac{\sigma_{x}(x,v)}{\sigma_{x}(x,v_{*})}=\tfrac{3-\gamma}{10}(1+|v-\mathfrak{u}|)^{-1+\gamma}(\delta x+l)^{\frac{1-\gamma}{3-\gamma}}\,.

In
\Omega_{1}
, one has
\delta x+l\leq(1+|v-\mathfrak{u}|)^{3-\gamma}
. Then

\displaystyle\tfrac{\sigma_{x}(x,v)}{\sigma_{x}(x,v_{*})}\leq\tfrac{3-\gamma}{10}(1+|v-\mathfrak{u}|)^{-1+\gamma}(1+|v-\mathfrak{u}|)^{1-\gamma}=\tfrac{3-\gamma}{10}\,.

In
\Omega_{3}
, one has
\delta x+l\geq 2(1+|v_{*}-\mathfrak{u}|)^{3-\gamma}
. Then

\displaystyle\tfrac{\sigma_{x}(x,v)}{\sigma_{x}(x,v_{*})}\geq 2^{\frac{1-\gamma}{3-\gamma}}\tfrac{3-\gamma}{10}(1+|v-\mathfrak{u}|)^{-1+\gamma}(1+|v_{*}-\mathfrak{u}|)^{1-\gamma}=2^{\frac{1-\gamma}{3-\gamma}}\tfrac{3-\gamma}{10}P_{v}\,.

In summary,
2^{\frac{1-\gamma}{3-\gamma}}\tfrac{3-\gamma}{10}P_{v}\leq P_{\sigma}=\tfrac{\sigma_{x}(x,v)}{\sigma_{x}(x,v_{*})}\leq\tfrac{3-\gamma}{10}
, i.e.,
P_{v}\lesssim P_{\sigma}\lesssim 1
.

Case 4.
(x,v)\in\Omega_{2}
,
(x,v_{*})\in\Omega_{1}
.

By the similar arguments in
Case 2
, there hold

\displaystyle\sigma_{x}(x,v)\thicksim(\delta x+l)^{-\frac{1-\gamma}{3-\gamma}}\thicksim(1+|v-\mathfrak{u}|)^{-1+\gamma}\,,\quad\sigma_{x}(x,v_{*})=(1+|v_{*}-\mathfrak{u}|)^{-1+\gamma}\,,

which implies
P_{\sigma}\thicksim P_{v}
.

Case 5.
(x,v)\in\Omega_{2}
,
(x,v_{*})\in\Omega_{2}
.

By the arguments in
Case 2
and
Case 3
, one knows

\displaystyle\sigma_{x}(x,v)\thicksim(1+|v-\mathfrak{u}|)^{-1+\gamma}\,,\quad\sigma_{x}(x,v_{*})\thicksim(1+|v_{*}-\mathfrak{u}|)^{-1+\gamma}\,,

which means
P_{\sigma}\thicksim P_{v}
.

Case 6.
(x,v)\in\Omega_{2}
,
(x,v_{*})\in\Omega_{3}
.

In
\Omega_{2}
,
\sigma_{x}(x,v)\thicksim(1+|v-\mathfrak{u}|)^{-1+\gamma}\thicksim(\delta x+l)^{-\frac{1-\gamma}{3-\gamma}}
. In
\Omega_{3}
,
\sigma_{x}(x,v_{*})=\tfrac{10\delta}{3-\gamma}(\delta x+l)^{-\frac{1-\gamma}{3-\gamma}}
. It thereby infers that
P_{\sigma}\thicksim 1
.

Case 7.
(x,v)\in\Omega_{3}
,
(x,v_{*})\in\Omega_{1}
.

For
(x,v)\in\Omega_{3}
, one has

\displaystyle\sigma_{x}(x,v)=\tfrac{10\delta}{3-\gamma}(\delta x+l)^{-\frac{1-\gamma}{3-\gamma}}\leq\tfrac{10\delta}{3-\gamma}2^{-\frac{1-\gamma}{3-\gamma}}(1+|v-\mathfrak{u}|)^{-1+\gamma}\,.

For
(x,v_{*})\in\Omega_{1}
, it is easy to see that
\sigma_{x}(x,v_{*})=\delta(1+|v_{*}-\mathfrak{u}|)^{-1+\gamma}\leq\delta(\delta x+l)^{-\frac{1-\gamma}{3-\gamma}}
. Then there hold

\displaystyle\tfrac{\sigma_{x}(x,v)}{\sigma_{x}(x,v_{*})}=\tfrac{10}{3-\gamma}\tfrac{(\delta x+l)^{-\frac{1-\gamma}{3-\gamma}}}{(1+|v_{*}-\mathfrak{u}|)^{-1+\gamma}}\leq\tfrac{10}{3-\gamma}2^{-\frac{1-\gamma}{3-\gamma}}\tfrac{(1+|v-\mathfrak{u}|)^{-1+\gamma}}{(1+|v_{*}-\mathfrak{u}|)^{-1+\gamma}}\,,

and

\displaystyle\tfrac{\sigma_{x}(x,v)}{\sigma_{x}(x,v_{*})}=\tfrac{10}{3-\gamma}\tfrac{(\delta x+l)^{-\frac{1-\gamma}{3-\gamma}}}{(1+|v_{*}-\mathfrak{u}|)^{-1+\gamma}}\geq\tfrac{10}{3-\gamma}\tfrac{(\delta x+l)^{-\frac{1-\gamma}{3-\gamma}}}{(\delta x+l)^{-\frac{1-\gamma}{3-\gamma}}}=\tfrac{10}{3-\gamma}\,.

As a result, one has
1\lesssim P_{\sigma}\lesssim P_{v}
.

Case 8.
(x,v)\in\Omega_{3}
,
(x,v_{*})\in\Omega_{2}
.

For
(x,v)\in\Omega_{3}
and
(x,v_{*})\in\Omega_{2}
,

\displaystyle\sigma_{x}(x,v)=\tfrac{10\delta}{3-\gamma}(\delta x+l)^{-\frac{1-\gamma}{3-\gamma}}\,,\quad\sigma_{x}(x.v_{*})\thicksim(\delta x+l)^{-\frac{1-\gamma}{3-\gamma}}\thicksim(1+|v_{*}-\mathfrak{u}|)^{-1+\gamma}\,,

which shows that
P_{\sigma}=\tfrac{\sigma_{x}(x,v)}{\sigma_{x}(x,v_{*})}\thicksim 1
.

Case 9.
(x,v)\in\Omega_{3}
,
(x,v_{*})\in\Omega_{3}
.

In this case, one has
\sigma_{x}(x,v)=\sigma_{x}(x,v_{*})=\tfrac{10\delta}{3-\gamma}(\delta x+l)^{-\frac{1-\gamma}{3-\gamma}}
, which means that
P_{\sigma}=\tfrac{\sigma_{x}(x,v)}{\sigma_{x}(x,v_{*})}=1
.

The proof of Lemma
8.1
is therefore completed.
∎

Proof of Lemma
2.3
.

For the simplicity of notations, we denote by

(8.4)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{p}(x,v)=\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}(x,v)e^{\hbar\sigma(x,v)}w_{\beta,\vartheta}(v)\,.

By the definition of the norm
\|\cdot\|_{\beta,\vartheta}
in (
1.32
), one knows that

(8.5)

\displaystyle\|\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}e^{\hbar\sigma}Kf\|_{A;\beta,\vartheta}=\|\mathfrak{p}Kf\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\,.

Let
\tilde{f}(x,v)=\mathfrak{p}f(x,v)
and
K_{\mathfrak{p}}(\cdot)=\mathfrak{p}K(\mathfrak{p}^{-1}\cdot)
. Then one has

(8.6)

\displaystyle\|\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}e^{\hbar\sigma}Kf\|_{A;\beta,\vartheta}=\|\mathfrak{p}Kf\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}=\|K_{\mathfrak{p}}\tilde{f}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\,,\ \|\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}e^{\hbar\sigma}f\|_{A;\beta+\gamma-1,\vartheta}=\|(1+|v|)^{\gamma-1}\tilde{f}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\,.

It thereby suffices to prove

(8.7)

\displaystyle\|K_{\mathfrak{p}}\tilde{f}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\leq C\|(1+|v|)^{\gamma-1}\tilde{f}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\,.

Let
\chi(r)
be a smooth monotone function satisfying
\chi(r)=0
for
0\leq r\leq 1
and
\chi(r)=1
for
r\geq 2
. Then, together with (
1.8
)-(
1.9
)-(
1.10
), the operator
K_{\mathfrak{p}}
can be decomposed as follows:

(8.8)

\displaystyle K_{\mathfrak{p}}\tilde{f}(x,v)=-K_{1\mathfrak{p}}\tilde{f}(x,v)+K_{2\mathfrak{p}}^{1-\chi}\tilde{f}(x,v)+K_{2\mathfrak{p}}^{\chi}\tilde{f}(x,v)\,,

where

\displaystyle K_{1\mathfrak{p}}\tilde{f}(x,v)=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\tfrac{\mathfrak{p}(x,v)}{\mathfrak{p}(x,v_{*})}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}\mathfrak{M}^{\frac{1}{2}}(v)\mathfrak{M}^{\frac{1}{2}}(v_{*})\tilde{f}(x,v_{*})\mathrm{d}v_{*}\,,

\displaystyle K_{2\mathfrak{p}}^{1-\chi}\tilde{f}(x,v)=

\displaystyle\mathfrak{p}(x,v)\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}(1-\chi)(|v_{*}-v|)\big[\mathfrak{M}^{\frac{1}{2}}(v_{*}^{\prime})\tfrac{\tilde{f}}{\mathfrak{p}}(x,v^{\prime})+\mathfrak{M}^{\frac{1}{2}}(v^{\prime})\tfrac{\tilde{f}}{\mathfrak{p}}(x,v_{*}^{\prime})\big]\mathfrak{M}^{\frac{1}{2}}(v_{*})b(\omega,v_{*}-v)\mathrm{d}\omega\mathrm{d}v_{*}\,,

(8.9)

\displaystyle K_{2\mathfrak{p}}^{\chi}\tilde{f}(x,v)=

\displaystyle\mathfrak{p}(x,v)\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}\chi(|v_{*}-v|)\big[\mathfrak{M}^{\frac{1}{2}}(v_{*}^{\prime})\tfrac{\tilde{f}}{\mathfrak{p}}(x,v^{\prime})+\mathfrak{M}^{\frac{1}{2}}(v^{\prime})\tfrac{\tilde{f}}{\mathfrak{p}}(x,v_{*}^{\prime})\big]\mathfrak{M}^{\frac{1}{2}}(v_{*})b(\omega,v_{*}-v)\mathrm{d}\omega\mathrm{d}v_{*}\,.

Step 1. Estimate of
\tfrac{\mathfrak{p}(x,v)}{\mathfrak{p}(x,v_{*})}
.

By (
8.4
), one has
\tfrac{\mathfrak{p}(x,v)}{\mathfrak{p}(x,v_{*})}=\big[\tfrac{\sigma_{x}(x,v)}{\sigma_{x}(x,v_{*})}\big]^{\frac{m}{2}}\tfrac{(1+|v|)^{\beta}}{(1+|v_{*}|)^{\beta}}e^{\hbar[\sigma(x,v)-\sigma(x,v_{*})]}e^{\vartheta(|v-\mathfrak{u}|^{2}-|v_{*}-\mathfrak{u}|^{2})}
. By Lemma
2.1
, it follows
|\sigma(x,v)-\sigma(x,v_{*})|\leq c\big||v-\mathfrak{u}|^{2}-|v_{*}-\mathfrak{u}|^{2}\big|
.

We claim that for any
\beta\in\mathbb{R}
,

(8.10)

\displaystyle\tfrac{(1+|v|)^{\beta}}{(1+|v_{*}|)^{\beta}}\leq(1+|v-v_{*}|)^{|\beta|}\,.

Indeed, if
\beta\geq 0
,

\displaystyle(1+|v|)^{\beta}\leq(1+|v_{*}|+|v-v_{*}|)^{\beta}\leq(1+|v_{*}|)^{\beta}(1+|v-v_{*}|)^{\beta}\,,

namely,
\tfrac{(1+|v|)^{\beta}}{(1+|v_{*}|)^{\beta}}\leq(1+|v-v_{*}|)^{\beta}
. If
\beta<0
,

\displaystyle(1+|v_{*}|)^{-\beta}\leq(1+|v_{*}|)^{-\beta}(1+|v-v_{*}|)^{-\beta}=(1+|v_{*}|)^{-\beta}(1+|v-v_{*}|)^{|\beta|}\,,

which means that
\tfrac{(1+|v|)^{\beta}}{(1+|v_{*}|)^{\beta}}\leq(1+|v-v_{*}|)^{|\beta|}
. As a result, the claim (
8.10
) holds. Moreover, Lemma
8.1
and similar arguments in (
8.10
) indicate that for any
m\in\mathbb{R}
,

(8.11)

\displaystyle\big[\tfrac{\sigma_{x}(x,v)}{\sigma_{x}(x,v_{*})}\big]^{\frac{m}{2}}\leq C_{m,\gamma}\Big(1+\tfrac{(1+|v-\mathfrak{u}|)^{-\frac{m(1-\gamma)}{2}}}{(1+|v_{*}-\mathfrak{u}|)^{-\frac{m(1-\gamma)}{2}}}\Big)\leq C_{m,\gamma}(1+|v-v_{*}|)^{\frac{|m|(1-\gamma)}{2}}\,.

In summary, one derives that

(8.12)

\displaystyle\tfrac{\mathfrak{p}(x,v)}{\mathfrak{p}(x,v_{*})}\leq C(1+|v-v_{*}|)^{k}\mathrm{exp}\big[(c\hbar+\vartheta)\big||v-\mathfrak{u}|^{2}-|v_{*}-\mathfrak{u}|^{2}\big|\big]\,,

where
k=|\beta|+|m|(1-\gamma)/2\geq 0
.

Step 2. Estimate of
K_{1\mathfrak{p}}\tilde{f}(x,v)
.

By the definition of
K_{1\mathfrak{p}}\tilde{f}(x,v)
in (
8.9
) and the bound (
8.12
), it is easy to see

(8.13)

\displaystyle|K_{1\mathfrak{p}}\tilde{f}(x,v)|\leq C\underbrace{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|v_{*}-v|^{\gamma}(1+|v_{*}-v|)^{k}[\mathfrak{M}(v)\mathfrak{M}(v_{*})]^{2\delta_{0}}|\tilde{f}(x,v_{*})|\mathrm{d}v_{*}}_{:=\mathsf{A}^{-}}\,,

where
\delta_{0}=\tfrac{1}{4}-T(c\hbar+\vartheta)>0
by the assumptions in Lemma
2.3
. Due to
|v-\mathfrak{u}|^{2}+|v_{*}-\mathfrak{u}|^{2}\geq\tfrac{1}{2}|v-v_{*}|^{2}
,

(8.14)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{M}(v)\mathfrak{M}(v_{*})\leq\big[\tfrac{\rho}{(2\pi T)^{3/2}}\big]^{2}\mathrm{exp}\big[-\tfrac{|v-v_{*}|^{2}}{4T}\big]\,.

Together with
[\mathfrak{M}(v)\mathfrak{M}(v_{*})]^{\delta_{0}}\leq C(1+|v_{*}|)^{\gamma-1}
, it infers that

(8.15)

\displaystyle\mathsf{A}^{-}\leq

\displaystyle C\|(1+|v|)^{\gamma-1}\tilde{f}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|v_{*}-v|^{\gamma}(1+|v_{*}-v|)^{k}\mathrm{exp}\big[-\tfrac{\delta_{0}|v-v_{*}|^{2}}{4T}\big]\mathrm{d}v_{*}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\|(1+|v|)^{\gamma-1}\tilde{f}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\,,

where the last inequality is followed from the convergence of the integral

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|v_{*}-v|^{\gamma}(1+|v_{*}-v|)^{k}\mathrm{exp}\big[-\tfrac{\delta_{0}|v-v_{*}|^{2}}{4T}\big]\mathrm{d}v_{*}<\infty\,,

which holds if and only if
\gamma>-3
.

Therefore, the relations (
8.13
) and (
8.15
) indicate that

(8.16)

\displaystyle|K_{1\mathfrak{p}}\tilde{f}(x,v)|\leq C\|(1+|v|)^{\gamma-1}\tilde{f}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}

for
\gamma>-3
,
\beta,m\in\mathbb{R}
and
\vartheta\geq 0
sufficiently small.

Step 3. Estimate of
K_{2\mathfrak{p}}^{1-\chi}\tilde{f}(x,v)
.

First, we assert that

(8.17)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{M}(v_{*})\leq C\mathfrak{M}^{1-\delta_{0}}(v)\,,\ \mathfrak{M}(v_{*}^{\prime})\leq C\mathfrak{M}^{1-\delta_{0}}(v^{\prime})\,,\ \mathfrak{M}(v^{\prime})\leq C\mathfrak{M}^{1-\delta_{0}}(v_{*}^{\prime})\,,

provided that
|v_{*}-v|=|v_{*}^{\prime}-v^{\prime}|\leq 2
, where
\delta_{0}\in(0,1/4)
is given in Lemma
2.3
. Indeed, if
|v_{*}-v|\leq 2
, it follows that

\displaystyle|v-\mathfrak{u}|^{2}=

\displaystyle|v-v_{*}+v_{*}-\mathfrak{u}|^{2}=|v_{*}-\mathfrak{u}|^{2}+2(v-v_{*})\cdot(v_{*}-\mathfrak{u})+|v-v_{*}|^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(1+\tfrac{\delta_{0}}{1-\delta_{0}})|v_{*}-\mathfrak{u}|^{2}+(1+\tfrac{1-\delta_{0}}{\delta_{0}})|v-v_{*}|^{2}\leq\tfrac{1}{1-\delta_{0}}|v_{*}-\mathfrak{u}|^{2}+\tfrac{4}{\delta_{0}}\,.

Then there hold

\displaystyle\mathfrak{M}(v)=\tfrac{\rho}{(2\pi T)^{\frac{3}{2}}}\mathrm{exp}\big(-\tfrac{|v-\mathfrak{u}|^{2}}{2T}\big)\geq\tfrac{\rho}{(2\pi T)^{\frac{3}{2}}}\mathrm{exp}\big[-\tfrac{|v_{*}-\mathfrak{u}|^{2}}{2T(1-\delta_{0})}-\tfrac{2}{\delta_{0}T}\big]\geq C\mathfrak{M}^{\frac{1}{1-\delta_{0}}}(v_{*})\,,

which concludes the first inequality in (
8.17
). Moreover, the same arguments imply the last two bounds in (
8.17
).

By (
8.17
) and the definition of
K_{2\mathfrak{p}}^{1-\chi}\tilde{f}(x,v)
in (
8.9
), it is easy to obtain

\displaystyle|K_{2\mathfrak{p}}^{1-\chi}\tilde{f}(x,v)|\leq C(\Pi^{\prime}+\Pi_{*}^{\prime})\,,

where

\displaystyle\Pi^{\prime}=

\displaystyle\mathfrak{p}(x,v)\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}(1-\chi)(|v_{*}-v|)\big|\tfrac{\tilde{f}}{\mathfrak{p}}(x,v^{\prime})\big|\mathfrak{M}^{\frac{1-\delta_{0}}{2}}(v^{\prime})\mathfrak{M}^{\frac{1}{2}}(v_{*})b(\omega,v_{*}-v)\mathrm{d}\omega\mathrm{d}v_{*}\,,

\displaystyle\Pi_{*}^{\prime}=

\displaystyle\mathfrak{p}(x,v)\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}(1-\chi)(|v_{*}-v|)\big|\tfrac{\tilde{f}}{\mathfrak{p}}(x,v_{*}^{\prime})\big|\mathfrak{M}^{\frac{1-\delta_{0}}{2}}(v_{*}^{\prime})\mathfrak{M}^{\frac{1}{2}}(v_{*})b(\omega,v_{*}-v)\mathrm{d}\omega\mathrm{d}v_{*}\,.

Now we focus on dominating the quantity
\Pi^{\prime}
. By (
8.12
), one observes that

\displaystyle\tfrac{\mathfrak{p}(x,v)}{\mathfrak{p}(x,v^{\prime})}\leq

\displaystyle C(1+|v-v^{\prime}|)^{k}\mathrm{exp}\big[(c\hbar+\vartheta)\big||v-\mathfrak{u}|^{2}-|v^{\prime}-\mathfrak{u}|^{2}\big|\big]

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C(1+|v-v^{\prime}|)^{k}[\mathfrak{M}(v)\mathfrak{M}(v^{\prime})]^{-2T(c\hbar+\vartheta)}=C(1+|v-v^{\prime}|)^{k}[\mathfrak{M}(v)\mathfrak{M}(v^{\prime})]^{2\delta_{0}-\frac{1}{2}}\,.

Then the quantiy
\Pi^{\prime}
can be bounded by

\displaystyle\Pi^{\prime}\leq

\displaystyle C\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}(1-\chi)(|v_{*}-v|)|\tilde{f}(x,v^{\prime})|(1+|v-v^{\prime}|)^{k}\mathfrak{M}^{2\delta_{0}-\frac{1}{2}}(v)\mathfrak{M}^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\delta_{0}}{4(1-\delta_{0})}}(v_{*})\mathfrak{M}^{\frac{3}{2}\delta_{0}}(v^{\prime})\mathfrak{M}^{\frac{\delta_{0}}{4(1-\delta_{0})}}(v_{*})b(\omega,v_{*}-v)\mathrm{d}\omega\mathrm{d}v_{*}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}(1-\chi)(|v_{*}-v|)|\tilde{f}(x,v^{\prime})|(1+|v-v^{\prime}|)^{k}\mathfrak{M}^{\frac{5}{4}\delta_{0}}(v)\mathfrak{M}^{\frac{3}{2}\delta_{0}}(v^{\prime})\mathfrak{M}^{\frac{\delta_{0}}{4(1-\delta_{0})}}(v_{*})b(\omega,v_{*}-v)\mathrm{d}\omega\mathrm{d}v_{*}\,,

where the last inequality is followed from the bound
\mathfrak{M}^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{\delta_{0}}{4(1-\delta_{0})}}(v_{*})\leq C\mathfrak{M}^{\frac{1}{2}-\frac{3}{4}\delta_{0}}(v)
by (
8.17
). It is derived from (
8.14
) that
\mathfrak{M}^{\frac{5}{8}\delta_{0}}(v)\mathfrak{M}^{\frac{5}{8}\delta_{0}}(v^{\prime})\leq C\mathrm{exp}\big(-\tfrac{\delta_{0}|v-v^{\prime}|^{2}}{20T}\big)
. Together with
\mathfrak{M}^{\frac{\delta_{0}}{4}}(v^{\prime})\leq C(1+|v^{\prime}|)^{\gamma-1}
, the quantity
\Pi^{\prime}
can be further bounded by

\displaystyle\Pi^{\prime}\leq

\displaystyle C\|(1+|v|)^{\gamma-1}\tilde{f}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}(1-\chi)(|v_{*}-v|)(1+|v-v^{\prime}|)^{k}\mathfrak{M}^{\frac{5}{8}\delta_{0}}(v)\mathfrak{M}^{\frac{5}{8}\delta_{0}}(v^{\prime})

\displaystyle\times\mathrm{exp}\big(-\tfrac{\delta_{0}|v-v^{\prime}|^{2}}{20T}\big)\mathfrak{M}^{\frac{\delta_{0}}{4(1-\delta_{0})}}(v_{*})b(\omega,v_{*}-v)\mathrm{d}\omega\mathrm{d}v_{*}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\|(1+|v|)^{\gamma-1}\tilde{f}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\,,

where we have used the estimates
(1+|v-v^{\prime}|)^{k}\mathrm{exp}\big(-\tfrac{\delta_{0}|v-v^{\prime}|^{2}}{20T}\big)\leq C
and

\displaystyle\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}(1-\chi)(|v_{*}-v|)\mathfrak{M}^{\frac{5}{8}\delta_{0}}(v)\mathfrak{M}^{\frac{5}{8}\delta_{0}}(v^{\prime})\mathfrak{M}^{\frac{\delta_{0}}{4(1-\delta_{0})}}(v_{*})b(\omega,v_{*}-v)\mathrm{d}\omega\mathrm{d}v_{*}\leq C\mathfrak{M}^{\frac{5}{8}\delta_{0}}(v)\nu(v)\leq C\,.

Similarly, the quantity
\Pi_{*}^{\prime}
can be bounded by
\Pi_{*}^{\prime}\leq C\|(1+|v|)^{\gamma-1}\tilde{f}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}
for
-3<\gamma\leq 1
,
\beta\in\mathbb{R}
and sufficiently small
\vartheta\geq 0
. Consequently, it concludes that

(8.18)

\displaystyle|K_{2\mathfrak{p}}^{1-\chi}\tilde{f}(x,v)|\leq C\|(1+|v|)^{\gamma-1}\tilde{f}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}

for
-3<\gamma\leq 1
,
\beta\in\mathbb{R}
and sufficiently small
\vartheta\geq 0
.

Step 4. Estimate of
K_{2\mathfrak{p}}^{\chi}\tilde{f}(x,v)
.

By letting
V=v_{*}-v
,
V_{\shortparallel}=(V\cdot\omega)\omega
,
V_{\perp}=V-V_{\shortparallel}
and
\omega=\frac{v^{\prime}-v}{|v^{\prime}-v|}
, one has
\mathrm{d}\omega\mathrm{d}v_{*}=2|V_{\shortparallel}|^{-2}\mathrm{d}V_{\perp}\mathrm{d}V_{\shortparallel}
, see Section 2 of
[
12
]
. Moreover,
v^{\prime}=v+V_{\shortparallel}
,
v_{*}^{\prime}=v+V_{\perp}
,
v_{*}=v+V
. Then, by (
8.9
), one has

\displaystyle K_{2\mathfrak{p}}^{\chi}\tilde{f}(x,v)=\Lambda_{1}+\Lambda_{2}\,,

where

(8.19)

\displaystyle\Lambda_{1}=2\mathfrak{p}(x,v)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{V_{\perp}\perp V_{\shortparallel}}|V_{\shortparallel}|^{-2}\chi(|V|)\tfrac{\tilde{f}}{\mathfrak{p}}(x,v+V_{\shortparallel})\mathfrak{M}^{\frac{1}{2}}(v+V_{\perp})\mathfrak{M}^{\frac{1}{2}}(v+V)b(\omega,V)\mathrm{d}V_{\perp}\mathrm{d}V_{\shortparallel}\,,

\displaystyle\Lambda_{2}=2\mathfrak{p}(x,v)\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{V_{\perp}\perp V_{\shortparallel}}|V_{\shortparallel}|^{-2}\chi(|V|)\tfrac{\tilde{f}}{\mathfrak{p}}(v+V_{\perp})\mathfrak{M}^{\frac{1}{2}}(x,v+V_{\shortparallel})\mathfrak{M}^{\frac{1}{2}}(v+V)b(\omega,V)\mathrm{d}V_{\perp}\mathrm{d}V_{\shortparallel}\,.

Step 4.1. Control of
|\Lambda_{1}|
.
Set

(8.20)

\displaystyle\zeta=v+\tfrac{1}{2}V_{\shortparallel}\,,\ \zeta_{\shortparallel}=[(\zeta-\mathfrak{u})\cdot\omega]\omega\,,\ \zeta_{\perp}=(\zeta-\mathfrak{u})-\zeta_{\shortparallel}\,.

A direct calculation implies

(8.21)

\displaystyle-\tfrac{|v+V_{\perp}-\mathfrak{u}|^{2}+|v+V-\mathfrak{u}|^{2}}{4T}=-\tfrac{|v+V_{\perp}-\mathfrak{u}|^{2}+|v+V_{\perp}-\mathfrak{u}+V_{\shortparallel}|^{2}}{4T}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\tfrac{|\zeta-\tfrac{1}{2}V_{\shortparallel}+V_{\perp}-\mathfrak{u}|^{2}+|\zeta+V_{\perp}-\mathfrak{u}+\tfrac{1}{2}V_{\shortparallel}|^{2}}{4T}=-\tfrac{|\zeta-\mathfrak{u}+V_{\perp}|^{2}+\tfrac{1}{4}|V_{\shortparallel}|^{2}}{2T}=-\tfrac{|\zeta_{\shortparallel}|^{2}+|V_{\perp}+\zeta_{\perp}|^{2}+\tfrac{1}{4}|V_{\shortparallel}|^{2}}{2T}\,,

which yields

(8.22)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{M}^{\frac{1}{2}}(v+V_{\perp})\mathfrak{M}^{\frac{1}{2}}(v+V)=\tfrac{\rho}{(2\pi T)^{\frac{3}{2}}}\mathrm{exp}\big[-\tfrac{|V_{\shortparallel}|^{2}}{8T}-\tfrac{|\zeta_{\shortparallel}|^{2}}{2T}-\tfrac{|V_{\perp}+\zeta_{\perp}|^{2}}{2T}\big]\,.

Moreover, (
1.4
) and (
1.5
) imply that

(8.23)

\displaystyle|b(\omega,V)|\leq\tilde{b}_{0}\tfrac{|V\cdot\omega|}{|V|}|V|^{\gamma}=\tfrac{\tilde{b}_{0}|V_{\shortparallel}|}{|V|^{1-\gamma}}\,.

It also follows from (
8.12
) that
\tfrac{\mathfrak{p}(x,v)}{\mathfrak{p}(x,v+V_{\shortparallel})}\leq C(1+|V_{\shortparallel}|)^{k}\mathrm{exp}\big[\tfrac{(1-4\delta_{0})}{4T}\big||v-\mathfrak{u}|^{2}-|v+V_{\shortparallel}-\mathfrak{u}|^{2}\big|\big]
, where
\delta_{0}=\tfrac{1}{4}-T(c\hbar+\vartheta)>0
has been used. Note that

(8.24)

\displaystyle|v-\mathfrak{u}|^{2}-|v+V_{\shortparallel}-\mathfrak{u}|^{2}=-V_{\shortparallel}\cdot[2(v-\mathfrak{u})+V_{\shortparallel}]=-V_{\shortparallel}\cdot(2\zeta_{\perp}+2\zeta_{\shortparallel})=-2V_{\shortparallel}\cdot\zeta_{\shortparallel}\,,

which infers

(8.25)

\displaystyle\tfrac{\mathfrak{p}(x,v)}{\mathfrak{p}(x,v+V_{\shortparallel})}\leq C(1+|V_{\shortparallel}|)^{k}\mathrm{exp}\big[\tfrac{(1-4\delta_{0})}{2T}|V_{\shortparallel}\cdot\zeta_{\shortparallel}|\big]\,.

Plugging (
8.22
)-(
8.23
)-(
8.25
) into the expression of
\Lambda_{1}
in (
8.19
), one has

(8.26)

\displaystyle|\Lambda_{1}|\leq C\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}\int_{V_{\perp}\perp V_{\shortparallel}}|V_{\shortparallel}|^{-1}(1+|V_{\shortparallel}|)^{k}\mathrm{exp}\big[-\tfrac{|V_{\shortparallel}|^{2}}{8T}-\tfrac{|\zeta_{\shortparallel}|^{2}}{2T}+\tfrac{(1-4\delta_{0})}{2T}|V_{\shortparallel}\cdot\zeta_{\shortparallel}|\big]

\displaystyle\times\mathrm{exp}\big(-\tfrac{|V_{\perp}+\zeta_{\perp}|^{2}}{2T}\big)\tfrac{\chi(|V|)}{|V|^{1-\gamma}}|\tilde{f}(x,v+V_{\shortparallel})|\mathrm{d}V_{\perp}\mathrm{d}V_{\shortparallel}\,.

It is easy to see that by
-\tfrac{|V_{\shortparallel}|^{2}}{8T}-\tfrac{|\zeta_{\shortparallel}|^{2}}{2T}+\tfrac{(1-4\delta_{0})}{2T}|V_{\shortparallel}\cdot\zeta_{\shortparallel}|\leq-\tfrac{\delta_{0}|V_{\shortparallel}|^{2}}{2T}-\tfrac{2\delta_{0}|\zeta_{\shortparallel}|^{2}}{T}
,

\displaystyle|\Lambda_{1}|\leq

\displaystyle C\|(1+|v|)^{\gamma-1}\tilde{f}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|V_{\shortparallel}|^{-1}(1+|V_{\shortparallel}|)^{k}(1+|v+V_{\shortparallel}|)^{1-\gamma}\mathrm{exp}\big[-\tfrac{\delta_{0}|V_{\shortparallel}|^{2}}{2T}-\tfrac{2\delta_{0}|\zeta_{\shortparallel}|^{2}}{T}\big]

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\ \times\int_{V_{\perp}\perp V_{\shortparallel}}\mathrm{exp}\big(-\tfrac{|V_{\perp}+\zeta_{\perp}|^{2}}{2T}\big)\tfrac{\chi(|V|)}{|V|^{1-\gamma}}\mathrm{d}V_{\perp}\mathrm{d}V_{\shortparallel}\,.

Recalling that
v+V_{\shortparallel}=\zeta_{\shortparallel}+\zeta_{\perp}+\tfrac{1}{2}V_{\shortparallel}+\mathfrak{u}
, it holds
|v+V_{\shortparallel}|\leq C(1+|\zeta_{\shortparallel}|+|\zeta_{\perp}|+|V_{\shortparallel}|)
, which means
(1+|v+V_{\shortparallel}|)^{1-\gamma}\leq C(1+|V_{\shortparallel}|)^{1-\gamma}(1+|\zeta_{\shortparallel}|)^{1-\gamma}(1+|\zeta_{\perp}|)^{1-\gamma}
. Observe that
(1+|V_{\shortparallel}|)^{k+1-\gamma}(1+|\zeta_{\shortparallel}|)^{1-\gamma}\mathrm{exp}\big[-\tfrac{\delta|V_{\shortparallel}|^{2}}{4T}-\tfrac{\delta|\zeta_{\shortparallel}|^{2}}{T}\big]\leq C
and
\tfrac{\chi(|V|)}{|V|^{1-\gamma}}\leq\tfrac{C}{(1+|V_{\shortparallel}|^{2}+|V_{\perp}|^{2})^{\frac{1-\gamma}{2}}}
. Then the quantity
\Lambda_{1}
can be further bounded by

\displaystyle|\Lambda_{1}|\leq C\|(1+|v|)^{\gamma-1}\tilde{f}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|V_{\shortparallel}|^{-1}\mathrm{exp}\big[-\tfrac{\delta|V_{\shortparallel}|^{2}}{4T}-\tfrac{\delta|\zeta_{\shortparallel}|^{2}}{T}\big]

\displaystyle\times\underbrace{\int_{V_{\perp}\perp V_{\shortparallel}}\mathrm{exp}\big(-\tfrac{|V_{\perp}+\zeta_{\perp}|^{2}}{2T}\big)\tfrac{(1+|\zeta_{\perp}|)^{1-\gamma}}{(1+|V_{\shortparallel}|^{2}+|V_{\perp}|^{2})^{\frac{1-\gamma}{2}}}\mathrm{d}V_{\perp}}_{:=U}\mathrm{d}V_{\shortparallel}\,.

It turns to show that the quantity
U
is uniformly bounded in
V_{\shortparallel}
and
\zeta_{\perp}
. Let

\displaystyle I_{1}=\{V_{\perp};V_{\perp}\perp V_{\shortparallel}\,,|V_{\perp}+\zeta_{\perp}|>\tfrac{1}{2}|\zeta_{\perp}|\}\,,\quad I_{2}=\{V_{\perp};V_{\perp}\perp V_{\shortparallel}\,,|V_{\perp}+\zeta_{\perp}|\leq\tfrac{1}{2}|\zeta_{\perp}|\}\,.

Then

\displaystyle U=\int_{I_{1}}(\cdots)\mathrm{d}V_{\perp}+\int_{I_{2}}(\cdots)\mathrm{d}V_{\perp}:=U_{1}+U_{2}\,.

If
|V_{\perp}+\zeta_{\perp}|>\tfrac{1}{2}|\zeta_{\perp}|
, one has
\tfrac{(1+|\zeta_{\perp}|)^{1-\gamma}}{(1+|V_{\shortparallel}|^{2}+|V_{\perp}|^{2})^{\frac{1-\gamma}{2}}}\leq C\mathrm{exp}\big(\tfrac{|V_{\perp}+\zeta_{\perp}|^{2}}{4T}\big)
. Then
U_{1}
can be bounded by

\displaystyle U_{1}\leq C\int_{V_{\perp}\perp V_{\shortparallel}}\mathrm{exp}\big(-\tfrac{|V_{\perp}+\zeta_{\perp}|^{2}}{4T}\big)\mathrm{d}V_{\perp}\leq C\,.

If
|V_{\perp}+\zeta_{\perp}|\leq\tfrac{1}{2}|\zeta_{\perp}|
, it holds
|V_{\perp}|\geq|\zeta_{\perp}|-|V_{\perp}+\zeta_{\perp}|\geq\tfrac{1}{2}|\zeta_{\perp}|
, which implies
\tfrac{(1+|\zeta_{\perp}|)^{1-\gamma}}{(1+|V_{\shortparallel}|^{2}+|V_{\perp}|^{2})^{\frac{1-\gamma}{2}}}\leq\tfrac{(1+|\zeta_{\perp}|)^{1-\gamma}}{(1+\tfrac{1}{4}|\zeta_{\perp}|^{2})^{\frac{1-\gamma}{2}}}\leq C
. Then
U_{2}
can be dominated by

\displaystyle U_{2}\leq C\int_{V_{\perp}\perp V_{\shortparallel}}\mathrm{exp}\big(-\tfrac{|V_{\perp}+\zeta_{\perp}|^{2}}{2T}\big)\mathrm{d}V_{\perp}\leq C\,.

It therefore follows that
U=U_{1}+U_{2}\leq C
, which implies

(8.27)

\displaystyle|\Lambda_{1}|\leq C\|(1+|v|)^{\gamma-1}\tilde{f}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|V_{\shortparallel}|^{-1}\mathrm{exp}\big[-\tfrac{\delta|V_{\shortparallel}|^{2}}{4T}-\tfrac{\delta|\zeta_{\shortparallel}|^{2}}{T}\big]\mathrm{d}V_{\shortparallel}\leq C\|(1+|v|)^{\gamma-1}\tilde{f}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}

for
-3<\gamma\leq 1
,
\beta\in\mathbb{R}
and sufficiently small
\vartheta\geq 0
.

Step 4.2. Control of
|\Lambda_{2}|
.
Recalling the definition (
8.20
), a direct computation implies

(8.28)

\displaystyle-\tfrac{|v+V_{\shortparallel}-\mathfrak{u}|^{2}+|v+V-\mathfrak{u}|^{2}}{4T}=-\tfrac{|\zeta_{\shortparallel}+\tfrac{1}{2}V_{\shortparallel}|^{2}}{2T}-\tfrac{|\zeta_{\perp}|^{2}}{4T}-\tfrac{|V_{\perp}+\zeta_{\perp}|^{2}}{4T}\,,

which yields

(8.29)

\displaystyle\mathfrak{M}^{\frac{1}{2}}(v+V_{\shortparallel})\mathfrak{M}^{\frac{1}{2}}(v+V)=\tfrac{\rho}{(2\pi T)^{\frac{3}{2}}}\mathrm{exp}\big[-\tfrac{|\zeta_{\shortparallel}+\tfrac{1}{2}V_{\shortparallel}|^{2}}{2T}-\tfrac{|\zeta_{\perp}|^{2}}{4T}-\tfrac{|V_{\perp}+\zeta_{\perp}|^{2}}{4T}\big]\,.

By (
8.12
), there holds
\tfrac{\mathfrak{p}(x,v)}{\mathfrak{p}(x,v+V_{\perp})}\leq C(1+|V_{\perp}|)^{k}\mathrm{exp}\big[\tfrac{1-4\delta_{0}}{4T}\big||v-\mathfrak{u}|^{2}-|v+V_{\perp}-\mathfrak{u}|^{2}\big|\big]
. By (
8.20
), it follows

(8.30)

\displaystyle|v-\mathfrak{u}|^{2}-|v+V_{\perp}-\mathfrak{u}|^{2}=-|V_{\perp}+\zeta_{\perp}|^{2}+|\zeta_{\perp}|^{2}\,.

Moreover,
(1+|V_{\perp}|)^{k}\leq(1+|\zeta_{\perp}|)^{k}(1+|V_{\perp}+\zeta_{\perp}|)^{k}
. Then

(8.31)

\displaystyle\tfrac{\mathfrak{p}(x,v)}{\mathfrak{p}(x,v+V_{\perp})}\leq C(1+|\zeta_{\perp}|)^{k}(1+|V_{\perp}+\zeta_{\perp}|)^{k}\mathrm{exp}\big[\tfrac{1-4\delta_{0}}{4T}\big(|V_{\perp}+\zeta_{\perp}|^{2}+|\zeta_{\perp}|^{2}\big)\big]\,.

Substituting (
8.23
), (
8.29
) and (
8.31
) into the expression of
\Lambda_{2}
in (
8.19
), it follows that

\displaystyle|\Lambda_{2}|\leq

\displaystyle C\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|V_{\shortparallel}|^{-1}(1+|\zeta_{\perp}|)^{k}\mathrm{exp}\big(-\tfrac{|\zeta_{\shortparallel}+\tfrac{1}{2}V_{\shortparallel}|^{2}}{2T}-\tfrac{\delta_{0}|\zeta_{\perp}|^{2}}{T}\big)

(8.32)

\displaystyle\times\int_{V_{\perp}\perp V_{\shortparallel}}(1+|V_{\perp}+\zeta_{\perp}|)^{k}\tfrac{\chi(|V|)}{|V|^{1-\gamma}}|\tilde{f}(x,v+V_{\perp})|\mathrm{exp}\big(-\tfrac{\delta_{0}|V_{\perp}+\zeta_{\perp}|^{2}}{T}\big)\mathrm{d}V_{\perp}\mathrm{d}V_{\shortparallel}\,.

Notice that
\tfrac{\chi(|V|)}{|V|^{1-\gamma}}\leq\tfrac{C}{(1+|V_{\shortparallel}|^{2}+|V_{\perp}|^{2})^{\frac{1-\gamma}{2}}}\leq\tfrac{C}{(1+|V_{\shortparallel}|)^{1-\gamma}}
and

\displaystyle|\tilde{f}(x,v+V_{\perp})|\leq

\displaystyle\|(1+|v|)^{\gamma-1}\tilde{f}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}(1+|v+V_{\perp}|)^{1-\gamma}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\|(1+|v|)^{\gamma-1}\tilde{f}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}(1+|V_{\perp}+\zeta_{\perp}|)^{1-\gamma}(1+|\zeta_{\shortparallel}+\tfrac{1}{2}V_{\shortparallel}|)^{1-\gamma}(1+|\zeta_{\shortparallel}|)^{1-\gamma}\,,

where the relation
v+V_{\perp}=\mathfrak{u}+\zeta_{\shortparallel}+\zeta_{\perp}-\tfrac{1}{2}V_{\shortparallel}+V_{\perp}
has been utilized. Moreover,

\displaystyle(1+|\zeta_{\perp}|)^{k}(1+|\zeta_{\shortparallel}+\tfrac{1}{2}V_{\shortparallel}|)^{1-\gamma}\mathrm{exp}\big(-\tfrac{\delta_{0}|\zeta_{\perp}|^{2}}{T}-\tfrac{|\zeta_{\shortparallel}+\tfrac{1}{2}V_{\shortparallel}|^{2}}{4T}\big)\leq C\,.

Then the quantity
\Lambda_{2}
can be further bounded by

\displaystyle|\Lambda_{2}|\leq

\displaystyle C\|(1+|v|)^{\gamma-1}\tilde{f}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|V_{\shortparallel}|^{-1}\tfrac{(1+|\zeta_{\shortparallel}|)^{1-\gamma}}{(1+|V_{\shortparallel}|)^{1-\gamma}}\mathrm{exp}\big(-\tfrac{|\zeta_{\shortparallel}+\tfrac{1}{2}V_{\shortparallel}|^{2}}{4T}\big)

\displaystyle\times\int_{V_{\perp}\perp V_{\shortparallel}}(1+|V_{\perp}+\zeta_{\perp}|)^{k+1-\gamma}\mathrm{exp}\big(-\tfrac{\delta_{0}|V_{\perp}+\zeta_{\perp}|^{2}}{T}\big)\mathrm{d}V_{\perp}\mathrm{d}V_{\shortparallel}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\|(1+|v|)^{\gamma-1}\tilde{f}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\underbrace{\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|V_{\shortparallel}|^{-1}\tfrac{(1+|\zeta_{\shortparallel}|)^{1-\gamma}}{(1+|V_{\shortparallel}|)^{1-\gamma}}\mathrm{exp}\big(-\tfrac{|\zeta_{\shortparallel}+\tfrac{1}{2}V_{\shortparallel}|^{2}}{4T}\big)\mathrm{d}V_{\shortparallel}}_{:=W}\,.

Set
I\!I_{1}=\{V_{\shortparallel};|\zeta_{\shortparallel}+\tfrac{1}{2}V_{\shortparallel}|>\tfrac{1}{2}|\zeta_{\shortparallel}|\}\,,I\!I_{2}=\{V_{\shortparallel};|\zeta_{\shortparallel}+\tfrac{1}{2}V_{\shortparallel}|\leq\tfrac{1}{2}|\zeta_{\shortparallel}|\}
. The quantity
W
can thereby be decomposed as

\displaystyle W=\int_{I\!I_{1}}(\cdots)\mathrm{d}V_{\shortparallel}+\int_{I\!I_{2}}(\cdots)\mathrm{d}V_{\shortparallel}:=W_{1}+W_{2}\,.

If
|\zeta_{\shortparallel}+\tfrac{1}{2}V_{\shortparallel}|>\tfrac{1}{2}|\zeta_{\shortparallel}|
, one has
\tfrac{(1+|\zeta_{\shortparallel}|)^{1-\gamma}}{(1+|V_{\shortparallel}|)^{1-\gamma}}\mathrm{exp}\big(-\tfrac{|\zeta_{\shortparallel}+\tfrac{1}{2}V_{\shortparallel}|^{2}}{8T}\big)\leq C
. Then

(8.33)

\displaystyle W_{1}\leq

\displaystyle C\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|V_{\shortparallel}|^{-1}\mathrm{exp}\big(-\tfrac{|\zeta_{\shortparallel}+\tfrac{1}{2}V_{\shortparallel}|^{2}}{8T}\big)\mathrm{d}V_{\shortparallel}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\int_{|V_{\shortparallel}|\leq 1}|V_{\shortparallel}|^{-1}\mathrm{d}V_{\shortparallel}+C\int_{|V_{\shortparallel}|>1}\mathrm{exp}\big(-\tfrac{|\zeta_{\shortparallel}+\tfrac{1}{2}V_{\shortparallel}|^{2}}{8T}\big)\mathrm{d}V_{\shortparallel}\leq C\,.

If
|\zeta_{\shortparallel}+\tfrac{1}{2}V_{\shortparallel}|\leq\tfrac{1}{2}|\zeta_{\shortparallel}|
, it follows
\tfrac{1}{2}|V_{\shortparallel}|\geq|\zeta_{\shortparallel}|-|\zeta_{\shortparallel}+\tfrac{1}{2}V_{\shortparallel}|\geq\tfrac{1}{2}|\zeta_{\shortparallel}|
, i.e.,
|V_{\shortparallel}|\geq|\zeta_{\shortparallel}|
, which infers that
\tfrac{(1+|\zeta_{\shortparallel}|)^{1-\gamma}}{(1+|V_{\shortparallel}|)^{1-\gamma}}\leq 1
. As a result,

(8.34)

\displaystyle W_{2}\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}|V_{\shortparallel}|^{-1}\mathrm{exp}\big(-\tfrac{|\zeta_{\shortparallel}+\tfrac{1}{2}V_{\shortparallel}|^{2}}{4T}\big)\mathrm{d}V_{\shortparallel}\leq C\,.

It thereby holds
W=W_{1}+W_{2}\leq C
, which shows

(8.35)

\displaystyle|\Lambda_{2}|\leq C\|(1+|v|)^{\gamma-1}\tilde{f}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\,.

By (
8.27
) and (
8.35
), one establishes that for
-3<\gamma\leq 1
,

(8.36)

\displaystyle|K_{2\mathfrak{p}}^{\chi}\tilde{f}(x,v)|\leq|\Lambda_{1}|+|\Lambda_{2}|\leq C\|(1+|v|)^{\gamma-1}\tilde{f}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}\,.

Finally, by plugging the estimates (
8.16
), (
8.18
) and (
8.36
) into (
8.8
), one knows that the bound (
8.7
) holds. The proof of Lemma
2.3
is therefore completed.
∎

8.2.
L^{\infty}_{x}L^{2}_{v}
-
L^{\infty}_{x,v}
property of
K
: Proof of Lemma
2.4

In this subsection, we will justify the property that the weighted
L^{\infty}_{x}L^{2}_{v}
norm can be bounded by the weighted
L^{\infty}_{x,v}
norm, i.e., proof of Lemma
2.4
.

Proof of Lemma
2.4
.

Let
\chi_{\varepsilon}(r)
be a smooth monotone function satisfying
\chi_{\varepsilon}(r)=0
for
0\leq r\leq\varepsilon
and
\chi_{\varepsilon}(r)=1
for
r\geq 2\varepsilon
, where
\varepsilon>0
is small to be determined. By (
1.8
)-(
1.9
)-(
1.10
), one has

(8.37)

\displaystyle K_{\hbar}g(x,v)=-K_{\hbar 1}g(x,v)+K_{\hbar 2}^{1-\chi_{\varepsilon}}g(x,v)+K_{\hbar 2}^{\chi_{\varepsilon}}g(x,v)\,,

where for
\Gamma=\chi_{\varepsilon}
and
1-\chi_{\varepsilon}
,

\displaystyle K_{\hbar 1}g(x,v)=

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}^{3}}e^{\hbar\sigma(x,v)-\hbar\sigma(x,v_{*})}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}\mathfrak{M}^{\frac{1}{2}}(v)\mathfrak{M}^{\frac{1}{2}}(v_{*})g(x,v_{*})\mathrm{d}v_{*}\,,

(8.38)

\displaystyle K_{\hbar 2}^{\Gamma}g(x,v)=

\displaystyle\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}\Gamma(|v-v_{*}|)e^{\hbar\sigma(x,v)-\hbar\sigma(x,v^{\prime})}\mathfrak{M}^{\frac{1}{2}}(v_{*}^{\prime})\mathfrak{M}^{\frac{1}{2}}(v_{*})g(x,v^{\prime})b(\omega,v_{*}-v)\mathrm{d}\omega\mathrm{d}v_{*}

\displaystyle+\iint_{\mathbb{R}^{3}\times\mathbb{S}^{2}}\Gamma(|v-v_{*}|)e^{\hbar\sigma(x,v)-\hbar\sigma(x,v_{*}^{\prime})}\mathfrak{M}^{\frac{1}{2}}(v^{\prime})\mathfrak{M}^{\frac{1}{2}}(v_{*})g(x,v_{*}^{\prime})b(\omega,v_{*}-v)\mathrm{d}\omega\mathrm{d}v_{*}\,.

Observe that
[\![\nu^{-1}K_{\hbar}g]\!]_{A;m,0,\vartheta}=\|\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}w_{-\gamma,\vartheta}K_{\hbar}g\|_{L^{\infty}_{x,v}}
.

Step 1. Control the quantity
[\![\nu^{-1}K_{\hbar 1}g]\!]_{A;m,0,\vartheta}
.

By splitting

\displaystyle\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}(x,v)w_{-\gamma,\vartheta}(v)K_{\hbar 1}g(x,v)=

\displaystyle\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}(x,v)w_{-\gamma,\vartheta}(v)\int_{|v-v_{*}|\leq\varepsilon}(\cdots)\mathrm{d}v_{*}

\displaystyle+\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}(x,v)w_{-\gamma,\vartheta}(v)\int_{|v-v_{*}|>\varepsilon}(\cdots)\mathrm{d}v_{*}=I_{1}+I_{2}\,.

Recalling the bound (
8.12
) with
\beta=0
, one has

(8.39)

\displaystyle\tfrac{\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}(x,v)w_{0,\vartheta}(v)e^{\hbar\sigma(x,v)}}{\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}(x,v_{*})w_{0,\vartheta}(v_{*})e^{\hbar\sigma(x,v_{*})}}\leq C(1+|v-v_{*}|)^{\frac{|m|(1-\gamma)}{2}}e^{(c\hbar+\vartheta)\big||v-\mathfrak{u}|^{2}-|v_{*}-\mathfrak{u}|^{2}\big|}

for sufficiently small
\hbar,\vartheta\geq 0
. It therefore holds

\displaystyle|I_{1}|\leq

\displaystyle C[\![g]\!]_{A;m,0,\vartheta}(1+|v|)^{-\gamma}\int_{|v-v_{*}|\leq\varepsilon}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}(1+|v-v_{*}|)^{\frac{|m|(1-\gamma)}{2}}e^{-[\tfrac{T}{4}-(c\hbar+\vartheta)](|v-\mathfrak{u}|^{2}+|v_{*}-\mathfrak{u}|^{2})}\mathrm{d}v_{*}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C[\![g]\!]_{A;m,0,\vartheta}\int_{|v-v_{*}|\leq\varepsilon}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}e^{-[\tfrac{T}{8}-(c\hbar+\vartheta)](|v-\mathfrak{u}|^{2}+|v_{*}-\mathfrak{u}|^{2})}\mathrm{d}v_{*}\leq C\varepsilon^{3+\gamma}[\![g]\!]_{A;m,0,\vartheta}\,,

provided that
\hbar,\vartheta\geq 0
are both sufficiently small and
\gamma>-3
.

By Lemma
2.1
and (
8.11
), one easily knows that

\displaystyle w_{-\gamma,\vartheta}(v)\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}(x,v)\sigma_{x}^{-\frac{m}{2}}(x,v_{*})e^{\hbar\sigma(x,v)-\hbar\sigma(x,v_{*})}|v-v_{*}|^{\gamma}\mathbf{1}_{|v-v_{*}|>\varepsilon}\mathfrak{M}^{\frac{1}{4}}(v)\mathfrak{M}^{\frac{1}{4}}(v_{*})\leq C_{\varepsilon}

uniformly in
(x,v,v_{*})
, provided that
\hbar,\vartheta\geq 0
are small enough. Then the quantity
I_{2}
can be bounded by

\displaystyle|I_{2}|\leq

\displaystyle C_{\varepsilon}\int_{|v-v_{*}|>\varepsilon}\mathfrak{M}^{\frac{1}{4}}(v)\mathfrak{M}^{\frac{1}{4}}(v_{*})\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}(x,v_{*})|g(x,v_{*})|\mathrm{d}v_{*}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{\varepsilon}\Big(\int_{|v-v_{*}|>\varepsilon}z_{-\alpha^{\prime}}^{2}(v_{*})\mathfrak{M}^{\frac{1}{2}}(v)\mathfrak{M}^{\frac{1}{2}}(v_{*})\mathrm{d}v_{*}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}z_{\alpha^{\prime}}g\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{\varepsilon}\|\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}z_{\alpha^{\prime}}g\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}\leq C_{\varepsilon}\|\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}z_{\alpha^{\prime}}w_{-\gamma,\vartheta}g\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}\,,

where the last inequality is derived from
0\leq\alpha^{\prime}<\frac{1}{2}
. Here
z_{\alpha^{\prime}}
is defined in (
1.29
). Consequently, it holds

\displaystyle|\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}(x,v)w_{-\gamma,\vartheta}(v)K_{\hbar 1}g(x,v)|\leq|I_{1}|+|I_{2}|\leq

\displaystyle C\varepsilon^{3+\gamma}[\![g]\!]_{A;m,0,\vartheta}+C_{\varepsilon}\|\sigma_{x}^{\frac{m}{2}}z_{\alpha^{\prime}}w_{-\gamma,\vartheta}g\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{2}_{v}}\,,

which means that

(8.40)
